का.आ. 830.--भारतीय रिजर्व बैंक अधिनियम, 4934 की धारा 8 की उपधारा (4) के साथ पठित धारा 8 की 


उपधारा () के खंड (क) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए, केन्द्रीय सरकार, एतद्द्वारा, भारतीय रिजर्व बैंक के 
कार्यपालक निदेशक श्री रोहित जैन (जन्म तिथि: 6.40.4967) को दिनांक 3.5.2026 को या उसके बाद कार्यभार ग्रहण 
करने की तारीख से तीन वर्ष की अवधि के लिए अथवा अगले आदेशों तक, जो भी पहले हो, भारतीय रिजर्व बैंक में 
उप-गवर्नर के पद पर नियुक्त करती है।

l. Admn. Commander Station Head Quarter (Civil Defence), Chandi Mandir Cantt. Panchkula, Haryana. 
Ds M/s Sun Rise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd., 2253/] 97 Floor, Pipli Wala Town, Mani Mazra, Chandigarh. 
----Respondents 


Present:-Sh. Jang Bahadur, AR for workman. 
Sh. Satinder Singh, AR for respondent no.|. 


Respondent no.2 already ex parte. 


4236 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 6, 2026/VAISAKHA 26, 948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


Award :0.03.2026 


l. Workman has filed the present claim petition under Section 2A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 
(hereinafter called the Act) against his termination w.e.f. 27.0.208 claiming reinstatement into service along with 
continuity of service and full back wages. 


2: It is maintained by the workman in the claim petition that that he was appointed as sweeper/safai karamchari 
on 3.0.20I5 by respondent no.! at minimum wages prescribed by the Central Government. But the said minimum 
wages were never paid to him. Management has unlawfully deducted 2% EPF share, .75% ESI and Rs.300/- as 
TDS from his salary despite of the fact that his total wages comes under the ambit of tax free limit. He was working 
under respondent no.! directly, but respondent no.! in order to avoid its liability always shows his employment in 
record under different contractors, which tantamount to unfair labour practice. 


3. It is further maintained that workman including 200 other safai karamcharis of respondent no.| raised the 
demand for non-deduction of TDS from the wages paid by respondent no.l. However, their demand was not 
accepted. Other workers working under respondent no.! formed union and subsequently his services were dispensed 
with w.e.f. 27.0.20I8. It is also maintained that respondent no.! retained juniors in service and also made fresh 
appointments after termination of his services and no compensation was paid under Section 25F of the Act. Thus, 
there is violation of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act. Hence this petition. 


4. Notice of the petition was given to respondents. 


3: Respondent no.! filed reply to the claim petition maintaining therein that the workman has not come to the 
Court with clean hands as he was appointed as sweeper by respondent no.2 (contractor) at minimum wage rate as 
prescribed by the Central Government. As per Government Policy dated !8.03.20l] (Ex.R-l), Non-Cantonment 
Military Stations have been authorized to outsource conservancy safaiwala. The answering respondent entered into 
contract with various private agencies for outsourcing of sweepers (safai karamcharis). In July 208, the answering 
respondent entered into contract with M/s AA Foundation for safety and M/s Sunrise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd. through e- 
tendering for outsourcing of safai karamcharis to provide conservancy services in Chandi Mandir Military Station for 
the financial year 20I8-l9. Respondent no.2 submits bill for payment for the days the safai karamcharis performed 
their duties and answering respondent is only responsible to forward these bills to PCDA Western Command 
Chandigarh to release payment directly to the contractor’s account. Salary is paid to the workman by the contractor. 
Compliance of PF, ESI Rules etc. is carried out by the contractor as per contract agreement. Answering respondent 
has no responsibility of disbursement of wages and deduction of EPF and ESI etc. The workman and other safai 
karamcharis formed union in the headquarter, which is not permitted and also carried out demonstration inside the 
premises of Station Headquarter on 27.0.208 and failed to perform his duties. Thereafter, answering respondent 
asked respondent no.2 to ask the workers to perform their duties. Answering respondent has no role or responsibility 
for termination services of the workman. There is no relationship of employer and employee between the answering 
respondent and the workman. In view of Army Headquarter Letter, the civilian employees of the answering 
respondent cannot organize themselves into trade unions as station headquarter is an administrative office of the 
Government of India. In spite of that, workman along with other safai karamcharis formed union unauthorizedly and 
indulge in unlawful activities. It is maintained that workman is the employee of contractor and therefore, termination 
is the prerogative of the contractor. It is prayed that the claim be dismissed. 


6. Respondent no.2 also filed reply maintaining therein that workman worked with it w.e.f. 05.07.208 to 
27.0.208. Respondent no.2 has not terminated the services of workman. Workman was paid minimum wages as 
declared by the Centre Government from time to time. Workman himself left the job at his own will without notice. It 
is prayed that the claim may be dismissed. 


Evidence of workman:- 


7. In order to prove his case, workman examined himself as १४०७] and tendered into evidence his affidavit 
WWI/A and has been cross examined by the AR for respondent no.!. AR for workman closed the evidence on 
30.07.2024. 


Evidence of respondent no.|:- 


8. Respondent no.l! examined Colonel K P Singh S/o Late Colonel P. P. Singh, OIC, Civic Cell, Station Head 
Quarter, Chandi Mandir Panchkula as ५०७], who tendered into evidence his affidavit ExMWI/A along with 
documents Ex.RI to Ex.R4. AR for respondent no.! closed the evidence on !0.03.2026.

4 Letter of Head Quarter 22.04.980 Ex.R-4 


[भाग aves 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 4237 


9. It is pertinent to mention here that respondent no.2 was proceeded ex parte on 20.04.2023. 
Submissions of workman:- 


0. While arguing the case, AR for workman contended that in this case, workman was appointed by respondent 
no.| under a camouflage agreement with respondent no.2. The workman was appointed directly by respondent no.| 
on |3.l0.205 as sweeper and his services were dispensed with on 27.0.20i8 without any reason in violation of 
Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act as no compensation was paid to the workman and junior to him were retained in 
service and fresh appointment were made after termination of the workman. So far as the agreements between the 
respondent no.! & 2 are concerned, they are totally not tenable and is unfair labour practice. The work performed by 
the workman is of perennial in nature. The contractor was not having any license. The contractor has left after three 
months. No domestic inquiry was held before terminating the service of the workman, which is mandatory under the 
Act. 


Submissions of respondent no.|:- 


Il. On the other hand, AR for respondent no.! contended that the workman was engaged by respondent no.2 
(contractor) as per agreement executed between respondent no.l & 2 on outsource basis and there is no relationship 
of employer and employee between respondent no.l and the workman. Wages were paid by the respondent no.2 to 
the workman and there was no role of respondent no.! in appointing or terminating the services of the workman. 
Workman is employee of respondent no.2. If there is any violation of provision of the Act, then it is done by 
respondent no.2. As per Ex.R3, all dues were paid by the contractor with regard to EPF and ESI etc. The workman 
and other safai karamcharis formed union in the Headquarter, which is not permitted as per Ex.R4. Respondent no.| 
being the Government Agency, workman is trying to take benefit of his appointment by respondent no.2 and has filed 
a false claim after concealing material facts and claim of the workman is not maintainable. 


2. I have given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 


Findings:- 

3. The first contention regarding the applicant is that whether applicant comes within the definition of 
workman as is defined in Section 2(s) of the Act. I may mention that applicant was appointed as sweeper as per his 
claim petition and affidavit submitted before the Tribunal. In plain words the applicant was performing his duties as 
labourer/unskilled worker. He was not in supervisory or administrative post requiring him to perform only 
administrative duties. While interpreting Section 2(s) Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Devinder Singh Vs. 
Municipal Council, Sanaur, AIR 20] Supreme Court 2532,has observed as follows:- 


“The source of employment, the quantum of recruitment, the terms & conditions of employment contract of 
service, the quantum of wages/ pay and mode of payment are not at all relevant for deciding whether or not a 
person is a workman within the meaning of Section 2(s) of the Act. The definition of workman also does not 
make any distinction between full time and part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. 
There is nothing in the plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be inferred that only person 
employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing whole time job is a workman and the one 
employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty 
for fixed hours is not a workman.” 


4. Thus Hon’ble Supreme Court has clarified that the definition of workman also does not make any distinction 
between full time or part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. There is nothing in plain language of 
Section 2(s) from which it can be infer that only person employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing 
whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a 
casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a workman. In view of the ratio of law enunciated in the 
above ruling, in my considered opinion the applicant herein admittedly falls within the definition of ‘workman’ under 
Section 2(s) of the Act. 


5. The Hon’ble Supreme Court after analyzing the catena of cases has laid down in Balwant Raj Saluja Vs. Air 
India Limited in Civil Appeal ५०. 70266 dated 25.08.204, two well recognized tests to find out whether the labours 
are the contract employees of the principal employer are:- 


3) Whether the principal employer pays the salary instead of contractor and 
2) Whether the principal employer controls and supervise the work of the employees? 


6. The facts regarding the payment of salary by the management or contractor has not been stated in the claim 
petition of the workman. In fact, claim petition is totally silent regarding the payment of wages, salary, letter of 
appointment by respondent no.! or by contractor or anything likewise. Thus, this basic feature for holding the 
relationship of employer and employee i.e. between respondent no.! and workman totally lacking not in the pleading 
but also in the evidence submitted by the workman.However, ५७४ Colonel K P Singh in his cross examination has 
stated that it is responsibility of the contractor to pay monthly wages to the conservancy staff provided by him. 
Respondent no.| has nothing to do with the minimum wages, DC rate to the conservancy staff. Contractor is 


4238 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 6, 2026/VAISAKHA 26, 948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


responsible to give notice to the conservancy staff provided by him. Respondent no.! has no roll in terminating the 
services of the workman. Thus, it was the respondent no.2 who was required to ensure the payment of wages to the 
workers engaged by him from time to time. 


7. It is also added here that respondent no.! has also placed on record documents Ex.R-| to Ex.R-4. Ex.R-2 is 
the contract agreement between respondent no.! & 2. Thus, from the adduced evidence, it emerges that workman was 
paid salary by the contractor and he was being controlled and supervised by the contractor. Thus, there does not exist 
any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.|. However, there exists relation 
of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


8. It is also added here that as per definition of contractor as given in the Section 2(c) of the Contract Labour 
(Regulation and Abolition) Act, 970 (hereinafter called as Contract Act), respondent no.2 was supplying contract 
labour to the respondent no.!. Thus, respondent no.2 would fall under the definition of contractor. Section 2(c) of the 
Contract Act is reproduced as under: 


“Contractor”, in relation to an establishment, means a person who undertakes to produce a given result for the 
establishment, other than a mere supply of goods or articles of manufacture to such establishment, through contract 
labour or who supplies contract labour for any work of the establishment and includes a sub-contractor; 


9. TheHon’ble Apex Court in the case of Banglore Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A. Rajappa /9०728(36) 
FLR 266 dealt at length with the ambit and scope of expression “industry” as defined in Section 2(j) of the Act, and 


has laid down triple test for determining whether a particular establishment is industry or not. The triple test is where 
(a) systematic activity (b) organized by cooperation between employer and employee (the direct and substantial 
element is commercial (c) for the production or distribution of goods and services to calculated to satisfy human 
wants and wishes prima facie is an “Industry” in that enterprise. The workman was doing work i.e. systematic activity 
for respondent no.| at the asking of respondent no.2 and there was cooperation between the workman and respondent 
no.2, which involve substantial commercial element for rendering service to satisfy human wants. Thus, respondent 
no.2 would be termed as industry. 


20. Workman in his deposition has clearly stated that there was non-compliance of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of 
the Act. He also stated that he was appointed on 3.0.20I5 and he was paid minimum wages prescribed by Central 
Government and was working with respondent no.l. He was not given any compensation before terminating his 
services on 27.!0.208. In his cross examination, he has further stated that his salary used to be credited in his bank 
account. But he has not placed on record any bank account, in which his salary was credited in order to enable this 
Court to see who was making payment to the workman. However, there is no denial that the workman was paid salary 
by respondent no.2 (contractor). However, from the agreement dated 05.07.20I8 (Ex.R-2) placed on file by 
respondent no.l, it emerges that safai karamcharis were provided by respondent no.2 (contractor) to work with 
respondent no.l. Thus, workman was employee of respondent no.2 and not of respondent no.| as has been discussed 
above. There does not exist any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.|. 
However, there exists relation of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


2I. Admittedly, there is non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2. However, as regards 
violation of other provisions i.e. Section 25G and 25H of the Act, there is no evidence lead by the workman. Hence, 
workman is entitled for compensation from respondent no.2. 


22% It is also added here that there is no iota of evidence that any unfair labour practice used by respondent no.! 
by entering into contract with contractors for supply of safai karamcharis and agreement executed between 
respondent no.! and 2 cannot be said to be any unfair labour practice. So far as contentions of AR for workman that 
the work performed by the workman is of perennial in nature and no domestic inquiry was held before terminating 
the services of workman are concerned, in this regard, it is pointed out that workman was entitled only for 
compensation under Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2 (contractor) and no domestic inquiry was required to 
be conducted. Merely because work is of perennial in nature does not mean that any right has accrued in favour of the 
workman to get employment with respondent no.l, which is a department of Civil Defence run by Government of 
India and for that regular recruitment procedure is provided. Workman cannot seek backdoor entry into service. 
Moreover, workman is not employee of respondent no.| being employee of respondent no.2 (contractor). 


23. In view of above discussion, it is held that since respondent no.2 (contractor) is currently not working with 
respondent no.|, so keeping in view the tenure of service rendered by the workman and salary given to him during the 
period of his stay with the contractor and non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act, this Tribunal is of the considered 
opinion that workman is entitled for compensation. 


24. Keeping in view the fact and circumstances of the present case, the following scheme of compensation is 
deemed fit by this Tribunal: 


i. Workman who worked for less than 2 years with contractor shall be entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 


ii. Workman who worked for more than 2 years but less than 5 years with contractor shall be entitled 
Rs.30,000/-. 


[भाग aves 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 4239 
ili. Workman who worked for more than 5 years with contractors shall be entitled for Rs.50,000/-. 


23. Present workman employed on 3.0.205 and was terminated on 27.0.208 (more than 2 years and less 
than 5 years), so he is entitled for Rs.30,000/-. 


26. Respondent no.2 is directed to pay the workman Rs.30,000/- within one month from the date of the 
notification of the award failing which the respondent no.2 shall further pay interest @6% per annum from the date of 
award till the date of making payment. It is also added here that in case respondent no.! requires safai karamcharis, 
then workman may be engaged through any contractor on sympathetic ground. 


27. Let copy of this award be sent to Central Government for publication as required under Section !7 of the 
Act. 


KAMAL KANT, PO-cum-Link Officer

l. Workman has filed the present claim petition under Section 2A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 
(hereinafter called the Act) against his termination w.e.f. 27.0.208 claiming reinstatement into service along with 
continuity of service and full back wages. 


4240 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 6, 2026/VAISAKHA 26, 948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


2. It is maintained by the workman in the claim petition that that he was appointed as sweeper/safai karamchari 
on 0.2.203 by respondent no.l at minimum wages prescribed by the Central Government. But the said minimum 
wages were never paid to him. Management has unlawfully deducted 2% EPF share, |.75% ESI and Rs.300/- as 
TDS from hissalary despite of the fact that his total wages comes under the ambit of taxfree limit. He was working 
under respondent no.! directly, but respondent no.! in order to avoid its liability always shows his employment in 
record under different contractors, which tantamount to unfair labour practice. 


3: It is further maintained that workman including 200 other safai karamcharis of respondent no.! raised the 
demand for non-deduction of TDS from the wages paid by respondent no.l. However, their demand was not 
accepted. Other workers working under respondent no.l formed union and subsequently his services were dispensed 
with w.e.f. 27.0.20I8. It is also maintained that respondent no.! retained juniors in service and also made fresh 
appointments after termination of his services and no compensation was paid under Section 25F of the Act. Thus, 
there is violation of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act. Hence this petition. 


4. Notice of the petition was given to respondents. 


5. Respondent no.! filed reply to the claim petition maintaining therein that the workman has not come to the 
Court with clean hands as he was appointed as sweeper by respondent no.2 (contractor) at minimum wage rate as 
prescribed by the Central Government. As per Government Policy dated 8.03.20l (Ex.R-l), Non-Cantonment 
Military Stations have been authorized to outsource conservancy safaiwala. The answering respondent entered into 
contract with various private agencies for outsourcing of sweepers (safai karamcharis). In July 208, the answering 
respondent entered into contract with M/s AA Foundation for safety and M/s Sunrise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd. through e- 
tendering for outsourcing of safai karamcharis to provide conservancy services in Chandi Mandir Military Station for 
the financial year 20I8-l9. Respondent no.2 submits bill for payment for the days the safai karamcharis performed 
their duties and answering respondent is only responsible to forward these bills to PCDA Western Command 
Chandigarh to release payment directly to the contractor’s account. Salary is paid to the workman by the contractor. 
Compliance of PF, ESI Rules etc. is carried out by the contractor as per contract agreement. Answering respondent 
has no responsibility of disbursement of wages and deduction of EPF and ESI etc. The workman and other safai 
karamcharis formed union in the headquarter, which is not permitted and also carried out demonstration inside the 
premises of Station Headquarter on 27.0.20l8 and failed to perform his duties. Thereafter, answering respondent 
asked respondent no.2 to ask the workers to perform their duties. Answering respondent has no role or responsibility 
for termination services of the workman. There is no relationship of employer and employee between the answering 
respondent and the workman. In view of Army Headquarter Letter, the civilian employees of the answering 
respondent cannot organize themselves into trade unions as station headquarter is an administrative office of the 
Government of India. In spite of that, workman along with other safai karamcharis formed union unauthorizedly and 
indulge in unlawful activities. It is maintained that workman is the employee of contractor and therefore, termination 
is the prerogative of the contractor. It is prayed that the claim be dismissed. 


6. Respondent no.2 also filed reply maintaining therein that workman worked with it w.e.f. 05.07.20I8 to 
27.0.208. Respondent no.2 has not terminated the services of workman. Workman was paid minimum wages as 
declared by the Centre Government from time to time. Workman himself left the job at his own will without notice. It 
is prayed that the claim may be dismissed. 


Evidence of workman:- 


7. In order to prove his case, workman examined himself as WWI and tendered into evidence his affidavit 
WWI/A and has been cross examined by the AR for respondent no.!. AR for workman closed the evidence on 
30.07.2024. 


Evidence of respondent no.|:- 


8. Respondent no.|! examined Colonel K P Singh S/o Late Colonel P. P. Singh, OIC, Civic Cell, Station Head 
Quarter, Chandi Mandir Panchkula as MWI, who tendered into evidence his affidavit Ex.MWI/A along with 
documents ४5.२] to Ex.R4. AR for respondent no.! closed the evidence on 0.03.2026.

9. It is pertinent to mention here that respondent no.2 was proceeded ex parte on 20.04.2023.

Submissions of workman:- 


0. While arguing the case, AR for workman contended that in this case, workman was appointed by respondent 
no.| under a camouflage agreement with respondent no.2. The workman was appointed directly by respondent no.| 
on 0.I2.20l3 as sweeper and his services were dispensed with on 27.0.20I8 without any reason in violation of 
Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act as no compensation was paid to the workman and junior to him were retained in 
service and fresh appointment were made after termination of the workman. So far as the agreements between the 
respondent no.! & 2 are concerned, they are totally not tenable and is unfair labour practice. The work performed by 
the workman is of perennial in nature. The contractor was not having any license. The contractor has left after three 
months. No domestic inquiry was held before terminating the service of the workman, which is mandatory under the 
Act. 


Submissions of respondent no.|:- 


Il. On the other hand, AR for respondent no.! contended that the workman was engaged by respondent no.2 
(contractor) as per agreement executed between respondent no.! & 2 on outsource basis and there is no relationship 
of employer and employee between respondent no.l and the workman. Wages were paid by the respondent no.2 to 
the workman and there was no role of respondent no.| in appointing or terminating the services of the workman. 
Workman is employee of respondent no.2. If there is any violation of provision of the Act, then it is done by 
respondent no.2. As per Ex.R3, all dues were paid by the contractor with regard to EPF and ESI etc. The workman 
and other safai karamcharis formed union in the Headquarter, which is not permitted as per Ex.R4. Respondent no.| 
being the Government Agency, workman is trying to take benefit of his appointment by respondent no.2 and has filed 
a false claim after concealing material facts and claim of the workman is not maintainable. 


2. I have given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 


Findings:- 

3. The first contention regarding the applicant is that whether applicant comes within the definition of 
workman as is defined in Section 2(s) of the Act. I may mention that applicant was appointed as sweeper as per his 
claim petition and affidavit submitted before the Tribunal. In plain words the applicant was performing his duties as 
labourer/unskilled worker. He was not in supervisory or administrative post requiring him to perform only 
administrative duties. While interpreting Section 2(s) Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Devinder Singh Vs. 
Municipal Council, Sanaur, AIR 20 Supreme Court 2532,has observed as follows:- 


“The source of employment, the quantum of recruitment, the terms & conditions of employment/ contract of 
service, the quantum of wages/ pay and mode of payment are not at all relevant for deciding whether or not a 
person is a workman within the meaning of Section 2(s) of the Act. The definition of workman also does not 
make any distinction between full time and part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. 
There is nothing in the plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be inferred that only person 
employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing whole time job is a workman and the one 
employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty 
for fixed hours is not a workman.” 


4. Thus Hon’ble Supreme Court has clarified that the definition of workman also does not make any distinction 
between full time or part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. There is nothing in plain language of 
Section 2(s) from which it can be infer that only person employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing 
whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a 
casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a workman. In view of the ratio of law enunciated in the 
above ruling, in my considered opinion the applicant herein admittedly falls within the definition of ‘workman’ under 
Section 2(s) of the Act. 


5. The Hon’ble Supreme Court after analyzing the catena of cases has laid down in Balwant Raj Saluja Vs. Air 
India Limited in Civil Appeal ५४०. 70266 dated 25.08.20l4, two well recognized tests to find out whether the labours 
are the contract employees of the principal employer are:- 


3) Whether the principal employer pays the salary instead of contractor and 
2) Whether the principal employer controls and supervise the work of the employees? 


6. The facts regarding the payment of salary by the management or contractor has not been stated in the claim 
petition of the workman. In fact, claim petition is totally silent regarding the payment of wages, salary, letter of 
appointment by respondent no.| or by contractor or anything likewise. Thus, this basic feature for holding the 
relationship of employer and employee i.e. between respondent no.! and workman totally lacking not in the pleading 
but also in the evidence submitted by the workman.However, ५७४ Colonel K P Singh in his cross examination has 
stated that it is responsibility of the contractor to pay monthly wages to the conservancy staff provided by him. 
Respondent no.| has nothing to do with the minimum wages, DC rate to the conservancy staff. Contractor is 
responsible to give notice to the conservancy staff provided by him. Respondent no.! has no roll in terminating the 
services of the workman. Thus, it was the respondent no.2 who was required to ensure the payment of wages to the 
workers engaged by him from time to time. 


4242 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 6, 2026/VAISAKHA 26, 948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


7. It is also added here that respondent no. has also placed on record documents Ex.R-| to Ex.R-4. Ex.R-2 is 
the contract agreement between respondent no.| & 2. Thus, from the adduced evidence, it emerges that workman was 
paid salary by the contractor and he was being controlled and supervised by the contractor. Thus, there does not exist 
any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.|. However, there exists relation 
of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


8. It is also added here that as per definition of contractor as given in the Section 2(c) of the Contract Labour 
(Regulation and Abolition) Act, 970 (hereinafter called as Contract Act), respondent no.2 was supplying contract 
labour to the respondent no.l. Thus, respondent no.2 would fall under the definition of contractor. Section 2(c) of the 
Contract Act is reproduced as under: 


“Contractor”, in relation to an establishment, means a person who undertakes to produce a given result for the 
establishment, other than a mere supply of goods or articles of manufacture to such establishment, through contract 
labour or who supplies contract labour for any work of the establishment and includes a sub-contractor; 


9. TheHon’ble Apex Court in the case of Banglore Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A. Rajappa /978(36) 
FLR 266 dealt at length with the ambit and scope of expression “industry” as defined in Section 2(j) of the Act, and 


has laid down triple test for determining whether a particular establishment is industry or not. The triple test is where 
(a) systematic activity (b) organized by cooperation between employer and employee (the direct and substantial 
element is commercial (c) for the production or distribution of goods and services to calculated to satisfy human 
wants and wishes prima facie is an “Industry” in that enterprise. The workman was doing work i.e. systematic activity 
for respondent no.| at the asking of respondent no.2 and there was cooperation between the workman and respondent 
no.2, which involve substantial commercial element for rendering service to satisfy human wants. Thus, respondent 
no.2 would be termed as industry. 


20. Workman in his deposition has clearly stated that there was non-compliance of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of 
the Act. He also stated that he was appointed on 0.2.203 and he was paid minimum wages prescribed by Central 
Government and was working with respondent no.!. He was not given any compensation before terminating his 
services on 27.!0.208. In his cross examination, he has further stated that his salary used to be credited in his bank 
account. But he has not placed on record any bank account, in which his salary was credited in order to enable this 
Court to see who was making payment to the workman. However, there is no denial that the workman was paid salary 
by respondent no.2 (contractor). However, from the agreement dated 05.07.20I8 (Ex.R-2) placed on file by 
respondent no.l, it emerges that safai karamcharis were provided by respondent no.2 (contractor) to work with 
respondent no.l. Thus, workman was employee of respondent no.2 and not of respondent no.| as has been discussed 
above. There does not exist any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.|. 
However, there exists relation of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


2. Admittedly, there is non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2. However, as regards 
violation of other provisions i.e. Section 25G and 25H of the Act, there is no evidence lead by the workman. Hence, 
workman is entitled for compensation from respondent no.2. 


22. It is also added here that there is no iota of evidence that any unfair labour practice used by respondent no.| 
by entering into contract with contractors for supply of safai karamcharis and agreement executed between 
respondent no.! and 2 cannot be said to be any unfair labour practice. So far as contentions of AR for workman that 
the work performed by the workman is of perennial in nature and no domestic inquiry was held before terminating 
the services of workman are concerned, in this regard, it is pointed out that workman was entitled only for 
compensation under Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2 (contractor) and no domestic inquiry was required to 
be conducted. Merely because work is of perennial in nature does not mean that any right has accrued in favour of the 
workman to get employment with respondent no.l, which is a department of Civil Defence run by Government of 
India and for that regular recruitment procedure is provided. Workman cannot seek backdoor entry into service. 
Moreover, workman is not employee of respondent no.| being employee of respondent no.2 (contractor). 


23. In view of above discussion, it is held that since respondent no.2 (contractor) is currently not working with 
respondent no.|, so keeping in view the tenure of service rendered by the workman and salary given to him during the 
period of his stay with the contractor and non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act, this Tribunal is of the considered 
opinion that workman is entitled for compensation. 


24. Keeping in view the fact and circumstances of the present case, the following scheme of compensation is 
deemed fit by this Tribunal: 


i. Workman who worked for less than 2 years with contractor shall be entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 

il. Workman who worked for more than 2 years but less than 5 years with contractor shall be entitled 
Rs.30,000/-. 

ili. Workman who worked for more than 5 years with contractors shall be entitled for Rs.50,000/-. 

25. Present workman employed on 0.2.203 and was terminated on 27.!0.20I8 (more than 2 years and less 


than 5 years), so he is entitled for Rs. 30,000/-. 


[भाग [aves 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 4243 


26. Respondent no.2 is directed to pay the workman Rs.30,000/- within one month from the date of the 
notification of the award failing which the respondent no.2 shall further pay interest @6% per annum from the date of 
award till the date of making payment. It is also added here that in case respondent no.| requires safai karamcharis, 
then workman may be engaged through any contractor on sympathetic ground. 


27. Let copy of this award be sent to Central Government for publication as required under Section 7 of the 
Act. 


KAMAL KANT, PO-cum-Link Officer

-------- Workman 
Versus 
l. Admn. Commander Station Head Quarter (Civil Defence), Chandi Mandir Cantt. Panchkula, Haryana. 
2. M/s Sun Rise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd., 2253/] one Floor, Pipli Wala Town, Mani Mazra, Chandigarh. 
----Respondents 
Present:-Sh. Jang Bahadur, AR for workman. 
Sh. Satinder Singh, AR for respondent no.|. 
Respondent no.2 already ex parte. 
Award :0.03.2026 

l. Workman has filed the present claim petition under Section 2A of the Industrial Disputes Act, !947 


(hereinafter called the Act) against her termination w.e.f. 27.0.208 claiming reinstatement into service along with 
continuity of service and full back wages. 


23 It is maintained by the workman in the claim petition that that she was appointed as sweeper/safai 
karamchari on 23.0.207 by respondent no.] at minimum wages prescribed by the Central Government. But the 
said minimum wages were never paid to her. Management has unlawfully deducted !2% EPF share, !.75% ESI and 
Rs.300/- as TDS from his salary despite of the fact that her total wages comes under the ambit of tax free limit. She 


4244 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 6, 2026/VAISAKHA 26, 948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


was working under respondent no.| directly, but respondent no.l in order to avoid its liability always shows his 
employment in record under different contractors, which tantamount to unfair labour practice. 


3: It is further maintained that workman including 200 other safai karamcharis of respondent no.! raised the 
demand for non-deduction of TDS from the wages paid by respondent no.l. However, their demand was not 
accepted. Other workers working under respondent no.l! formed union and subsequently her services were 
dispensed with w.e.f. 27.0.208. It is also maintained that respondent no.! retained juniors in service and also 
made fresh appointments after termination of her services and no compensation was paid under Section 25F of the 
Act. Thus, there is violation of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act. Hence this petition. 


4. Notice of the petition was given to respondents. 


5. Respondent no.! filed reply to the claim petition maintaining therein that the workman has not come to the 
Court with clean hands as she was appointed as sweeper by respondent no.2 (contractor) at minimum wage rate as 
prescribed by the Central Government. As per Government Policy dated 8.03.20ll (Ex.R-l), Non-Cantonment 
Military Stations have been authorized to outsource conservancy safaiwala. The answering respondent entered into 
contract with various private agencies for outsourcing of sweepers (safai karamcharis). In July 208, the answering 
respondent entered into contract with M/s AA Foundation for safety and M/s Sunrise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd. through e- 
tendering for outsourcing of safai karamcharis to provide conservancy services in Chandi Mandir Military Station 
for the financial year 20l8-l9. Respondent no.2 submits bill for payment for the days the safai karamcharis 
performed their duties and answering respondent is only responsible to forward these bills to PCDA Western 
Command Chandigarh to release payment directly to the contractor’s account. Salary is paid to the workman by the 
contractor. Compliance of PF, ESI Rules etc. is carried out by the contractor as per contract agreement. Answering 
respondent has no responsibility of disbursement of wages and deduction of EPF and ESI etc. The workman and 
other safai karamcharis formed union in the headquarter, which is not permitted and also carried out demonstration 
inside the premises of Station Headquarter on 27.0.208 and failed to perform his duties. Thereafter, answering 
respondent asked respondent no.2 to ask the workers to perform their duties. Answering respondent has no role or 
responsibility for termination services of the workman. There is no relationship of employer and employee between 
the answering respondent and the workman. In view of Army Headquarter Letter, the civilian employees of the 
answering respondent cannot organize themselves into trade unions as station headquarter is an administrative office 
of the Government of India. In spite of that, workman along with other safai karamcharis formed union 
unauthorizedly and indulge in unlawful activities. It is maintained that workman is the employee of contractor and 
therefore, termination is the prerogative of the contractor. It is prayed that the claim be dismissed. 


6. Respondent no.2 also filed reply maintaining therein that workman worked with it w.e.f. 05.07.20l8 to 
27.0.208. Respondent no.2 has not terminated the services of workman. Workman was paid minimum wages as 
declared by the Centre Government from time to time. Workman himself left the job at his own will without notice. 
It is prayed that the claim may be dismissed. 


Evidence of workman:- 


7. In order to prove his case, workman examined himself as WWI and tendered into evidence his affidavit 
WWI/A and has been cross examined by the AR for respondent no.!. AR for workman closed the evidence on 
30.07.2024. 


Evidence of respondent no.|:- 


8. Respondent no.l! examined Colonel K P Singh S/o Late Colonel P. P. Singh, OIC, Civic Cell, Station Head 
Quarter, Chandi Mandir Panchkula as MWI, who tendered into evidence his affidavit Ex.MWI/A along with 
documents 75.२] to Ex.R4. AR for respondent no. closed the evidence on !0.03.2026. 


Sr. No. Document Dated Exhibit 

l. Policy 8.03.20I] Ex.R-! 

2 Agreement with respondent no.2 05.07.208 Ex.R-2 

3. Copy of ECR of EPFO Ex.R-3 

4 Letter of Head Quarter 22.04.980 Ex.R-4 

9. It is pertinent to mention here that respondent no.2 was proceeded ex parte on 20.04.2023. 


Submissions of workman:- 


0. While arguing the case, AR for workman contended that in this case, workman was appointed by respondent 
no.| under a camouflage agreement with respondent no.2. The workman was appointed directly by respondent no.| 
on 23.0.20I7 as sweeper and her services were dispensed with on 27.0.20l8 without any reason in violation of 
Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act as no compensation was paid to the workman and junior to him were retained 


[भाग [ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 4245 


in service and fresh appointment were made after termination of the workman. So far as the agreements between the 
respondent no.| & 2 are concerned, they are totally not tenable and is unfair labour practice. The work performed by 
the workman is of perennial in nature. The contractor was not having any license. The contractor has left after three 
months. No domestic inquiry was held before terminating the service of the workman, which is mandatory under the 
Act. 


Submissions of respondent no.:- 

Il. On the other hand, AR for respondent no.! contended that the workman was engaged by respondent no.2 
(contractor) as per agreement executed between respondent no.| & 2 on outsource basis and there is no relationship 
of employer and employee between respondent no.! and the workman. Wages were paid by the respondent no.2 to 
the workman and there was no role of respondent no.| in appointing or terminating the services of the workman. 
Workman is employee of respondent no.2. If there is any violation of provision of the Act, then it is done by 
respondent no.2. As per Ex.R3, all dues were paid by the contractor with regard to EPF and ESI etc. The workman 
and other safai karamcharis formed union in the Headquarter, which is not permitted as per Ex.R4. Respondent no.| 
being the Government Agency, workman is trying to take benefit of his appointment by respondent no.2 and has 
filed a false claim after concealing material facts and claim of the workman is not maintainable. 


2. Ihave given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 


Findings:- 

3. The first contention regarding the applicant is that whether applicant comes within the definition of 
workman as is defined in Section 2(s) of the Act. I may mention that applicant was appointed as sweeper as per his 
claim petition and affidavit submitted before the Tribunal. In plain words the applicant was performing his duties as 
labourer/unskilled worker. She was not in supervisory or administrative post requiring him to perform only 
administrative duties. While interpreting Section 2(s) Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Devinder Singh Vs. 
Municipal Council, Sanaur, AIR 20 Supreme Court 2532,has observed as follows:- 


“The source of employment, the quantum of recruitment, the terms & conditions of employment contract of 
service, the quantum of wages/ pay and mode of payment are not at all relevant for deciding whether or not a 
person is a workman within the meaning of Section 2(s) of the Act. The definition of workman also does not 
make any distinction between full time and part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. 
There is nothing in the plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be inferred that only person 
employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing whole time job is a workman and the one 
employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty 
for fixed hours is not a workman.” 


4. Thus Hon’ble Supreme Court has clarified that the definition of workman also does not make any distinction 
between full time or part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. There is nothing in plain language 
of Section 2(s) from which it can be infer that only person employed on regular basis or a person employed for 
doing whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages 
or as a casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a workman. In view of the ratio of law enunciated in 
the above ruling, in my considered opinion the applicant herein admittedly falls within the definition of ‘workman’ 
under Section 2(s) of the Act. 


5. The Hon’ble Supreme Court after analyzing the catena of cases has laid down in Balwant Raj Saluja Vs. Air 
India Limited in Civil Appeal ७०.70266 dated 25.08.20/4, two well recognized tests to find out whether the 
labours are the contract employees of the principal employer are:- 


3) Whether the principal employer pays the salary instead of contractor and 
2) Whether the principal employer controls and supervise the work of the employees? 


6. The facts regarding the payment of salary by the management or contractor has not been stated in the claim 
petition of the workman. In fact, claim petition is totally silent regarding the payment of wages, salary, letter of 
appointment by respondent no.| or by contractor or anything likewise. Thus, this basic feature for holding the 
relationship of employer and employee i.e. between respondent no.l and workman totally lacking not in the 
pleading but also in the evidence submitted by the workman. However, MWI Colonel K P Singh in his cross 
examination has stated that it is responsibility of the contractor to pay monthly wages to the conservancy staff 
provided by him. Respondent no.! has nothing to do with the minimum wages, DC rate to the conservancy staff. 
Contractor is responsible to give notice to the conservancy staff provided by him. Respondent no.! has no roll in 
terminating the services of the workman. Thus, it was the respondent no.2 who was required to ensure the payment 
of wages to the workers engaged by him from time to time. 


7. It is also added here that respondent no.! has also placed on record documents Ex.R-| to Ex.R-4. Ex.R-2 is 
the contract agreement between respondent no.l & 2. Thus, from the adduced evidence, it emerges that workman 
was paid salary by the contractor and she was being controlled and supervised by the contractor. Thus, there does 
not exist any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.l. However, there 
exists relation of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no. 2. 


4246 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 6, 2026/VAISAKHA 26, 948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


8. It is also added here that as per definition of contractor as given in the Section 2(c) of the Contract Labour 
(Regulation and Abolition) Act, 970 (hereinafter called as Contract Act), respondent no.2 was supplying contract 
labour to the respondent no.!. Thus, respondent no.2 would fall under the definition of contractor. Section 2(c) of 
the Contract Act is reproduced as under: 


“Contractor”, in relation to an establishment, means a person who undertakes to produce a given result for the 
establishment, other than a mere supply of goods or articles of manufacture to such establishment, through contract 
labour or who supplies contract labour for any work of the establishment and includes a sub-contractor; 


9. The Hon’ble Apex Court in the case of Banglore Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A. Rajappa 
2928(36) FLR 266 dealt at length with the ambit and scope of expression “industry” as defined in Section 2(j) of the 
Act, and has laid down triple test for determining whether a particular establishment is industry or not. The triple test 
is where (a) systematic activity (b) organized by cooperation between employer and employee (the direct and 
substantial element is commercial (c) for the production or distribution of goods and services to calculated to satisfy 
human wants and wishes prima facie is an “Industry” in that enterprise. The workman was doing work i.e. systematic 
activity for respondent no.| at the asking of respondent no.2 and there was cooperation between the workman and 
respondent no.2, which involve substantial commercial element for rendering service to satisfy human wants. Thus, 
respondent no.2 would be termed as industry. 


20. Workman in her deposition has clearly stated that there was non-compliance of Section 25F, 25G and 25H 
of the Act. She also stated that she was appointed on 23.0!.20l7 and she was paid minimum wages prescribed by 
Central Government and was working with respondent no.l. She was not given any compensation before terminating 
his services on 27.0.208. In his cross examination, she has further stated that her salary used to be credited in his 
bank account. But she has not placed on record any bank account, in which her salary was credited in order to enable 
this Court to see who was making payment to the workman. However, there is no denial that the workman was paid 
salary by respondent no.2 (contractor). However, from the agreement dated 05.07.20i8 (Ex.R-2) placed on file by 
respondent no.l, it emerges that safai karamcharis were provided by respondent no.2 (contractor) to work with 
respondent no.l. Thus, workman was employee of respondent no.2 and not of respondent no.! as has been discussed 
above. There does not exist any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.|. 
However, there exists relation of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


2I. Admittedly, there is non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2. However, as regards 
violation of other provisions i.e. Section 25G and 25H of the Act, there is no evidence lead by the workman. Hence, 
workman is entitled for compensation from respondent no.2. 


22 It is also added here that there is no iota of evidence that any unfair labour practice used by respondent no.| 
by entering into contract with contractors for supply of safai karamcharis and agreement executed between 
respondent no.! and 2 cannot be said to be any unfair labour practice. So far as contentions of AR for workman that 
the work performed by the workman is of perennial in nature and no domestic inquiry was held before terminating 
the services of workman are concerned, in this regard, it is pointed out that workman was entitled only for 
compensation under Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2 (contractor) and no domestic inquiry was required to 
be conducted. Merely because work is of perennial in nature does not mean that any right has accrued in favour of the 
workman to get employment with respondent no.l, which is a department of Civil Defence run by Government of 
India and for that regular recruitment procedure is provided. Workman cannot seek backdoor entry into service. 
Moreover, workman is not employee of respondent no.! being employee of respondent no.2 (contractor). 


23. In view of above discussion, it is held that since respondent no.2 (contractor) is currently not working with 
respondent no.|, so keeping in view the tenure of service rendered by the workman and salary given to him during the 
period of his stay with the contractor and non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act, this Tribunal is of the considered 
opinion that workman is entitled for compensation. 


24. Keeping in view the fact and circumstances of the present case, the following scheme of compensation is 
deemed fit by this Tribunal: 

i. Workman who worked for less than 2 years with contractor shall be entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 

il. Workman who worked for more than 2 years but less than 5 years with contractor shall be entitled 

Rs.30,000/-. 

ili. Workman who worked for more than 5 years with contractors shall be entitled for Rs.50,000/-. 

25. Present workman employed on 23.0!.20!7 and was terminated on 27.0.208 (less than 2 years), so she is 
entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 

26. Respondent no.2 is directed to pay the workman Rs.20,000/- within one month from the date of the 


notification of the award failing which the respondent no.2 shall further pay interest @6% per annum from the date 
of award till the date of making payment. It is also added here that in case respondent no.! requires safai 
karamcharis, then workman may be engaged through any contractor on sympathetic ground. 


27s Let copy of this award be sent to Central Government for publication as required under Section |7 of the 
Act. 


KAMAL KANT, PO-cum-Link Officer

In case No. R/36/209- 


As per letter dated /02/209 by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference is 
made to this Tribunal under Section-0 of Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (in short the ‘Act’)as per Notification No. L- 
20/87/208 (IR/B-ID) dt. !/02/209. The dispute under reference relates to: 


“() Whether the Applicants in the dispute (as per list appended in Annexure-A) who were & are engaged 
by the Indian Overseas Bank in their respective Branches as Individual contractors are workmen within the 
meaning of Section 2(s) of the I.D. Act, 7947 & if so whether the contract agreement so entered is a mere 
camouflage and a smoke screen? 


(ti) Whether, the Applicants claim for permanent absorption is justified? If yes, what relief the applicants 
are entitled to?” 


In case No. R/37/2023 — 


As per letter dated 24/26.04.2023 by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference 
is made to this Tribunal under Section-0 of Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (in short the ‘Act’)as per Notification No. 
RP-7(I-)/2023-ES.III dt. 24/26.04.2023. The dispute under reference relates to: 


“Whether the Action on the part of the Management of Indian Overseas Bank, Raipur (CG) in 
Terminating the services of Shri Bodhram Karsh during the pendency of proceedings before the CGIT Jabalpur is 
Legal and Justified ? If not, to what relief Shri Bodhram Karsh is entitled to ?” 


Since nature of dispute in the Case R/37/2023 is covered in the case R/36/209, hence this case has been 
consolidated with R/36/20l9 vide order of this Tribunal. R/36/20I9 is a leading case. Both the cases are being 
disposed by a common Award. 


Case of the Workman Union is mainly that, the Theka Mazdoor Sangh is the Registered Union which is 
affiliated to Bharitya Mazdoor Sangh, its Registration No. is 43. The Applicant is its General Secretary and has been 
authorized by the Claimants, who are the members of the Union as well by the Union to prosecute this case. The 32 
workers whose names are enclosed with the list of reference, were initially engaged by Bank on the post of 
Messenger/Peon on the different Branches at Raipur and Durg Division against vacant and permanent post after 
interview in full time Peon/Messenger/Safai Cleaner but were not paid wages admissible to temporary employees or 
Collector rate declared by the Government in Bank Centre, though they worked for the full day. They are ‘ workman’ 
under Section 2(s) and the Management Bank is an ‘/ndustry’ under Section 2(j) of the Act. 


That as per the reference schedule list Annexed as Annexure-'A’' of the all 32 employees, the workmen no. |

The workmen no. 0I, 45 and 9 are terminated bythe management during conciliation proceeding because 
they are active members of the union and rest of all workmen’s are continue in service with the management/second 
party in their branches. It is submitted that the management taking work continuously as a messenger/peon from the 
all workmen’s and uses their power an agreement for the cleaning and housekeeping work done with the workmen 
and paying wages only work of cleaning and housekeeping work as per agreement. The management some of the 
workmen’s giving wages of messenger/peon as per daily rate which is colorable exercise of the power and only 
avoiding the provisions of labour laws and liability of regularization and pay-scale and also provident fund they have 
forcefully done the agreement for cleaning and housekeeping work with the workmen’s and continue in service from 
many years from the initial date of appointment in the bank. The work taking by the management bank is perennial in 
nature which has also same work done by the regular and permanent employee of the management bank but 
management paid wages as per agreement to the some workmen and daily wage to the some workman which is unfair 
labour practice adopted by the management with the all workmen. 


It is also the case of the Union that, these workmen continuously worked for full day with the Management 
Bank and thus acquired status of permanent employees but were not granted benefits admissible to permanent 
employees. There is circular of Management dated 23.03.20l! applicable to all the Branches and Regions which 
provides that absorption of Messengers/Sweepers on permanent posts who are engaged in the Branches/Offices of the 
Bank subject to their confirming to recruitment norms. As per circular of the management workmens are entitle to 
absorb in permanent post after completing 240 days continuously in a calendar year and all workmen continuously 
working with the management from the date of his initial appointment in the bank and already completed 240 days in 
every calendar year but the management not absorb the workmens on permanent post and not giving benefit of pay- 
scale and using power forcefully made agreement with the workers for cleaning and sweeping work as per agreement 
rate of per square fit area of the bank building but they have been taking work with the workers full time from the 
opening of the bank to close the bank and paying wages only for cleaning and housekeeping work and want to see 
that workmans are contractual employee. This is colourable exercise of the power by the management the personal 
service contract/agreement done by the management with the workmans forcefully and that is bogus document. The 
management only make a paper arrangement which is colourable exercise of the power and personnel contract 
agreement system entered by the management is sham and camouflage and all workmens are entitled to absorb in 
permanent post of messenger/peon/Sweeper as per their engagement and work 


Since, these workmen though shown to be working as Contract worker on contract of cleaning granted to 
them by the Bank have worked on full times basis and continuously with the Bank. They have discharged all the 
duties discharged by the permanent employees working as Peons/Cleaners. Their nature of work is also of permanent 
and perennial in nature, thus the Management Bank has adopted unfair labour practice by way of showing them work 
contractors which is sham, bogus and camouflage adopted by Bank just to deny their rights with respect to permanent 
status and regularization. This action of Management is unjust, illegal and arbitrary. The Union has prayed that 
holding this action of Management Bank showing the workmen as work contractors as a camouflage and a smoke 
screen as well sham transaction, they be held entitled to be granted status of full time permanent employee. 


Case of Management Bank is mainly that, there was a settlement between the Bank Unions and 
Management of the Bank and Circular dated 23.03.20l was issued by Bank as a one-time measure for absorption of 
casuals/temporary Messenger/sweepers working with the Bank. 


That, it is specifically mentioned in the aforesaid Circular that the absorption of causal/temporary 
messenger/sweepers shall be in aphased manner. The circular is applicable for causal/temporary messenger/sweepers 
those who have worked in permanent vacancies. The absorption of casual or temporary messengers/sweepers, those 
who have been worked in permanent vacancies were divided into three phases, Firstly, the causal temporary 
messengers/ sweepers who have worked for more than S years in any of the branches/offices in permanent vacancies 
and completed 240 days or more than days continuously in a calendar year as on I5.!!.20I0 and are still working 
shall be absorbed in Phase- I before 30.06.20ll, subject to their completing/submitting the required 
information/certificates. Secondly, causal or temporary messengers/ sweepers who have worked for more than 3 years 
but less than 5 years in any of the branches/offices in permanent vacancies and completed 240 days or more days 
continuously in a calendar year as on I5.!.20l0 and are sill working shall be absorbed in Phase-II, once the 
absorption under Phase-I is completed, subject to their submitting the required information/certificates and Thirdly, 
causal temporary messengers/sweepers who have completed 240 days or more days continuously in a calendar year 


4250 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 6, 2026/VAISAKHA 26, 948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


and have been engaged by branches/offices in permanent vacancies for less than 3 years as on I5..20I0 and are still 
working shall be absorbed in Phase-lI, once the absorption under Phase-II is completed, subject to their submitting the 
required information/certificates. It is noteworthy that the causal or temporary messengers/sweepers who are covered 
I under the settlement and Circular for absorption were required to submit undertaking as per Annexure A-I attached 
to the Circular dated 23.03.20. 


That, it is provided in the Circular dated 23.03.20 that any conditional undertaking or undertaking in any 
other format and or refusal to the order of placemen/deployment shall be deemed as refusal to absorption of such 
concern causal temporary person and in such an event their claim for absorption shall not be considered thereafter, 
Moreover, causal or temporary messengers/ sweepers who are absorbed in Bank service shall be liable to be 
terminated if it is found at any time that he/she has made a false declaration in the undertaking letter with regard to 
number of days they have been engaged or he/she submitted a false fake documents service for joining the Non- 
Applicant/Bank. 


That, the circular further provides that the future vacancies of sweeper vacancies arise on or after 5..200 
are outsourced. Hence, there is no permanent vacancies of sweeper post in the Non-applicant Bank. The settlement 
arrived between the General Secretary of Indian Overseas Bank Union and the management of the Indian Overseas 
Bank, Chennai on !7.02.20il, is not applicable to persons if any engaged on causal or temporary basis after 
5..20i0 or who has not completed 240 days in a calendar year as on I5.!.200. The said condition indicates that 
a cut-off date was provided in the memorandum of settlement dated !7.02.20 itself and thus, the benefit of this 
circular cannot be given to such workmen who were engaged on causal or temporary basis after 
5..200.Thecandidates of applicant are also required to complete 240 days in a Calendar year subject to requested 
qualification before cut of dated i.e I5.!.20l0 to make them eligible for absorption. In the present matter all the 
candidates were provided service as freelancer after the cut-off provided in the memorandum of settlement dated 
7.02.20 las well as circular dated 23.03.20.Therefore, the candidates of applicant did not come with the ambit of 
the circular dated 23.03.20 and no benefit of the circular dated 23.03.20 can be given to the Applicant/Union to 
get absorption in the Non-Applicant/Bank. 


That, the candidates of applicant at Serial No. l, [5, 2] and 32 of list annexed as Annexure A-l, were not 
appointed on the basis of contract, the Non-Applicant Bank never entered into the contract with the workman at serial 
l, I5, 2] and 32. Thus, the question of termination of their services does not arise. Thus, the assertion of the 
Applicant/Union is without substance and is hereby denied. 


That, the other candidates of applicant have entered into a contract with Non-Applicant Bank on different 
dates, the details of which are given in below mentioned table. From bare perusal of the aforesaid contract would 
reveal that the candidates of the applicant were not required to provide service entire day in the branches of Non- 
Applicant Bank. Itis specifically mentioned in in the contract that the candidates of the applicant are free to carry out 
any other work as per their own wishes after their work in branches is completed. The clause 6 of the agreement is 
quoted below for kind perusal of this Hon’ble Tribunal. 


While the second party is free to carry on any trade or occupation, on his/her own, he'she should make

That, as per the agreement the candidates of the applicant were entitled to receive Rs.3/-per sgft in rural areas 
and Rs. 2l-per sgft in urban areas for cleaning the branch. It is pertinent to note that all the candidates of the applicant 
who have executed the agreement have adhered to the terms and conditions of the agreement. Thus, the same is 
binding on the candidates, the candidates on the later stage cannot question the nature of service rendered by them, as 
they were fully aware about the nature of work, which they were required to do in the branches of Non-Applicant
