Thus according to Management Bank, the applicants involved in the reference have never been under 
employment of the Bank as a employee in any capacity rather they have been work contractors who have been given 
contract of cleaning by the Bank extended from time to time as mention in the list above. They use to do this job just 
for few hours in the Bank. They are not appointed through legal channels following recruitment process against any 
sanctioned vacancy hence firstly, there is no relation of employee and employer between the parties and secondly, 
they are not entitled to the reliefs claimed. The Management Bank has requested that the reference to be answered 
against the Workman Union. 


During proceedings, 2 workmen filed two applications dated 08..2023 and 2..2023 stating that 
their claim has been settled between them and Management Bank and have requested that no dispute Award 
be passed with respect to their claims. These applications were transcription of order of this Tribunal dated 
05.2.2023 stating that no dispute Award shall be passed with respect to these 2 workmen when the final 
award will be passed. These 2 workmen are Motilal, Naresh Kumar Sahu, Bhupendra Kumar Kosariya, 
Bhusan Sahu, Sunderlal Shrivas, Kishore Kumar Chouhan, Yogesh Kumar Shrivas, Deepak Sahu, Haresh 
Kumar Yadav, Rajan Jaysawal, Suresh Kumar Sahu, Toshbant Gartiya. 


In evidence, the Workman Union has filed affidavit of Workman Subash Chandra Sahu, Bodhram Karsh, 
Vinod Yadav, Toshbant Gartiya Om Prakash Sahu, Chandrakant Sahu, Ashok, they have been cross-examined from 
the side of Management Bank on their affidavits field as their examination in chief. The Management Bank has filed 
affidavit of Gatikrishna Panigarhi Chief Manager as his examination in chief. He has been cross-examined by 
Workman Union. 


The Workman Union has filed and proved documents which are Circular of Bank dated 23.03.20]], 
admitted and proved, marked as Exhibit १४- to W-5, Letter of Senior Manager to the Chief Manager of the Bank and 
the undertaking letter signed by the Workman Subash Chandra Sahu and certificate admitted by Management Bank, 
marked as Exhibit W-6 to W-9, Letter of Chief Manager dated 22..203 with regard to forwarding original HHC 
Certificate cum Mark-sheet of Subash Chandra Sahu marked as Exhibit ॥-0, different payment vouchers, total 09 in 
numbers which are admitted and marked as Exhibit W-I], notice issued by Assistant Labour Commissioner on 
22.03.20i8 and 05.06.20I8 which are Exhibit W-l2 and ४४-3, letter of Senior Manager sent to General Manager 
forwarding the undertaking letter from Workman Bodhram Karsh (Temporary Messenger) and Sarita Rathore (Part 
time sweeper), marked as Exhibit १४-4, Agreement for cleaning and housekeeping for different periods with different 
applicants which are Exhibit W-I5 to W-34. 


The Management has filed photocopy of memorandum of settlement between the Union and the 
Management Bank in circular dated 23.03.20l! issued by Bank in the light of settlkement, 27 work contract 
agreements for cleaning and house-keeping services between the individual applicants and Bank for different periods 
which are marked Exhibits M- to M-45. 


I have heard argument of Learned Counsel for the Workman Union Mr. Arun Patel and Mr. Abhinav S. 
Khedkar Learned Counsel for the Bank. Written submissions have also been filed on behalf of Bank, which is part of 
record, I have gone through the written statement and record of both the cases. 


At the very outset, it is to be meant clear that a no dispute Award will be passed with respect to the 
Workmen who have entered into settlement with the Management Bank, details mentioned earlier in this Judgment. 
Further, according to the Bank itself, the Workmen Bodhram Karsh, was providing service as a Contractor till the 
dispute in R/36/209 was raised. As it comes out from the reference in R/37/2023 referred to above this Bodhram 
Karsh was terminated according to reference during the pendency of the dispute i.e. Case R/36/20l9 before this 
Tribunal and this Tribunal has been required to adjudicate the reference in R/37/2023 whether the action of 
termination of Bodhram Karsh by the Bank during pendency of the dispute before this Tribunal is justified or not. 


Needless to mention here that, the case of Management Bank with respect to Both Ram Karsh is also the 
same as it is with respect to the other claimants that they were awarded contracts of cleaning for different periods in 
different Branches of the Bank, extended from time to time. Contract with some of the Applicants mention in the para 
of the Written Submission of the Bank are continuing and has been discontinuing with respect to other claimants. 


On perusal of record in the light of rival arguments, following issues arise for determination in both the 
cases. 


4254 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 6, 2026/VAISAKHA 26, 948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


4. Whether, the work contracts between the Management Bank and Applicants are camouflage and some 
screen? 


2. Whether, the claim of the applicants for permanent absorption is justified? 
Issue No. I — 
Pleadings of the parties on this issue have been elaborated earlier in this Judgment. 


Learned Counsel for the Applicants has submitted that the Applicants have been working with the Bank on 
full time basis, the so called argument with respect to cleaning and house keeping stated to be entered between the 
Bank and the Applicants, is mere camouflage, a smoke screen just to deny the Applicants their claim with respect to 
permanency in status and regularization. Learned Counsel has referred to judgment of Hon’ble Supreme Court in the 
case of Devendra Singh V.s. Municipal Corporation Sandore (20ll) 4 SC Page 62. Hon’ble Supreme Court has laid 
down that the definition of the ‘Workman’ under Section 2(s) of the Act does not make any distinction between full 
time and permanent employee or a person appointed on contract basis. Also that, there is nothing under Section 2(s) 
of the Act from which it can be inferred that only a person employed on a regular basis or a person employed for 
whole time job is a ‘workman’ and the one employed on temporary, part-time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a 
casual employee or for doing a duty for fixed hours is not a Workman. Para 3, l4 and I5 of this judgment have 
referred to in this respect. 


Learned Counsel for Workmen has further submitted that by way of these work agreements, the Bank has 
practiced unfair labour practice, which is prohibited under the Act and this Tribunal is well within its powers to lift 
the wave to enquire into the fact whether this so called work contract is a sham and camouflage just to deny these 
Applicants their legally admissible claims. 


I have gone through the submissions of the Applicants in both the cases. There is on record affidavits of the 
Applicants Subash Chandra Sahu, son of Srinath Sahu, Bodhram Karsh, Vinod Yadav, Toshbant Gartiya, Om Prakash 
Sahu, Chandrakant Sahu, Ashok as their examination in chief. In their affidavits filed as their examination in chief, 
the applicants Subash Chandra Sahu has stated that he was first appointed as a temporary mazdoor in Bhilai Branch of 
the Bank on 2.0!.2008, his work was filing, voucher stitching, maintenance of voucher register, taking posts and 
cheques to different Branches, cleaning of the Bank and all the other jobs directed by the Branch Manager. He worked 
from the date of appointment i.e. !2.0!.2008 to 8.04.2008. Applicant Bodhram Karsh has stated that, he was first 
appointed in the Korba Branch of the Bank on 09.0!.200 as a temporary Mazdoor, he worked till 30..2022 in his 
affidavit he has further stated that he was also doing the same jobs which the applicants Subash Chandra Sahu was 
doing. Applicant Vinod Kumar as stated that, he was first engaged in March, 20]4 and worked till I4..2022 his 
statement with regards to the post and nature of jobs is the same as stated by the Applicant Subash Chandra Sahu and 
Bodhram Karsh. Applicant Toshbant Gartiya has stated that he was appointed as a temporary sweeper/cleaner on 
08.02.20i3 and worked till 3!.03.2022. His statement with regards to nature of his work and duties is same as that of 
the applicant Subash and others. Applicant Chandrakant Sahu has stated that, he was first appointed on 23.0.204 
and continued till 3!.07.2023, Applicant Ashok has stated that he was first appointed in May, 204 as temporary 
mazdoor cum sweeper and continued till 07.06.202, regarding the nature of work and duties preferred his statement 
as same as that of applicants Subash Chandra and others. All these witnesses have further stated that they were paid 
on monthly basis by way of payment voucher, the Bank had pressed upon them that they will be granted permanent 
status. They were made to sign the work agreements by the Bank under the impression that they will be granted 
permanent status and they can request it only when they sign these agreements. They have further stated that they 
worked continuously as mazdoor cum cleaner for the whole day and were disengaged during pendency of the dispute. 


In their cross-examination, they have generally stated that their appointment was on daily rate basis, the 
post against which they were engaged was permanent. They did not have any objection to work at other place during 
the Banking hours; they have also admitted that their first work contract was extended twice. They further stated that 
they were not issued any appointment letter. They did not follow any recruitment process. They are engaged only on 
the basis of interview taken by the Branch Manager. 


The witness from Bank has stated that after the process of regularization in the light of Circular dated 
23.09.20i issued for regularization/absorption of part-time casual daily wage employee working in different 
branches of Bank in the light of the settlement between the Bank and employees Union, the Applicant workmen were 
granted contracts of cleaning and housekeeping of the Branches for two years which was extended further for two 
years or three times. They were paid their wages in the light of their work contracts on per sq. ft. basis. Their work 
was only for few hours. In cross-examination, this witness has admitted that two applicants, which were working 
before 20ll were not given benefit of Circular of 20i] and he does not known the reason behind it. Before his this 
statement, he has denied the case of the Applicants Bodhram Karsh and Shubash Chandra Sahu that they were first 
engaged in 200 and 2008 respectively as claimed by them. 


Learned Counsel for the Management Bank has stated that after 20l, the Bank stopped recruitment of 
cleaners and housekeepers, it entered into contracts with different persons, initially for two years and which was 
extended later on. The Applicants entered into a Work contract with the Bank in different Branches for cleaning and 
housekeeping of the Branches. They were paid their wages on per sq. ft. basis. These agreements have been filed and 


[भाग aves 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 4255 


proved by the Bank hence, there is no relation of workman and employee between the applicants and the Bank as 
defined under the Act. He further submits that there is no evidence to show that these work contracts were camouflage 
or sham except the serving statement of the Applicants. 


Section 2(ra) read with Schedule 4 describes unfair labour practice adopted by Management. It is being 
reproduced as follows - 


2(ra) "unfair labour practice" means any of the practices specified in the Fifth Schedule; 
On the part of employers and trade unions of emplloyers 
3. To interfere with, restrain from, or coerce, workmen in the exercise of their right to organize, from, 


Join or assist a trade union or to engage in concerted activities for the purposes of collective bargaining 
or orther mutual aid or protection, that is to stay:- 


(a) threating wormen with discharge or dismissal, if they join a trade union; 
(b) threating a lock-out or closure, if a trade union is organized; 


(c) granting wage increase to workmen at crucial periods of trade union organization, with a view to 
undermining the efforts of the trade union organization. 


2. To dominate, interfere with or contribute support, financial or otherwise, to any trade union, that is to 
Stay:- 


(a) an employer taking an active interest in organising a trade union of wormen; and 


(b) an employer showing partially or granting favour to one of several trade unions attempting to organize 
his workmen ot to its members, where such a trade union is not a recognized trade union. 


3. To establish employer sponsored trade unions of workmen. 


4. To encourage or discourage membership in any trade union by discriminating against any workmen, 
that is to stay;- 


(a) discharging or punishment a workmen, because he urged other workmen to join or organize a trade 
union; 


(b) discharging or punishment a workmen for takimg part in any strike (not being as strike which is 
deemed to be an illegal strike under this Act); 


(c) changing seniority rating of workmen because of trade union activities; 
(व) refusing to promote workmen to higher posts on account of their trade union activities; 


(e) giving unmerited promotions to certain workmen with a view to creating discord amongst other 
workmen, or to undermine the strength of their trade union; 


(f) discharging office-bearers or active members of the trade union on account of their trade union 
activities/ 


5. To discharge or dismiss workmen 

(a) by way of victimisation; 

(b) not in good faith, but in the colourable exercise of the employers rights; 

(c) by falsely implicating a workmen in a criminal case on false evidence or on concocted evidence; 
(d) for penalty false reasons; 

(e) on untrue or trumped up allegation of absence without leave; 


(f) in utter disregard of the principles of natural justice in the conduct of domestic enquiry or with undue 
haste; 


(g) for misconduct or a minor or technical character, without having any regard to the nature of the 
particular misconduct or the past record or service of the workmen, thereby leading to a 
disproportionate punishment. 


6. To abolish the work of a regulat nature being done by workmen, and to give such work to contractors 
as a measure of breaking a strike. 


7. To transfer a workmen mala fide from one place to another under the guise of following management 
policy. 


8.To insist upon individual workmen, who are on a legal strike to sign a good conduct bond, as a pre- 
condition to allowing them to resume work. 


4256 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 6, 2026/VAISAKHA 26, 948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


9. To show favourisation or partially to one set of workers regardless of merit. 


40, To employ workmen as "badlis", casuals or temporaries and to continue them as such for years, with 
the object of depriving them of the same and privileges of permanent workmen. 


Il. To discharge or discriminate against any workmen for filing charges or testifying against and 
employer in any enquiry or proceeding relating to any industrial dispute. 


42, To recruit workmen during a strike which is not an illegal strike. 

43, Failure to implement award, settlemt or agreement. 

4, To indulging in acts of force or violence / 

45. To refuse to bargain collectively, in good faith with the recognised trade unions. 
46. Proposing or continuing a lock-out deemed to be illegal under this Act. 


Learned Counsel for the Applicants has submitted that these so called work agreements are being 
personated by the Bank since more than 40 years for the same nature of job. It is established that the job is of 
permanent nature and is available with the Bank. He further submits that, when the job is available which is the 
permanent in nature, the Bank has prohibited under Section 70 of Contract Labour (Regulation & Abolition) Act, 
4970 to employee Contractors for these jobs. It shows that they so called work contracts are sham and camouflage 
because their very intention is to do an Act which is prohibited under the law. He has referred to Section 25T and 25U 
of the Act in this respect which prohibits unfair labour practice. 


Perusal of the 4 Schedule under Section 2(ra) of the Act, as mentioned above makes it clear that Awarding 
a Work Contract even though the Work is of permanent nature is not included in the acts which are unfair labour 
practice. 


“Section 70 of the Contract Labour (Regulation and Abolition) Act, 7970 is reproduced as follows - 
40. Prohibition of employment of contract labour.- 


(7) Notwithstanding anything contained in this Act, the appropriate Government may, after 
consultation with the Central Board or, as the case may be, a State Board, prohibit, by 
notification in the Official Gazette, employment of contract labour in any process, operation or 
other work in any establishment. 


(2) Before issuing any notification under sub-section (7) in relation to an establishment, the 
appropriate Government shall have regard to the conditions of work and benefits provided for the 
contract labour that establishment and other relevant factors, such as- 


(a) whether the process, operation or other work is incidental to, or necessary for the 
industry, trade, business, manufacture or occupation that is carried on in the 
establishment ; 


(b) whether it is of perennial nature, that is to say, it is so of sufficient duration having 
regard to the nature of industry, trade, business, manufacture or occupation carried on 
in that establishment ; 


(c) whether it is done ordinarily through regular workmen in that establishment or an 
establishment similar thereto; 


(व) whether it is sufficient to employ considerable number of whole-time workmen. 


This provides with respect to Abolition of Contract Labour in works of permanent and perennial nature, it 
does not abolish Awarding of Contract for the Work which does not involve manpower supply by the Contractor. 


Hence, in the light of these facts, the action of Management in Awarding work contracts to the Applicants 
for cleaning and housekeeping cannot be held to be a unfair labour practice or prohibited under Section 70० of 
Contract Labour (Regulation and Abolition) Act, 7970. 


It is also held that, the Applicants have failed to prove these work contracts as sham or camouflage. 


Two Workmen Bodhram Karsh and Shubash Chandra Sahu have taken a case that they are first appointed in 
20l0 and 2008 respectively i.e. before the Circular of 20 (referred to above) was made applicable and were not 
granted benefit under the Circular. Details of the Circulars have been stated earlier. Both the workmen have 
corroborated their this case in their affidavits as their examination in chief and been cross examined by the 
Management side. In case reference No. R/37/2023 the Workman Bodhram Karsh has filed and proved a Certificate 
dated 04.05.20I! issued by the Branch Head which shows that this Workman has been engaged continuously for 240 
days or more in a year preceding !5.!.20l0 and that no other person has been engaged in his place. Another 
document, which is part of this certificate Exhibit ४-] in R/37/2023 is under taking letter for Temporary 
Mazdoor/Sweeper signed by this applicant Bodhram Karsh in terms of Clause 3(B) of settlement dated 7.02.20I for 


[भाग II—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 4257 


absorption as temporary mazdoors/full-time/part-time sweepers. These documents established that Bodhram Karsh 
was working with the Bank since 20l0. He did apply for his absorption under the application for forwarding by 
Branch in this respect with his undertaking along with his documents. Case of Bodhram Karsh is that he was not 
granted benefit of the Circular with regard to his claim for regularization/absorption. 


In their statement of defense, the Bank is silent on this allegation in the statement of claim which has been 
proved by his affidavit and been cross-examined as well documents referred to above. In its written statement of 
defense, in the case R/36/209 also, on the point why claim of Bodhram Karsh was not granted. 


Learned Counsel for the Bank has submitted that the code offer link for grant of claim in the Circular of 
20] was that the Claimant should have completed 240 days in Calendar year as on 5..200. As stated earlier, the 
document Exhibit W-! filed in connected case R/37/2023, it speaks that the Applicant Bodhram Karsh was engaged 
on 09.0.200 and had completed 240 days or more continuously in a Calendar year preceding the date 5..200. 
Hence, it is held that the claimant Bodhram Karsh had fulfilled the conditions of grant of regular employment under 
the Circular of 20li. There is nothing on record from the side of Bank in form of document or affidavit to justify 
Learned Counsel on this claim of Applicant Bodhram Karsh. 


Since, it is established and proved that Bodhram Karsh was working as a casual employee on daily wage 
and had completed 240 days in a year preceding his disengagement as a casual labour, without any notice or 
compensation, disengagement of Bodhram Karsh is held in violation of Section 25F and 25G of the Act. 


It is therefore pleaded that, Bodhram Karsh signed work contract after reading agreement for cleaning and 
housekeeping along with other applicants but the fact still remains that he was disengaged without any notice or 
compensation though he had worked for 240 days in the year preceding the date of his disengagement which is 
against law. 


As regards the other applicant Subash Chandra there is nothing on record except his affidavit and cross- 
examination to corroborate his this claim that he was working since 2008, hence his claim that he was engaged since 
2008 is held not proved. 


Issue No. 4 is answered accordingly. 
Issue No. 2 — 


Learned Counsel for the Workmen has referred to judgment of Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of 
Maharastra State Road Transport Corporation Vs. Casteribe Rajya P. Karmchari Sanghatana (2009) 8 SCC 556. 
In this case it has been held that the referred judgment of Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Secretary, State of 
Karnataka Vs. Umadevi (2006) 4 SCC I will not help the employees, once unfair labour practice on the part of 
employee is established in such a case Industrial Tribunal and Labour Courts are empowered to issue prohibitory as 
well as positive direction to an erring employee (Para 26 of this Judgment be referred to in this respect). 


Another judgment of Supreme Court referred in the case of Amarkant Rai Vs. State of Bihar (2075) Vol. 8 
SCC 265 referred to from the side of the Workmen also does not help. 


The judgments of Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Secretary, Haryana State Electricity Board Vs. 
Suresh AIR (7999) SC 7760 can also be distinguished on facts. 


From the above discussion and findings, the applicants may be classified in three categories with respect 
to above prayed reliefs. 


4... First category of applicants should have been entered into settlement with the Management 
Bank, no dispute Award be passed with respect to them. 


2. The second category of Applicants, except Shubash Chandra Sahu which is held entitled to 
no relief in the light of findings recorded on Issue No. ॥. 


3. The third category of Applicant Bodhram Karsh he is held entitled to be considered for 
reinstatement and grant of permanent as well regularized status in the light of the Circular 
of the Management Bank dated 23.03.20]. 


In the light of above discussion and findings, the reference is answered as follows - 
AWARD 


3. First category of applicants who have been entered into settlement with the Management Bank, no dispute 
Award is passed with respect to them. 


2. The second category of Applicants Shubash Chandra Sahu which is held entitled to no relief in the light of 
findigs recorded on Issue No. 7. 


3. The third category of Applicants, Bodhram Karsh he is held entitled to be reinstated within 90 days from 
the date of Award and to be considered for grant of permanent as well regularize status in the light of the 


4258 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 6, 2026/VAISAKHA 26, 948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 
Circular of the Management Bank dated 23.03, 2077, within a period of six months fro tne date of Award. 


4. The Fourth category of Applicants who have worked with the Management Bank on different work 
contractors, are held entitled to no relief because these work contracts cannot be hele to be a camouflage.

l. Workman has filed the present claim petition under Section 2A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 


(hereinafter called the Act) against his termination w.e.f. 27.0.20l8 claiming reinstatement into service along with 
continuity of service and full back wages. 


2. It is maintained by the workman in the claim petition that that he was appointed as sweeper/safai karamchari 
on 0I.04.204 by respondent no.! at minimum wages prescribed by the Central Government. But the said minimum 
wages were never paid to him. Management has unlawfully deducted 2% EPF share, !.75% ESI and Rs.300/- as 
TDS from hissalary despite of the fact that his total wages comes under the ambit of taxfree limit. He was working 
under respondent no.! directly, but respondent no.! in order to avoid its liability always shows his employment in 
record under different contractors, which tantamount to unfair labour practice. 


3. It is further maintained that workman including 200 other safai karamcharis of respondent no.| raised the 
demand for non-deduction of TDS from the wages paid by respondent no.!. However, their demand was not 


[भाग aves 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 4259 


accepted. Other workers working under respondent no.! formed union and subsequently his services were dispensed 
with w.e.f. 27.0.20I8. It is also maintained that respondent no.! retained juniors in service and also made fresh 
appointments after termination of his services and no compensation was paid under Section 25F of the Act. Thus, 
there is violation of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act. Hence this petition. 


4. Notice of the petition was given to respondents. 


5. Respondent no.! filed reply to the claim petition maintaining therein that the workman has not come to the 
Court with clean hands as he was appointed as sweeper by respondent no.2 (contractor) at minimum wage rate as 
prescribed by the Central Government. As per Government Policy dated 8.03.20l] (Ex.R-l), Non-Cantonment 
Military Stations have been authorized to outsource conservancy safaiwala. The answering respondent entered into 
contract with various private agencies for outsourcing of sweepers (safai karamcharis). In July 208, the answering 
respondent entered into contract with M/s AA Foundation for safety and M/s Sunrise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd. through e- 
tendering for outsourcing of safai karamcharis to provide conservancy services in Chandi Mandir Military Station for 
the financial year 20!8-l9. Respondent no.2 submits bill for payment for the days the safai karamcharis performed 
their duties and answering respondent is only responsible to forward these bills to PCDA Western Command 
Chandigarh to release payment directly to the contractor’s account. Salary is paid to the workman by the contractor. 
Compliance of PF, ESI Rules etc. is carried out by the contractor as per contract agreement. Answering respondent 
has no responsibility of disbursement of wages and deduction of EPF and ESI etc. The workman and other safai 
karamcharis formed union in the headquarter, which is not permitted and also carried out demonstration inside the 
premises of Station Headquarter on 27.0.208 and failed to perform his duties. Thereafter, answering respondent 
asked respondent no.2 to ask the workers to perform their duties. Answering respondent has no role or responsibility 
for termination services of the workman. There is no relationship of employer and employee between the answering 
respondent and the workman. In view of Army Headquarter Letter, the civilian employees of the answering 
respondent cannot organize themselves into trade unions as station headquarter is an administrative office of the 
Government of India. In spite of that, workman along with other safai karamcharis formed union unauthorizedly and 
indulge in unlawful activities. It is maintained that workman is the employee of contractor and therefore, termination 
is the prerogative of the contractor. It is prayed that the claim be dismissed. 


6. Respondent no.2 also filed reply maintaining therein that workman worked with it w.e.f. 05.07.208 to 
27.0.208. Respondent no.2 has not terminated the services of workman. Workman was paid minimum wages as 
declared by the Centre Government from time to time. Workman himself left the job at his own will without notice. It 
is prayed that the claim may be dismissed. 


Evidence of workman:- 


7. In order to prove his case, workman examined himself as १४०७] and tendered into evidence his affidavit 
WWI/A and has been cross examined by the AR for respondent no.!. AR for workman closed the evidence on 
30.07.2024. 


Evidence of respondent no.|:- 


8. Respondent no.l! examined Colonel K P Singh S/o Late Colonel P. P. Singh, OIC, Civic Cell, Station Head 
Quarter, ChandiMandir Panchkula as ०७], who tendered into evidence his affidavit Ex.MWI/A along with 
documents 7.२] to Ex.R4. AR for respondent no.! closed the evidence on 0.03.2026.

3. Copy of ECR of EPFO Ex.R-3 
4 Letter of Head Quarter 22.04.980 Ex.R-4 
9. It is pertinent to mention here that respondent no.2 was proceeded ex parte on 20.04.2023. 


Submissions of workman:- 


0. While arguing the case, AR for workman contended that in this case, workman was appointed by respondent 
no.! under a camouflage agreement with respondent no.2. The workman was appointed directly by respondent no.| 
on 0.04.20i4 as sweeper and his services were dispensed with on 27.l0.20i8 without any reason in violation of 
Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act as no compensation was paid to the workman and junior to him were retained in 
service and fresh appointment were made after termination of the workman. So far as the agreements between the 
respondent no.! & 2 are concerned, they are totally not tenable and is unfair labour practice. The work performed by 
the workman is of perennial in nature. The contractor was not having any license. The contractor has left after three 
months. No domestic inquiry was held before terminating the service of the workman, which is mandatory under the 
Act. 


4260 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 6, 2026/VAISAKHA 26, 948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


Submissions of respondent no.|:- 


Il. On the other hand, AR for respondent no.! contended that the workman was engaged by respondent no.2 
(contractor) as per agreement executed between respondent no.l & 2 on outsource basis and there is no relationship 
of employer and employee between respondent no.l and the workman. Wages were paid by the respondent no.2 to 
the workman and there was no role of respondent no.| in appointing or terminating the services of the workman. 
Workman is employee of respondent no.2. If there is any violation of provision of the Act, then it is done by 
respondent no.2. As per Ex.R3, all dues were paid by the contractor with regard to EPF and ESI etc. The workman 
and other safai karamcharis formed union in the Headquarter, which is not permitted as per Ex.R4. Respondent no.| 
being the Government Agency, workman is trying to take benefit of his appointment by respondent no.2 and has filed 
a false claim after concealing material facts and claim of the workman is not maintainable. 


2. I have given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 


Findings:- 

3. The first contention regarding the applicant is that whether applicant comes within the definition of 
workman as is defined in Section 2(s) of the Act. I may mention that applicant was appointed as sweeper as per his 
claim petition and affidavit submitted before the Tribunal. In plain words the applicant was performing his duties as 
labourer/unskilled worker. He was not in supervisory or administrative post requiring him to perform only 
administrative duties. While interpreting Section 2(s) Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Devinder Singh Vs. 
Municipal Council, Sanaur, AIR 20I] Supreme Court 2532,has observed as follows:- 


“The source of employment, the quantum of recruitment, the terms & conditions of employment contract of 
service, the quantum of wages/ pay and mode of payment are not at all relevant for deciding whether or not a 
person is a workman within the meaning of Section 2(s) of the Act. The definition of workman also does not 
make any distinction between full time and part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. 
There is nothing in the plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be inferred that only person 
employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing whole time job is a workman and the one 
employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty 
for fixed hours is not a workman.” 


4. Thus Hon’ble Supreme Court has clarified that the definition of workman also does not make any distinction 
between full time or part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. There is nothing in plain language of 
Section 2(s) from which it can be infer that only person employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing 
whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a 
casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a workman. In view of the ratio of law enunciated in the 
above ruling, in my considered opinion the applicant herein admittedly falls within the definition of ‘workman’ under 
Section 2(s) of the Act. 


5. The Hon’ble Supreme Court after analyzing the catena of cases has laid down in Balwant Raj Saluja Vs. Air 
India Limited in Civil Appeal ४०.70266 dated 25.08, 207/4, two well recognized tests to find out whether the labours 
are the contract employees of the principal employer are:- 


3) Whether the principal employer pays the salary instead of contractor and 
2) Whether the principal employer controls and supervise the work of the employees? 


6. The facts regarding the payment of salary by the management or contractor has not been stated in the claim 
petition of the workman. In fact, claim petition is totally silent regarding the payment of wages, salary, letter of 
appointment by respondent no.! or by contractor or anything likewise. Thus, this basic feature for holding the 
relationship of employer and employee i.e. between respondent no.! and workman totally lacking not in the pleading 
but also in the evidence submitted by the workman.However, ५७४] Colonel K P Singh in his cross examination has 
stated that it is responsibility of the contractor to pay monthly wages to the conservancy staff provided by him. 
Respondent no.!l has nothing to do with the minimum wages, DC rate to the conservancy staff. Contractor is 
responsible to give notice to the conservancy staff provided by him. Respondent no.! has no roll in terminating the 
services of the workman. Thus, it was the respondent no.2 who was required to ensure the payment of wages to the 
workers engaged by him from time to time. 


7. It is also added here that respondent no.! has also placed on record documents Ex.R-| to Ex.R-4. Ex.R-2 is 
the contract agreement between respondent no.! & 2. Thus, from the adduced evidence, it emerges that workman was 
paid salary by the contractor and he was being controlled and supervised by the contractor. Thus, there does not exist 
any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.|. However, there exists relation 
of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


8. It is also added here that as per definition of contractor as given in the Section 2(c) of the Contract Labour 
(Regulation and Abolition) Act, 970 (hereinafter called as Contract Act), respondent no.2 was supplying contract 
labour to the respondent no.l. Thus, respondent no.2 would fall under the definition of contractor. Section 2(c) of the 
Contract Act is reproduced as under: 


[भाग aves 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 426I 


“Contractor”, in relation to an establishment, means a person who undertakes to produce a given result for the 
establishment, other than a mere supply of goods or articles of manufacture to such establishment, through contract 
labour or who supplies contract labour for any work of the establishment and includes a sub-contractor; 


9. TheHon’ble Apex Court in the case of Banglore Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A. Rajappa /9०7280(36) 


FLR 266 dealt at length with the ambit and scope of expression “industry” as defined in Section 2(j) of the Act, and 
has laid down triple test for determining whether a particular establishment is industry or not. The triple test is where 
(a) systematic activity (b) organized by cooperation between employer and employee (the direct and substantial 
element is commercial (c) for the production or distribution of goods and services to calculated to satisfy human 
wants and wishes prima facie is an “Industry” in that enterprise. The workman was doing work i.e. systematic activity 
for respondent no. at the asking of respondent no.2 and there was cooperation between the workman and respondent 
no.2, which involve substantial commercial element for rendering service to satisfy human wants. Thus, respondent 
no.2 would be termed as industry. 


20. Workman in his deposition has clearly stated that there was non-compliance of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of 
the Act. He also stated that he was appointed on 0.04.204 and he was paid minimum wages prescribed by Central 
Government and was working with respondent no.l. He was not given any compensation before terminating his 
services on 27.!0.208. In his cross examination, he has further stated that his salary used to be credited in his bank 
account. But he has not placed on record any bank account, in which his salary was credited in order to enable this 
Court to see who was making payment to the workman. However, there is no denial that the workman was paid salary 
by respondent no.2 (contractor). However, from the agreement dated 05.07.20I8 (Ex.R-2) placed on file by 
respondent no.l, it emerges that safai karamcharis were provided by respondent no.2 (contractor) to work with 
respondent no.l. Thus, workman was employee of respondent no.2 and not of respondent no.| as has been discussed 
above. There does not exist any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.|. 
However, there exists relation of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


2I. Admittedly, there is non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2. However, as regards 
violation of other provisions i.e. Section 25G and 25H of the Act, there is no evidence lead by the workman. Hence, 
workman is entitled for compensation from respondent no.2. 


22, It is also added here that there is no iota of evidence that any unfair labour practice used by respondent no.| 
by entering into contract with contractors for supply of safai karamcharis and agreement executed between 
respondent no.! and 2 cannot be said to be any unfair labour practice. So far as contentions of AR for workman that 
the work performed by the workman is of perennial in nature and no domestic inquiry was held before terminating 
the services of workman are concerned, in this regard, it is pointed out that workman was entitled only for 
compensation under Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2 (contractor) and no domestic inquiry was required to 
be conducted. Merely because work is of perennial in nature does not mean that any right has accrued in favour of the 
workman to get employment with respondent no.l, which is a department of Civil Defence run by Government of 
India and for that regular recruitment procedure is provided. Workman cannot seek backdoor entry into service. 
Moreover, workman is not employee of respondent no.| being employee of respondent no.2 (contractor). 


23. In view of above discussion, it is held that since respondent no.2 (contractor) is currently not working with 
respondent no.|, so keeping in view the tenure of service rendered by the workman and salary given to him during the 
period of his stay with the contractor and non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act, this Tribunal is of the considered 
opinion that workman is entitled for compensation. 


24. Keeping in view the fact and circumstances of the present case, the following scheme of compensation is 

deemed fit by this Tribunal: 

i. Workman who worked for less than 2 years with contractor shall be entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 

il. Workman who worked for more than 2 years but less than 5 years with contractor shall be entitled 
Rs.30,000/-. 

ili. Workman who worked for more than 5 years with contractors shall be entitled for Rs.50,000/-. 

25. Present workman employed on 0!.04.20l4 and was terminated on 27.0.20I8 (more than 2 years and less 

than 5 years), so he is entitled for Rs.30,000/-. 

26. Respondent no.2 is directed to pay the workman Rs.30,000/- within one month from the date of the 


notification of the award failing which the respondent no.2 shall further pay interest @6% per annum from the date of 
award till the date of making payment. It is also added here that in case respondent no.! requires safai karamcharis, 
then workman may be engaged through any contractor on sympathetic ground. 


27. Let copy of this award be sent to Central Government for publication as required under Section |7 of the 
Act. 


KAMAL KANT, PO-cum-Link Officer

l, Workman has filed the present claim petition under Section 2A of the Industrial Disputes Act, !947 


(hereinafter called the Act) against his termination w.e.f. 27.0.208 claiming reinstatement into service along with 
continuity of service and full back wages. 


2D: It is maintained by the workman in the claim petition that that he was appointed as sweeper/safai karamchari 
on 0.04.207 by respondent no.| at minimum wages prescribed by the Central Government. But the said minimum 
wages were never paid to him. Management has unlawfully deducted 2% EPF share, |.75% ESI and Rs.300/- as 
TDS from his salary despite of the fact that his total wages comes under the ambit of tax free limit. He was working 
under respondent no.! directly, but respondent no.! in order to avoid its liability always shows his employment in 
record under different contractors, which tantamount to unfair labour practice. 


3. It is further maintained that workman including 200 other safai karamcharis of respondent no.| raised the 
demand for non-deduction of TDS from the wages paid by respondent no.!. However, their demand was not 
accepted. Other workers working under respondent no.l formed union and subsequently his services were dispensed 
with w.e.f. 27.0.208. It is also maintained that respondent no.| retained juniors in service and also made fresh 
appointments after termination of his services and no compensation was paid under Section 25F of the Act. Thus, 
there is violation of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act. Hence this petition. 


4. Notice of the petition was given to respondents. 


[भाग aves 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 4263 


5. Respondent no.! filed reply to the claim petition maintaining therein that the workman has not come to the 
Court with clean hands as he was appointed as sweeper by respondent no.2 (contractor) at minimum wage rate as 
prescribed by the Central Government. As per Government Policy dated 8.03.20l (Ex.R-l), Non-Cantonment 
Military Stations have been authorized to outsource conservancy safaiwala. The answering respondent entered into 
contract with various private agencies for outsourcing of sweepers (safai karamcharis). In July 208, the answering 
respondent entered into contract with M/s AA Foundation for safety and M/s Sunrise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd. through e- 
tendering for outsourcing of safai karamcharis to provide conservancy services in Chandi Mandir Military Station for 
the financial year 20I8-l9. Respondent no.2 submits bill for payment for the days the safai karamcharis performed 
their duties and answering respondent is only responsible to forward these bills to PCDA Western Command 
Chandigarh to release payment directly to the contractor’s account. Salary is paid to the workman by the contractor. 
Compliance of PF, ESI Rules etc. is carried out by the contractor as per contract agreement. Answering respondent 
has no responsibility of disbursement of wages and deduction of EPF and ESI etc. The workman and other safai 
karamcharis formed union in the headquarter, which is not permitted and also carried out demonstration inside the 
premises of Station Headquarter on 27.0.208 and failed to perform his duties. Thereafter, answering respondent 
asked respondent no.2 to ask the workers to perform their duties. Answering respondent has no role or responsibility 
for termination services of the workman. There is no relationship of employer and employee between the answering 
respondent and the workman. In view of Army Headquarter Letter, the civilian employees of the answering 
respondent cannot organize themselves into trade unions as station headquarter is an administrative office of the 
Government of India. In spite of that, workman along with other safai karamcharis formed union unauthorizedly and 
indulge in unlawful activities. It is maintained that workman is the employee of contractor and therefore, termination 
is the prerogative of the contractor. It is prayed that the claim be dismissed. 


6. Respondent no.2 also filed reply maintaining therein that workman worked with it w.e.f. 05.07.20I8 to 
27.0.208. Respondent no.2 has not terminated the services of workman. Workman was paid minimum wages as 
declared by the Centre Government from time to time. Workman himself left the job at his own will without notice. It 
is prayed that the claim may be dismissed. 


Evidence of workman:- 


7. In order to prove his case, workman examined himself as WWI and tendered into evidence his affidavit 
WWI/A and has been cross examined by the AR for respondent no.!. AR for workman closed the evidence on 
30.07.2024. 


Evidence of respondent no.|:- 


8. Respondent no.l! examined Colonel K P Singh S/o Late Colonel P. P. Singh, OIC, Civic Cell, Station Head 
Quarter, Chandi Mandir Panchkula as ०७], who tendered into evidence his affidavit ExMWI/A along with 
documents ४5.२] to Ex.R4. AR for respondent no. closed the evidence on 0.03.2026. 


Sr. No. Document Dated Exhibit 
l. Policy 8.03.20I] Ex.R-! 
2 Agreement with respondent no.2 05.07.208 Ex.R-2 
3. Copy of ECR of EPFO Ex.R-3 
4 Letter of Head Quarter 22.04.980 Ex.R-4 
9. It is pertinent to mention here that respondent no.2 was proceeded ex parte on 20.04.2023. 


Submissions of workman:- 


0. While arguing the case, AR for workman contended that in this case, workman was appointed by respondent 
no.| under a camouflage agreement with respondent no.2. The workman was appointed directly by respondent no.| 
on 0.04.207 as sweeper and his services were dispensed with on 27.0.20I8 without any reason in violation of 
Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act as no compensation was paid to the workman and junior to him were retained in 
service and fresh appointment were made after termination of the workman. So far as the agreements between the 
respondent no.! & 2 are concerned, they are totally not tenable and is unfair labour practice. The work performed by 
the workman is of perennial in nature. The contractor was not having any license. The contractor has left after three 
months. No domestic inquiry was held before terminating the service of the workman, which is mandatory under the 
Act. 


Submissions of respondent no.|:- 


Il. On the other hand, AR for respondent no.l contended that the workman was engaged by respondent no. 2 
(contractor) as per agreement executed between respondent no.| & 2 on outsource basis and there is no relationship 
of employer and employee between respondent no.! and the workman. Wages were paid by the respondent no. 2 to 


4264 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 6, 2026/VAISAKHA 26, 948 [Part II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


the workman and there was no role of respondent no.| in appointing or terminating the services of the workman. 
Workman is employee of respondent no.2. If there is any violation of provision of the Act, then it is done by 
respondent no.2. As per Ex.R3, all dues were paid by the contractor with regard to EPF and ESI etc. The workman 
and other safai karamcharis formed union in the Headquarter, which is not permitted as per Ex.R4. Respondent no.| 
being the Government Agency, workman is trying to take benefit of his appointment by respondent no.2 and has filed 
a false claim after concealing material facts and claim of the workman is not maintainable. 


2. Ihave given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 


Findings:- 

3. The first contention regarding the applicant is that whether applicant comes within the definition of 
workman as is defined in Section 2(s) of the Act. I may mention that applicant was appointed as sweeper as per his 
claim petition and affidavit submitted before the Tribunal. In plain words the applicant was performing his duties as 
labourer/unskilled worker. He was not in supervisory or administrative post requiring him to perform only 
administrative duties. While interpreting Section 2(s) Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Devinder Singh Vs. 
Municipal Council, Sanaur, AIR 20I] Supreme Court 2532,has observed as follows:- 


“The source of employment, the quantum of recruitment, the terms & conditions of employment/ contract of 
service, the quantum of wages/ pay and mode of payment are not at all relevant for deciding whether or not a 
person is a workman within the meaning of Section 2(s) of the Act. The definition of workman also does not 
make any distinction between full time and part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. 
There is nothing in the plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be inferred that only person 
employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing whole time job is a workman and the one 
employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty 
for fixed hours is not a workman.” 


4. Thus Hon’ble Supreme Court has clarified that the definition of workman also does not make any distinction 
between full time or part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. There is nothing in plain language of 
Section 2(s) from which it can be infer that only person employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing 
whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a 
casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a workman. In view of the ratio of law enunciated in the 
above ruling, in my considered opinion the applicant herein admittedly falls within the definition of ‘workman’ under 
Section 2(s) of the Act. 


5. The Hon’ble Supreme Court after analyzing the catena of cases has laid down in Balwant Raj Saluja Vs. Air 
India Limited in Civil Appeal ५०. 70266 dated 25.08.204, two well recognized tests to find out whether the labours 
are the contract employees of the principal employer are:- 


3) Whether the principal employer pays the salary instead of contractor and 
2) Whether the principal employer controls and supervise the work of the employees? 


6. The facts regarding the payment of salary by the management or contractor has not been stated in the claim 
petition of the workman. In fact, claim petition is totally silent regarding the payment of wages, salary, letter of 
appointment by respondent no.| or by contractor or anything likewise. Thus, this basic feature for holding the 
relationship of employer and employee i.e. between respondent no.! and workman totally lacking not in the pleading 
but also in the evidence submitted by the workman.However, MWI Colonel K P Singh in his cross examination has 
stated that it is responsibility of the contractor to pay monthly wages to the conservancy staff provided by him. 
Respondent no.! has nothing to do with the minimum wages, DC rate to the conservancy staff. Contractor is 
responsible to give notice to the conservancy staff provided by him. Respondent no.! has no roll in terminating the 
services of the workman. Thus, it was the respondent no.2 who was required to ensure the payment of wages to the 
workers engaged by him from time to time. 


7. It is also added here that respondent no.! has also placed on record documents Ex.R-| to Ex.R-4. Ex.R-2 is 
the contract agreement between respondent no.| & 2. Thus, from the adduced evidence, it emerges that workman was 
paid salary by the contractor and he was being controlled and supervised by the contractor. Thus, there does not exist 
any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.l. However, there exists relation 
of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


8. It is also added here that as per definition of contractor as given in the Section 2(c) of the Contract Labour 
(Regulation and Abolition) Act, 970 (hereinafter called as Contract Act), respondent no.2 was supplying contract 
labour to the respondent no.l. Thus, respondent no.2 would fall under the definition of contractor. Section 2(c) of the 
Contract Act is reproduced as under: 


“Contractor”, in relation to an establishment, means a person who undertakes to produce a given result for the 
establishment, other than a mere supply of goods or articles of manufacture to such establishment, through contract 
labour or who supplies contract labour for any work of the establishment and includes a sub-contractor; 


9. TheHon’ble Apex Court in the case of Banglore Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A. Rajappa /928(36) 
FLR 266 dealt at length with the ambit and scope of expression “industry” as defined in Section 2(j) of the Act, and 


[भाग aves 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 4265 


has laid down triple test for determining whether a particular establishment is industry or not. The triple test is where 
(a) systematic activity (b) organized by cooperation between employer and employee (the direct and substantial 
element is commercial (c) for the production or distribution of goods and services to calculated to satisfy human 
wants and wishes prima facie is an “Industry” in that enterprise. The workman was doing work i.e. systematic activity 
for respondent no.| at the asking of respondent no.2 and there was cooperation between the workman and respondent 
no.2, which involve substantial commercial element for rendering service to satisfy human wants. Thus, respondent 
no.2 would be termed as industry. 


20. Workman in his deposition has clearly stated that there was non-compliance of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of 
the Act. He also stated that he was appointed on 0!.04.207 and he was paid minimum wages prescribed by Central 
Government and was working with respondent no.l. He was not given any compensation before terminating his 
services on 27.!0.208. In his cross examination, he has further stated that his salary used to be credited in his bank 
account. But he has not placed on record any bank account, in which his salary was credited in order to enable this 
Court to see who was making payment to the workman. However, there is no denial that the workman was paid salary 
by respondent no.2 (contractor). However, from the agreement dated 05.07.20I8 (Ex.R-2) placed on file by 
respondent no.l, it emerges that safai karamcharis were provided by respondent no.2 (contractor) to work with 
respondent no.l. Thus, workman was employee of respondent no.2 and not of respondent no.! as has been discussed 
above. There does not exist any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.|. 
However, there exists relation of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


2I. Admittedly, there is non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2. However, as regards 
violation of other provisions i.e. Section 25G and 25H of the Act, there is no evidence lead by the workman. Hence, 
workman is entitled for compensation from respondent no.2. 


22. It is also added here that there is no iota of evidence that any unfair labour practice used by respondent no.| 
by entering into contract with contractors for supply of safai karamcharis and agreement executed between 
respondent no.! and 2 cannot be said to be any unfair labour practice. So far as contentions of AR for workman that 
the work performed by the workman is of perennial in nature and no domestic inquiry was held before terminating 
the services of workman are concerned, in this regard, it is pointed out that workman was entitled only for 
compensation under Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2 (contractor) and no domestic inquiry was required to 
be conducted. Merely because work is of perennial in nature does not mean that any right has accrued in favour of the 
workman to get employment with respondent no.l, which is a department of Civil Defence run by Government of 
India and for that regular recruitment procedure is provided. Workman cannot seek backdoor entry into service. 
Moreover, workman is not employee of respondent no.| being employee of respondent no.2 (contractor). 


23. In view of above discussion, it is held that since respondent no.2 (contractor) is currently not working with 
respondent no.|, so keeping in view the tenure of service rendered by the workman and salary given to him during the 
period of his stay with the contractor and non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act, this Tribunal is of the considered 
opinion that workman is entitled for compensation. 


24. Keeping in view the fact and circumstances of the present case, the following scheme of compensation is 
deemed fit by this Tribunal: 


i. Workman who worked for less than 2 years with contractor shall be entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 

ii. Workman who worked for more than 2 years but less than 5 years with contractor shall be entitled 
Rs.30,000/-. 

ili. Workman who worked for more than 5 years with contractors shall be entitled for Rs.50,000/-. 


25. Present workman employed on 0!.04.20!7 and was terminated on 27.0.20I8 (less than 2 years), so he is 
entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 


26. Respondent no.2 is directed to pay the workman Rs.20,000/- within one month from the date of the 
notification of the award failing which the respondent no.2 shall further pay interest @6% per annum from the date of 
award till the date of making payment. It is also added here that in case respondent no.! requires safai karamcharis, 
then workman may be engaged through any contractor on sympathetic ground. 


27. Let copy of this award be sent to Central Government for publication as required under Section |7 of the 
Act. 


KAMAL KANT, PO-cum-Link Officer 
नई दिल्‍ली, 7 मई, 2026

I; Workman has filed the present claim petition under Section 2A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 


(hereinafter called the Act) against his termination w.e.f. 27.0.20l8 claiming reinstatement into service along with 
continuity of service and full back wages.

on 9.[].206 by respondent no.! at minimum wages prescribed by the Central Government. But the said minimum 
wages were never paid to him. Management has unlawfully deducted 2% EPF share, .75% ESI and Rs.300/- as

respondent no.2. As per Ex.R3, all dues were paid by the contractor with regard to EPF and ESI etc. The workman 
and other safai karamcharis formed union in the Headquarter, which is not permitted as per Ex.R4. Respondent no. |

2. I have given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 

Findings:- 

3. The first contention regarding the applicant is that whether applicant comes within the definition of 
workman as is defined in Section 2(s) of the Act. I may mention that applicant was appointed as sweeper as per his 
claim petition and affidavit submitted before the Tribunal. In plain words the applicant was performing his duties as 
labourer/unskilled worker. He was not in supervisory or administrative post requiring him to perform only 
administrative duties. While interpreting Section 2(s) Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Devinder Singh Vs. 
Municipal Council, Sanaur, AIR 20I] Supreme Court 2532,has observed as follows:- 


“The source of employment, the quantum of recruitment, the terms & conditions of employment contract of 
service, the quantum of wages/ pay and mode of payment are not at all relevant for deciding whether or not a 
person is a workman within the meaning of Section 2(s) of the Act. The definition of workman also does not 
make any distinction between full time and part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. 
There is nothing in the plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be inferred that only person 
employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing whole time job is a workman and the one 
employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty 
for fixed hours is not a workman.” 


4. Thus Hon’ble Supreme Court has clarified that the definition of workman also does not make any distinction 
between full time or part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. There is nothing in plain language of 
Section 2(s) from which it can be infer that only person employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing 
whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a 
casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a workman. In view of the ratio of law enunciated in the 
above ruling, in my considered opinion the applicant herein admittedly falls within the definition of ‘workman’ under 
Section 2(s) of the Act. 


5. The Hon’ble Supreme Court after analyzing the catena of cases has laid down in Balwant Raj Saluja Vs. Air 
India Limited in Civil Appeal ५०. 70266 dated 25.08, 20/4, two well recognized tests to find out whether the labours 
are the contract employees of the principal employer are:- 


3) Whether the principal employer pays the salary instead of contractor and 
2) Whether the principal employer controls and supervise the work of the employees? 


6. The facts regarding the payment of salary by the management or contractor has not been stated in the claim 
petition of the workman. In fact, claim petition is totally silent regarding the payment of wages, salary, letter of 
appointment by respondent no.! or by contractor or anything likewise. Thus, this basic feature for holding the 
relationship of employer and employee i.e. between respondent no.! and workman totally lacking not in the pleading 
but also in the evidence submitted by the workman.However, ५४] Colonel K P Singh in his cross examination has 
stated that it is responsibility of the contractor to pay monthly wages to the conservancy staff provided by him. 
Respondent no.l has nothing to do with the minimum wages, DC rate to the conservancy staff. Contractor is 
responsible to give notice to the conservancy staff provided by him. Respondent no.| has no roll in terminating the 
services of the workman. Thus, it was the respondent no.2 who was required to ensure the payment of wages to the 
workers engaged by him from time to time. 


7. It is also added here that respondent no. has also placed on record documents Ex.R-| to Ex.R-4. Ex.R-2 is 
the contract agreement between respondent no.! & 2. Thus, from the adduced evidence, it emerges that workman was 
paid salary by the contractor and he was being controlled and supervised by the contractor. Thus, there does not exist 
any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.|. However, there exists relation 
of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


8. It is also added here that as per definition of contractor as given in the Section 2(c) of the Contract Labour 
(Regulation and Abolition) Act, 970 (hereinafter called as Contract Act), respondent no.2 was supplying contract 
labour to the respondent no.!. Thus, respondent no.2 would fall under the definition of contractor. Section 2(c) of the 
Contract Act is reproduced as under: 


“Contractor”, in relation to an establishment, means a person who undertakes to produce a given result for the 
establishment, other than a mere supply of goods or articles of manufacture to such establishment, through contract 
labour or who supplies contract labour for any work of the establishment and includes a sub-contractor; 


9. TheHon’ble Apex Court in the case of Banglore Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A. Rajappa /928(36) 
FLR 266 dealt at length with the ambit and scope of expression “industry” as defined in Section 2(j) of the Act, and 


has laid down triple test for determining whether a particular establishment is industry or not. The triple test is where 


[भाग aves 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 4269 


(a) systematic activity (b) organized by cooperation between employer and employee (the direct and substantial 
element is commercial (c) for the production or distribution of goods and services to calculated to satisfy human 
wants and wishes prima facie is an “Industry” in that enterprise. The workman was doing work i.e. systematic activity 
for respondent no.| at the asking of respondent no.2 and there was cooperation between the workman and respondent 
no.2, which involve substantial commercial element for rendering service to satisfy human wants. Thus, respondent 
no.2 would be termed as industry. 

20. Workman in his deposition has clearly stated that there was non-compliance of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of 
the Act. He also stated that he was appointed on !9.!.206 and he was paid minimum wages prescribed by Central 
Government and was working with respondent no.l. He was not given any compensation before terminating his 
services on 27.!0.208. In his cross examination, he has further stated that his salary used to be credited in his bank 
account. But he has not placed on record any bank account, in which his salary was credited in order to enable this 
Court to see who was making payment to the workman. However, there is no denial that the workman was paid salary 
by respondent no.2 (contractor). However, from the agreement dated 05.07.20I8 (Ex.R-2) placed on file by 
respondent no.l, it emerges that safai karamcharis were provided by respondent no.2 (contractor) to work with 
respondent no.l. Thus, workman was employee of respondent no.2 and not of respondent no.! as has been discussed 
above. There does not exist any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.|. 
However, there exists relation of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 

2: Admittedly, there is non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2. However, as regards 
violation of other provisions i.e. Section 25G and 25H of the Act, there is no evidence lead by the workman. Hence, 
workman is entitled for compensation from respondent no.2. 

22. It is also added here that there is no iota of evidence that any unfair labour practice used by respondent no.! 
by entering into contract with contractors for supply of safai karamcharis and agreement executed between 
respondent no.! and 2 cannot be said to be any unfair labour practice. So far as contentions of AR for workman that 
the work performed by the workman is of perennial in nature and no domestic inquiry was held before terminating 
the services of workman are concerned, in this regard, it is pointed out that workman was entitled only for 
compensation under Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2 (contractor) and no domestic inquiry was required to 
be conducted. Merely because work is of perennial in nature does not mean that any right has accrued in favour of the 
workman to get employment with respondent no.l, which is a department of Civil Defence run by Government of 
India and for that regular recruitment procedure is provided. Workman cannot seek backdoor entry into service. 
Moreover, workman is not employee of respondent no.! being employee of respondent no.2 (contractor). 

23. In view of above discussion, it is held that since respondent no.2 (contractor) is currently not working with 
respondent no.|, so keeping in view the tenure of service rendered by the workman and salary given to him during the 
period of his stay with the contractor and non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act, this Tribunal is of the considered 
opinion that workman is entitled for compensation. 


24. Keeping in view the fact and circumstances of the present case, the following scheme of compensation is 

deemed fit by this Tribunal: 

. Workman who worked for less than 2 years with contractor shall be entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 

il. Workman who worked for more than 2 years but less than 5 years with contractor shall be entitled 
Rs.30,000/-. 

ili. Workman who worked for more than 5 years with contractors shall be entitled for Rs.50,000/-. 

25. Present workman employed on 9.!.20l6 and was terminated on 27.0.208 (less than 2 years), so he is 

entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 

26. Respondent no.2 is directed to pay the workman Rs.20,000/- within one month from the date of the 


notification of the award failing which the respondent no.2 shall further pay interest @6% per annum from the date of 
award till the date of making payment. It is also added here that in case respondent no.! requires safai karamcharis, 
then workman may be engaged through any contractor on sympathetic ground. 


27. Let copy of this award be sent to Central Government for publication as required under Section !7 of the 
Act. 


KAMAL KANT, PO-cum-Link Officer
