l. Admn. Commander Station Head Quarter (Civil Defence), Chandi +Mandir Cantt. Panchkula, Haryana. 
2. M/s Sun Rise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd., 2253/] one Floor, PipliWala Town, Mani Mazra, Chandigarh. 
----Respondents 
Present:- Sh. Jang Bahadur, AR for workman. 


Sh. Satinder Singh, AR for respondent no.|. 
Respondent no.2 already ex parte. 
Award :0.03.2026 


l.Workman has filed the present claim petition under Section 2A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 
(hereinafter called the Act) against his termination w.e.f. 27.0.208 claiming reinstatement into service 
along with continuity of service and full back wages. 


2.It is maintained by the workman in the claim petition that that he was appointed as sweeper/safai karamchari 
on 5.07.20l6 by respondent no.l at minimum wages prescribed by the Central Government. But the said 
minimum wages were never paid to him. Management has unlawfully deducted 2% EPF share, .75% ESI 
and Rs.300/- as TDS from hissalary despite of the fact that his total wages comes under the ambit of taxfree 
limit. He was working under respondent no.| directly, but respondent no.| in order to avoid its liability always 
shows his employment in record under different contractors, which tantamount to unfair labour practice. 


3.It is further maintained that workman including 200 other safai karamcharis of respondent no.! raised the 
demand for non-deduction of TDS from the wages paid by respondent no.l!. However, their demand was not 
accepted. Other workers working under respondent no.| formed union and subsequently his services were 
dispensed with w.e.f. 27.0.208. It is also maintained that respondent no. retained juniors in service and also 
made fresh appointments after termination of his services and no compensation was paid under Section 25F of 
the Act. Thus, there is violation of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act. Hence this petition. 


4.Notice of the petition was given to respondents. 


5.Respondent no.! filed reply to the claim petition maintaining therein that the workman has not come to the 
Court with clean hands as he was appointed as sweeper by respondent no.2 (contractor) at minimum wage rate 
as prescribed by the Central Government. As per Government Policy dated 8.03.20l] (Ex.R-l), Non- 
Cantonment Military Stations have been authorized to outsource conservancy safaiwala. The answering 
respondent entered into contract with various private agencies for outsourcing of sweepers (safai karamcharis). 
In July 208, the answering respondent entered into contract with M/s AA Foundation for safety and M/s 
Sunrise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd. through e-tendering for outsourcing of safai karamcharis to provide conservancy 
services in ChandiMandir Military Station for the financial year 20I8-l9. Respondent no.2 submits bill for 
payment for the days the safai karamcharis performed their duties and answering respondent is only 
responsible to forward these bills to PCDA Western Command Chandigarh to release payment directly to the 
contractor’s account. Salary is paid to the workman by the contractor. Compliance of PF, ESI Rules etc. is 
carried out by the contractor as per contract agreement. Answering respondent has no responsibility of 
disbursement of wages and deduction of EPF and ESI etc. The workman and other safai karamcharis formed 
union in the headquarter, which is not permitted and also carried out demonstration inside the premises of 
Station Headquarter on 27.0.20l8 and failed to perform his duties. Thereafter, answering respondent asked 
respondent no.2 to ask the workers to perform their duties. Answering respondent has no role or responsibility 
for termination services of the workman. There is no relationship of employer and employee between the 


[भाग II—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 427] 


answering respondent and the workman. In view of Army Headquarter Letter, the civilian employees of the 
answering respondent cannot organize themselves into trade unions as station headquarter is an administrative 
office of the Government of India. In spite of that, workman along with other safai karamcharis formed union 
unauthorizedly and indulge in unlawful activities. It is maintained that workman is the employee of contractor 
and therefore, termination is the prerogative of the contractor. It is prayed that the claim be dismissed. 


6.Respondent no.2 also filed reply maintaining therein that workman worked with it w.e.f. 05.07.208 to 
27.0.208. Respondent no.2 has not terminated the services of workman. Workman was paid minimum 
wages as declared by the Centre Government from time to time. Workman himself left the job at his own will 
without notice. It is prayed that the claim may be dismissed. 


Evidence of workman:- 


7.In order to prove his case, workman examined himself as ११] and tendered into evidence his affidavit 
WWI/A and has been cross examined by the AR for respondent no.!. AR for workman closed the evidence on 
30.07.2024. 


Evidence of respondent no.I:- 
8.Respondent no.| examined Colonel K P Singh S/o Late Colonel P. P. Singh, OIC, Civic Cell, Station Head 
Quarter, ChandiMandir Panchkula as ॥५७।, who tendered into evidence his affidavit Ex.MWI/A along with 
documents Ex.RI to Ex.R4. AR for respondent no.! closed the evidence on 0.03.2026. 


Sr. No. Document Dated Exhibit 
l. Policy 8.03.20I] Ex.R-| 
2: Agreement with respondent no.2 05.07.208 Ex.R-2 
ce Copy of ECR of EPFO Ex.R-3 
4. Letter of Head Quarter 22.04.980 Ex.R-4 


9.It is pertinent to mention here that respondent no.2 was proceeded ex parte on 20.04.2023. 
Submissions of workman:- 


0. While arguing the case, AR for workman contended that in this case, workman was appointed by 
respondent no.! under a camouflage agreement with respondent no.2. The workman was appointed directly by 
respondent no. on l5.07.20l6 as sweeper and his services were dispensed with on 27.0.20l8 without any 
reason in violation of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act as no compensation was paid to the workman and 
junior to him were retained in service and fresh appointment were made after termination of the workman. So 
far as the agreements between the respondent no.| & 2 are concerned, they are totally not tenable and is unfair 
labour practice. The work performed by the workman is of perennial in nature. The contractor was not having 
any license. The contractor has left after three months. No domestic inquiry was held before terminating the 
service of the workman, which is mandatory under the Act. 


Submissions of respondent no.|:- 


Il. On the other hand, AR for respondent no.| contended that the workman was engaged by respondent 
no.2 (contractor) as per agreement executed between respondent no.! & 2 on outsource basis and there is no 
relationship of employer and employee between respondent no.| and the workman. Wages were paid by the 
respondent no.2 to the workman and there was no role of respondent no.| in appointing or terminating the 
services of the workman. Workman is employee of respondent no.2. If there is any violation of provision of 
the Act, then it is done by respondent no.2. As per Ex.R3, all dues were paid by the contractor with regard to 
EPF and ESI etc. The workman and other safai karamcharis formed union in the Headquarter, which is not 
permitted as per Ex.R4. Respondent no.! being the Government Agency, workman is trying to take benefit of 
his appointment by respondent no.2 and has filed a false claim after concealing material facts and claim of the 
workman is not maintainable. 


2. Ihave given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 


Findings:- 
3. The first contention regarding the applicant is that whether applicant comes within the definition of 
workman as is defined in Section 2(s) of the Act. I may mention that applicant was appointed as sweeper as 
per his claim petition and affidavit submitted before the Tribunal. In plain words the applicant was performing 
his duties as labourer/unskilled worker. He was not in supervisory or administrative post requiring him to 
perform only administrative duties. While interpreting Section 2(s) Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of 


Devinder Singh Vs. Municipal Council, Sanaur, AIR 20I] Supreme Court 2532,has observed as follows:- 


4272 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 6, 2026/VAISAKHA 26, 948 [Part II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


“The source of employment, the quantum of recruitment, the terms & conditions of 
employment/ contract of service, the quantum of wages/ pay and mode of payment are 
not at all relevant for deciding whether or not a person is a workman within the 
meaning of Section 2(s) of the Act. The definition of workman also does not make any 
distinction between full time and part time employee or a person appointed on contract 
basis. There is nothing in the plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be 
inferred that only person employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing 
whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or contract 
basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a 
workman.” 


4. Thus Hon’ble Supreme Court has clarified that the definition of workman also does not make any 
distinction between full time or part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. There is nothing in 
plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be infer that only person employed on regular basis or a 
person employed for doing whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or 
contract basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a workman. In 
view of the ratio of law enunciated in the above ruling, in my considered opinion the applicant herein 
admittedly falls within the definition of ‘workman’ under Section 2(s) of the Act. 


5. The Hon’ble Supreme Court after analyzing the catena of cases has laid down in Balwant Raj 


Saluja Vs. Air India Limited in Civil Appeal ५०. 70266 dated 25.08. 20/4, two well recognized tests to find 
out whether the labours are the contract employees of the principal employer are:- 


3) Whether the principal employer pays the salary instead of contractor and 
2) Whether the principal employer controls and supervise the work of the employees? 


6. The facts regarding the payment of salary by the management or contractor has not been stated in 
the claim petition of the workman. In fact, claim petition is totally silent regarding the payment of wages, 
salary, letter of appointment by respondent no.! or by contractor or anything likewise. Thus, this basic feature 
for holding the relationship of employer and employee i.e. between respondent no.! and workman totally 
lacking not in the pleading but also in the evidence submitted by the workman.However, MWI Colonel K P 
Singh in his cross examination has stated that it is responsibility of the contractor to pay monthly wages to the 
conservancy staff provided by him. Respondent no.! has nothing to do with the minimum wages, DC rate to 
the conservancy staff. Contractor is responsible to give notice to the conservancy staff provided by him. 
Respondent no.! has no roll in terminating the services of the workman. Thus, it was the respondent no.2 who 
was required to ensure the payment of wages to the workers engaged by him from time to time. 


7. It is also added here that respondent no.! has also placed on record documents Ex.R-| to Ex.R-4. 
Ex.R-2 is the contract agreement between respondent no.l & 2. Thus, from the adduced evidence, it emerges 
that workman was paid salary by the contractor and he was being controlled and supervised by the contractor. 
Thus, there does not exist any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent 
no.l. However, there exists relation of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


8. It is also added here that as per definition of contractor as given in the Section 2(c) of the Contract 
Labour (Regulation and Abolition) Act, 970 (hereinafter called as Contract Act), respondent no.2 was 
supplying contract labour to the respondent no.l. Thus, respondent no.2 would fall under the definition of 
contractor. Section 2(c) of the Contract Act is reproduced as under: 


“Contractor”, in relation to an establishment, means a person who undertakes to produce 
a given result for the establishment, other than a mere supply of goods or articles of 
manufacture to such establishment, through contract labour or who supplies contract 
labour for any work of the establishment and includes a sub-contractor; 


9. TheHon’ble Apex Court in the case of Banglore Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A. Rajappa 
2928(36) FLR 266 dealt at length with the ambit and scope of expression “industry” as defined in Section 2(j) 


of the Act, and has laid down triple test for determining whether a particular establishment is industry or not. 
The triple test is where (a) systematic activity (b) organized by cooperation between employer and employee 
(the direct and substantial element is commercial (c) for the production or distribution of goods and services to 
calculated to satisfy human wants and wishes prima facie is an “Industry” in that enterprise. The workman was 
doing work i.e. systematic activity for respondent no.l at the asking of respondent no.2 and there was 
cooperation between the workman and respondent no.2, which involve substantial commercial element for 
rendering service to satisfy human wants. Thus, respondent no.2 would be termed as industry. 


20. Workman in his deposition has clearly stated that there was non-compliance of Section 25F, 25G 
and 25H of the Act. He also stated that he was appointed on !5.07.20l6 and he was paid minimum wages 
prescribed by Central Government and was working with respondent no.l. He was not given any 
compensation before terminating his services on 27.0.20I8. In his cross examination, he has further stated 


lil. 


[भाग IL_—avzs 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 4273 


that his salary used to be credited in his bank account. But he has not placed on record any bank account, in 
which his salary was credited in order to enable this Court to see who was making payment to the workman. 
However, there is no denial that the workman was paid salary by respondent no.2 (contractor). However, from 
the agreement dated 05.07.20I8 (Ex.R-2) placed on file by respondent no., it emerges that safai karamcharis 
were provided by respondent no.2 (contractor) to work with respondent no.l. Thus, workman was employee of 
respondent no.2 and not of respondent no.! as has been discussed above. There does not exist any relationship 
of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.l!. However, there exists relation of 
employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


2I. Admittedly, there is non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2. However, as 
regards violation of other provisions i.e. Section 25G and 25H of the Act, there is no evidence lead by the 
workman. Hence, workman is entitled for compensation from respondent no.2. 


22. It is also added here that there is no iota of evidence that any unfair labour practice used by 
respondent no.! by entering into contract with contractors for supply of safai karamcharis and agreement 
executed between respondent no.| and 2 cannot be said to be any unfair labour practice. So far as contentions 
of AR for workman that the work performed by the workman is of perennial in nature and no domestic inquiry 
was held before terminating the services of workman are concerned, in this regard, it is pointed out that 
workman was entitled only for compensation under Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2 (contractor) and 
no domestic inquiry was required to be conducted. Merely because work is of perennial in nature does not 
mean that any right has accrued in favour of the workman to get employment with respondent no.!, which is a 
department of Civil Defence run by Government of India and for that regular recruitment procedure is 
provided. Workman cannot seek backdoor entry into service. Moreover, workman is not employee of 
respondent no. being employee of respondent no.2 (contractor). 


23. In view of above discussion, it is held that since respondent no.2 (contractor) is currently not 
working with respondent no.l, so keeping in view the tenure of service rendered by the workman and salary 
given to him during the period of his stay with the contractor and non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act, 
this Tribunal is of the considered opinion that workman is entitled for compensation. 


24. Keeping in view the fact and circumstances of the present case, the following scheme of 
compensation is deemed fit by this Tribunal: 


Workman who worked for less than 2 years with contractor shall be entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 


Workman who worked for more than 2 years but less than 5 years with contractor shall be entitled 
Rs.30,000/-. 


Workman who worked for more than 5 years with contractors shall be entitled for Rs.50,000/-. 


25. Present workman employed on !5.07.20I6 and was terminated on 27.0.20I8 (more than 2 years 
and less than 5 years), so he is entitled for Rs.30,000/-. 


26. Respondent no.2 is directed to pay the workman Rs.30,000/- within one month from the date of the 
notification of the award failing which the respondent no.2 shall further pay interest @6% per annum from the 
date of award till the date of making payment. It is also added here that in case respondent no.| requires safai 
karamcharis, then workman may be engaged through any contractor on sympathetic ground. 


27. Let copy of this award be sent to Central Government for publication as required under Section 47 
of the Act. 


KAMAL KANT, PO-cum-Link Officer 
नई दिल्‍ली, 7 मई, 2026

l. Admn. Commander Station Head Quarter (Civil Defence), Chandi Mandir Cantt. Panchkula, Haryana.

Present:- Sh. Jang Bahadur, AR for workman. 
Sh. Satinder Singh, AR for respondent no.|. 
Respondent no.2 already ex parte. 
Award :0.03.2026 
I; Workman has filed the present claim petition under Section 2A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 


(hereinafter called the Act) against his termination w.e.f. 27.0.20l8 claiming reinstatement into service along with 
continuity of service and full back wages. 


2 It is maintained by the workman in the claim petition that that he was appointed as sweeper/safai karamchari 
on .08.207 by respondent no.| at minimum wages prescribed by the Central Government. But the said minimum 
wages were never paid to him. Management has unlawfully deducted [2% EPF share, .75% ESI and Rs.300/- as 
TDS from his salary despite of the fact that his total wages comes under the ambit of tax free limit. He was working 
under respondent no.| directly, but respondent no.! in order to avoid its liability always shows his employment in 
record under different contractors, which tantamount to unfair labour practice. 


3: It is further maintained that workman including 200 other safai karamcharis of respondent no.! raised the 
demand for non-deduction of TDS from the wages paid by respondent no.!. However, their demand was not accepted. 
Other workers working under respondent no. formed union and subsequently his services were dispensed with w.e.f. 
27.0.208. It is also maintained that respondent no.! retained juniors in service and also made fresh appointments 
after termination of his services and no compensation was paid under Section 25F of the Act. Thus, there is violation 
of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act. Hence this petition. 


4. Notice of the petition was given to respondents. 


3: Respondent no.! filed reply to the claim petition maintaining therein that the workman has not come to the 
Court with clean hands as he was appointed as sweeper by respondent no.2 (contractor) at minimum wage rate as 
prescribed by the Central Government. As per Government Policy dated 8.03.20I (Ex.R-l), Non-Cantonment 
Military Stations have been authorized to outsource conservancy safaiwala. The answering respondent entered into 
contract with various private agencies for outsourcing of sweepers (safai karamcharis). In July 208, the answering 
respondent entered into contract with M/s AA Foundation for safety and M/s Sunrise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd. through e- 
tendering for outsourcing of safai karamcharis to provide conservancy services in Chandi Mandir Military Station for 
the financial year 208-l9. Respondent no.2 submits bill for payment for the days the safai karamcharis performed 
their duties and answering respondent is only responsible to forward these bills to PCDA Western Command 
Chandigarh to release payment directly to the contractor’s account. Salary is paid to the workman by the contractor. 
Compliance of PF, ESI Rules etc. is carried out by the contractor as per contract agreement. Answering respondent 
has no responsibility of disbursement of wages and deduction of EPF and ESI etc. The workman and other safai 
karamcharis formed union in the headquarter, which is not permitted and also carried out demonstration inside the 
premises of Station Headquarter on 27.l0.208 and failed to perform his duties. Thereafter, answering respondent 


[भाग [ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 4275 


asked respondent no.2 to ask the workers to perform their duties. Answering respondent has no role or responsibility 
for termination services of the workman. There is no relationship of employer and employee between the answering 
respondent and the workman. In view of Army Headquarter Letter, the civilian employees of the answering 
respondent cannot organize themselves into trade unions as station headquarter is an administrative office of the 
Government of India. In spite of that, workman along with other safai karamcharis formed union unauthorizedly and 
indulge in unlawful activities. It is maintained that workman is the employee of contractor and therefore, termination 
is the prerogative of the contractor. It is prayed that the claim be dismissed. 


6. Respondent no.2 also filed reply maintaining therein that workman worked with it w.e.f. 05.07.20I8 to 
27.0.208. Respondent no.2 has not terminated the services of workman. Workman was paid minimum wages as 
declared by the Centre Government from time to time. Workman himself left the job at his own will without notice. It 
is prayed that the claim may be dismissed. 


Evidence of workman:- 


7. In order to prove his case, workman examined himself as WWI and tendered into evidence his affidavit 
WWI/A and has been cross examined by the AR for respondent no.l. AR for workman closed the evidence on 
30.07.2024. 


Evidence of respondent no.|:- 


8. Respondent no.l examined Colonel K P Singh S/o Late Colonel P. P. Singh, OIC, Civic Cell, Station Head 
Quarter, Chandi Mandir Panchkula as MWI, who tendered into evidence his affidavit Ex.MWI/A along with 
documents 7.२] to Ex.R4. AR for respondent no.! closed the evidence on !0.03.2026. 


Sr. No. Document Dated Exhibit 
l. Policy 8.03.20I] Ex.R-! 
2 Agreement with respondent no.2 05.07.208 Ex.R-2 
3. Copy of ECR of EPFO Ex.R-3 
4 Letter of Head Quarter 22.04.980 Ex.R-4 
9. It is pertinent to mention here that respondent no.2 was proceeded ex parte on 20.04.2023. 


Submissions of workman:- 


0. While arguing the case, AR for workman contended that in this case, workman was appointed by respondent 
no.| under a camouflage agreement with respondent no.2. The workman was appointed directly by respondent no.| 
on I!.08.207 as sweeper and his services were dispensed with on 27.0.20l8 without any reason in violation of 
Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act as no compensation was paid to the workman and junior to him were retained in 
service and fresh appointment were made after termination of the workman. So far as the agreements between the 
respondent no.! & 2 are concerned, they are totally not tenable and is unfair labour practice. The work performed by 
the workman is of perennial in nature. The contractor was not having any license. The contractor has left after three 
months. No domestic inquiry was held before terminating the service of the workman, which is mandatory under the 
Act. 


Submissions of respondent no.|:- 


Il. On the other hand, AR for respondent no.l contended that the workman was engaged by respondent no.2 
(contractor) as per agreement executed between respondent no.| & 2 on outsource basis and there is no relationship of 
employer and employee between respondent no.| and the workman. Wages were paid by the respondent no.2 to the 
workman and there was no role of respondent no.| in appointing or terminating the services of the workman. 
Workman is employee of respondent no.2. If there is any violation of provision of the Act, then it is done by 
respondent no.2. As per Ex.R3, all dues were paid by the contractor with regard to EPF and ESI etc. The workman 
and other safai karamcharis formed union in the Headquarter, which is not permitted as per Ex.R4. Respondent no.| 
being the Government Agency, workman is trying to take benefit of his appointment by respondent no.2 and has filed 
a false claim after concealing material facts and claim of the workman is not maintainable. 


2. I have given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 
Findings:- 
3. The first contention regarding the applicant is that whether applicant comes within the definition of workman 


as is defined in Section 2(s) of the Act. I may mention that applicant was appointed as sweeper as per his claim 
petition and affidavit submitted before the Tribunal. In plain words the applicant was performing his duties as 


4276 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 6, 2026/VAISAKHA 26, 948 [Part II—SECc. 3(ii)] 


labourer/unskilled worker. He was not in supervisory or administrative post requiring him to perform only 
administrative duties. While interpreting Section 2(s) Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Devinder Singh Vs. 


Municipal Council, Sanaur, AIR 20I] Supreme Court 2532,has observed as follows:- 


“The source of employment, the quantum of recruitment, the terms & conditions of employment/ contract of 
service, the quantum of wages/ pay and mode of payment are not at all relevant for deciding whether or not a 
person is a workman within the meaning of Section 2(s) of the Act. The definition of workman also does not 
make any distinction between full time and part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. 
There is nothing in the plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be inferred that only person 
employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing whole time job is a workman and the one 
employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty 
for fixed hours is not a workman.” 


4. Thus Hon’ble Supreme Court has clarified that the definition of workman also does not make any distinction 
between full time or part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. There is nothing in plain language of 
Section 2(s) from which it can be infer that only person employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing 
whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a 
casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a workman. In view of the ratio of law enunciated in the 
above ruling, in my considered opinion the applicant herein admittedly falls within the definition of ‘workman’ under 
Section 2(s) of the Act. 


5. The Hon’ble Supreme Court after analyzing the catena of cases has laid down in Balwant Raj Saluja Vs. Air 
India Limited in Civil Appeal ५७०.70266 dated 25.06. 20/4, two well recognized tests to find out whether the labours 
are the contract employees of the principal employer are:- 


3) Whether the principal employer pays the salary instead of contractor and 
2) Whether the principal employer controls and supervise the work of the employees? 


6. The facts regarding the payment of salary by the management or contractor has not been stated in the claim 
petition of the workman. In fact, claim petition is totally silent regarding the payment of wages, salary, letter of 
appointment by respondent no.! or by contractor or anything likewise. Thus, this basic feature for holding the 
relationship of employer and employee i.e. between respondent no.! and workman totally lacking not in the pleading 
but also in the evidence submitted by the workman.However, MWI Colonel K P Singh in his cross examination has 
stated that it is responsibility of the contractor to pay monthly wages to the conservancy staff provided by him. 
Respondent no.l has nothing to do with the minimum wages, DC rate to the conservancy staff. Contractor is 
responsible to give notice to the conservancy staff provided by him. Respondent no.! has no roll in terminating the 
services of the workman. Thus, it was the respondent no.2 who was required to ensure the payment of wages to the 
workers engaged by him from time to time. 


7. It is also added here that respondent no.! has also placed on record documents Ex.R-| to Ex.R-4. Ex.R-2 is 
the contract agreement between respondent no.| & 2. Thus, from the adduced evidence, it emerges that workman was 
paid salary by the contractor and he was being controlled and supervised by the contractor. Thus, there does not exist 
any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.l. However, there exists relation 
of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


8. It is also added here that as per definition of contractor as given in the Section 2(c) of the Contract Labour 
(Regulation and Abolition) Act, 970 (hereinafter called as Contract Act), respondent no.2 was supplying contract 
labour to the respondent no.!. Thus, respondent no.2 would fall under the definition of contractor. Section 2(c) of the 
Contract Act is reproduced as under: 


“Contractor”, in relation to an establishment, means a person who undertakes to produce a given result for the 
establishment, other than a mere supply of goods or articles of manufacture to such establishment, through contract 
labour or who supplies contract labour for any work of the establishment and includes a sub-contractor; 


9. TheHon’ble Apex Court in the case of Banglore Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A. Rajappa /०78(36) 
FLR 266 dealt at length with the ambit and scope of expression “industry” as defined in Section 2(j) of the Act, and 


has laid down triple test for determining whether a particular establishment is industry or not. The triple test is where 
(a) systematic activity (b) organized by cooperation between employer and employee (the direct and substantial 
element is commercial (c) for the production or distribution of goods and services to calculated to satisfy human 
wants and wishes prima facie is an “Industry” in that enterprise. The workman was doing work i.e. systematic activity 
for respondent no.| at the asking of respondent no.2 and there was cooperation between the workman and respondent 
no.2, which involve substantial commercial element for rendering service to satisfy human wants. Thus, respondent 
no.2 would be termed as industry. 


[भाग II—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 4277 


20. Workman in his deposition has clearly stated that there was non-compliance of Section 257, 250 and 25H of 
the Act. He also stated that he was appointed on .08.207 and he was paid minimum wages prescribed by Central 
Government and was working with respondent no.l. He was not given any compensation before terminating his 
services on 27.0.208. In his cross examination, he has further stated that his salary used to be credited in his bank 
account. But he has not placed on record any bank account, in which his salary was credited in order to enable this 
Court to see who was making payment to the workman. However, there is no denial that the workman was paid salary 
by respondent no.2 (contractor). However, from the agreement dated 05.07.20I8 (Ex.R-2) placed on file by 
respondent no.l, it emerges that safai karamcharis were provided by respondent no.2 (contractor) to work with 
respondent no.l. Thus, workman was employee of respondent no.2 and not of respondent no.! as has been discussed 
above. There does not exist any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.|. 
However, there exists relation of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


2]. Admittedly, there is non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2. However, as regards 
violation of other provisions i.e. Section 25G and 25H of the Act, there is no evidence lead by the workman. Hence, 
workman is entitled for compensation from respondent no.2. 


22. It is also added here that there is no iota of evidence that any unfair labour practice used by respondent no.| 
by entering into contract with contractors for supply of safai karamcharis and agreement executed between respondent 
no.! and 2 cannot be said to be any unfair labour practice. So far as contentions of AR for workman that the work 
performed by the workman is of perennial in nature and no domestic inquiry was held before terminating the services 
of workman are concerned, in this regard, it is pointed out that workman was entitled only for compensation under 
Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2 (contractor) and no domestic inquiry was required to be conducted. Merely 
because work is of perennial in nature does not mean that any right has accrued in favour of the workman to get 
employment with respondent no.l, which is a department of Civil Defence run by Government of India and for that 
regular recruitment procedure is provided. Workman cannot seek backdoor entry into service. Moreover, workman is 
not employee of respondent no.! being employee of respondent no.2 (contractor). 


23. In view of above discussion, it is held that since respondent no.2 (contractor) is currently not working with 
respondent no.|, so keeping in view the tenure of service rendered by the workman and salary given to him during the 
period of his stay with the contractor and non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act, this Tribunal is of the considered 
opinion that workman is entitled for compensation. 


24. Keeping in view the fact and circumstances of the present case, the following scheme of compensation is 

deemed fit by this Tribunal: 

. Workman who worked for less than 2 years with contractor shall be entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 

il. Workman who worked for more than 2 years but less than 5 years with contractor shall be entitled 
Rs.30,000/-. 

ili. Workman who worked for more than 5 years with contractors shall be entitled for Rs.50,000/-. 

25. Present workman employed on .08.20i7 and was terminated on 27.0.20l8 (less than 2 years), so he is 

entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 

26. Respondent no.2 is directed to pay the workman Rs.20,000/- within one month from the date of the 


notification of the award failing which the respondent no.2 shall further pay interest @6% per annum from the date of 
award till the date of making payment. It is also added here that in case respondent no.! requires safai karamcharis, 
then workman may be engaged through any contractor on sympathetic ground. 


27. Let copy of this award be sent to Central Government for publication as required under Section !7 of the 
Act. 


KAMAL KANT, PO-cum-Link Officer

Award :0.03.2026 
I, Workman has filed the present claim petition under Section 2A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 


(hereinafter called the Act) against his termination w.e.f. 27.0.20l8 claiming reinstatement into service along with 
continuity of service and full back wages. 


2, It is maintained by the workman in the claim petition that that he was appointed as sweeper/safai karamchari 
on 0.04.204 by respondent no.l at minimum wages prescribed by the Central Government. But the said minimum 
wages were never paid to him. Management has unlawfully deducted 2% EPF share, |.75% ESI and Rs.300/- as 
TDS from hissalary despite of the fact that his total wages comes under the ambit of taxfree limit. He was working 
under respondent no.! directly, but respondent no.! in order to avoid its liability always shows his employment in 
record under different contractors, which tantamount to unfair labour practice. 


3. It is further maintained that workman including 200 other safai karamcharis of respondent no.! raised the 
demand for non-deduction of TDS from the wages paid by respondent no.!. However, their demand was not accepted. 
Other workers working under respondent no.| formed union and subsequently his services were dispensed with w.e.f. 
27.0.208. It is also maintained that respondent no.| retained juniors in service and also made fresh appointments 
after termination of his services and no compensation was paid under Section 25F of the Act. Thus, there is violation 
of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act. Hence this petition. 


4. Notice of the petition was given to respondents. 


5. Respondent no.! filed reply to the claim petition maintaining therein that the workman has not come to the 
Court with clean hands as he was appointed as sweeper by respondent no.2 (contractor) at minimum wage rate as 
prescribed by the Central Government. As per Government Policy dated 8.03.20l (Ex.R-l), Non-Cantonment 
Military Stations have been authorized to outsource conservancy safaiwala. The answering respondent entered into 
contract with various private agencies for outsourcing of sweepers (safai karamcharis). In July 208, the answering 
respondent entered into contract with M/s AA Foundation for safety and M/s Sunrise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd. through e- 
tendering for outsourcing of safai karamcharis to provide conservancy services in Chandi Mandir Military Station for 
the financial year 208-l9. Respondent no.2 submits bill for payment for the days the safai karamcharis performed 
their duties and answering respondent is only responsible to forward these bills to PCDA Western Command 
Chandigarh to release payment directly to the contractor’s account. Salary is paid to the workman by the contractor. 
Compliance of PF, ESI Rules etc. is carried out by the contractor as per contract agreement. Answering respondent 
has no responsibility of disbursement of wages and deduction of EPF and ESI etc. The workman and other safai 
karamcharis formed union in the headquarter, which is not permitted and also carried out demonstration inside the 


[भाग [ave 3(ii)] भारत FT UAT : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 4279 


premises of Station Headquarter on 27.0.208 and failed to perform his duties. Thereafter, answering respondent 
asked respondent no.2 to ask the workers to perform their duties. Answering respondent has no role or responsibility 
for termination services of the workman. There is no relationship of employer and employee between the answering 
respondent and the workman. In view of Army Headquarter Letter, the civilian employees of the answering 
respondent cannot organize themselves into trade unions as station headquarter is an administrative office of the 
Government of India. In spite of that, workman along with other safai karamcharis formed union unauthorizedly and 
indulge in unlawful activities. It is maintained that workman is the employee of contractor and therefore, termination 
is the prerogative of the contractor. It is prayed that the claim be dismissed. 


6. Respondent no.2 also filed reply maintaining therein that workman worked with it w.e.f. 05.07.20I8 to 
27.0.208. Respondent no.2 has not terminated the services of workman. Workman was paid minimum wages as 
declared by the Centre Government from time to time. Workman himself left the job at his own will without notice. It 
is prayed that the claim may be dismissed. 


Evidence of workman:- 


7. In order to prove his case, workman examined himself as WWI and tendered into evidence his affidavit 
WWI/A and has been cross examined by the AR for respondent no.l. AR for workman closed the evidence on 
30.07.2024. 


Evidence of respondent no.|:- 


8. Respondent no.l examined Colonel K P Singh S/o Late Colonel P. P. Singh, OIC, Civic Cell, Station Head 
Quarter, ChandiMandir Panchkula as MWI, who tendered into evidence his affidavit Ex.MWI/A along with 
documents Ex.RI to Ex.R4. AR for respondent no.| closed the evidence on !0.03.2026.

Submissions of workman:- 


0. While arguing the case, AR for workman contended that in this case, workman was appointed by respondent 
no.| under a camouflage agreement with respondent no.2. The workman was appointed directly by respondent no.| 
on 0.04.20I4 as sweeper and his services were dispensed with on 27.0.20l8 without any reason in violation of

42, I have given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 


Findings:- 

3. The first contention regarding the applicant is that whether applicant comes within the definition of workman 
as is defined in Section 2(s) of the Act. I may mention that applicant was appointed as sweeper as per his claim 
petition and affidavit submitted before the Tribunal. In plain words the applicant was performing his duties as 
labourer/unskilled worker. He was not in supervisory or administrative post requiring him to perform only 
administrative duties. While interpreting Section 2(s) Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Devinder Singh Vs.

Municipal Council, Sanaur, AIR 20I] Supreme Court 2532,has observed as follows:- 


“The source of employment, the quantum of recruitment, the terms & conditions of employment/ contract of 
service, the quantum of wages/ pay and mode of payment are not at all relevant for deciding whether or not a 
person is a workman within the meaning of Section 2(s) of the Act. The definition of workman also does not

for fixed hours is not a workman.”

between full time or part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. There is nothing in plain language of

above ruling, in my considered opinion the applicant herein admittedly falls within the definition of ‘workman’ under 
Section 2(s) of the Act.

India Limited in Civil Appeal ५७०.70266 dated 25.06. 20/4, two well recognized tests to find out whether the labours

appointment by respondent no.l or by contractor or anything likewise. Thus, this basic feature for holding the 
relationship of employer and employee i.e. between respondent no.! and workman totally lacking not in the pleading 
but also in the evidence submitted by the workman.However, ४७] Colonel K P Singh in his cross examination has 
stated that it is responsibility of the contractor to pay monthly wages to the conservancy staff provided by him. 
Respondent no.l has nothing to do with the minimum wages, DC rate to the conservancy staff. Contractor is 
responsible to give notice to the conservancy staff provided by him. Respondent no.! has no roll in terminating the

the contract agreement between respondent no.| & 2. Thus, from the adduced evidence, it emerges that workman was 
paid salary by the contractor and he was being controlled and supervised by the contractor. Thus, there does not exist 
any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.l. However, there exists relation 
of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


8. It is also added here that as per definition of contractor as given in the Section 2(c) of the Contract Labour 
(Regulation and Abolition) Act, 970 (hereinafter called as Contract Act), respondent no.2 was supplying contract 
labour to the respondent no.!. Thus, respondent no.2 would fall under the definition of contractor. Section 2(c) of the 
Contract Act is reproduced as under: 


“Contractor”, in relation to an establishment, means a person who undertakes to produce a given result for the 
establishment, other than a mere supply of goods or articles of manufacture to such establishment, through contract 
labour or who supplies contract labour for any work of the establishment and includes a sub-contractor; 


9. TheHon’ble Apex Court in the case of Banglore Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A. Rajappa /9०78(36) 
FLR 266 dealt at length with the ambit and scope of expression “industry” as defined in Section 2(j) of the Act, and

no.2 would be termed as industry. 


20. Workman in his deposition has clearly stated that there was non-compliance of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of 
the Act. He also stated that he was appointed on 0!.04.204 and he was paid minimum wages prescribed by Central 
Government and was working with respondent no.l. He was not given any compensation before terminating his 
services on 27.0.208. In his cross examination, he has further stated that his salary used to be credited in his bank 
account. But he has not placed on record any bank account, in which his salary was credited in order to enable this 
Court to see who was making payment to the workman. However, there is no denial that the workman was paid salary 
by respondent no.2 (contractor). However, from the agreement dated 05.07.20I8 (Ex.R-2) placed on file by 


[भाग aves 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 428 


respondent no.l, it emerges that safai karamcharis were provided by respondent no.2 (contractor) to work with 
respondent no.l. Thus, workman was employee of respondent no.2 and not of respondent no.! as has been discussed 
above. There does not exist any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.|. 
However, there exists relation of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


2६ Admittedly, there is non-compliance of Section 257 of the Act by respondent no.2. However, as regards 
violation of other provisions i.e. Section 25G and 25H of the Act, there is no evidence lead by the workman. Hence, 
workman is entitled for compensation from respondent no.2. 


22. It is also added here that there is no iota of evidence that any unfair labour practice used by respondent no.|! 
by entering into contract with contractors for supply of safai karamcharis and agreement executed between respondent 
no.! and 2 cannot be said to be any unfair labour practice. So far as contentions of AR for workman that the work 
performed by the workman is of perennial in nature and no domestic inquiry was held before terminating the services 
of workman are concerned, in this regard, it is pointed out that workman was entitled only for compensation under 
Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2 (contractor) and no domestic inquiry was required to be conducted. Merely 
because work is of perennial in nature does not mean that any right has accrued in favour of the workman to get 
employment with respondent no.l, which is a department of Civil Defence run by Government of India and for that 
regular recruitment procedure is provided. Workman cannot seek backdoor entry into service. Moreover, workman is 
not employee of respondent no.| being employee of respondent no.2 (contractor). 


23. In view of above discussion, it is held that since respondent no.2 (contractor) is currently not working with 
respondent no.|, so keeping in view the tenure of service rendered by the workman and salary given to him during the 
period of his stay with the contractor and non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act, this Tribunal is of the considered 
opinion that workman is entitled for compensation. 


24. Keeping in view the fact and circumstances of the present case, the following scheme of compensation is 
deemed fit by this Tribunal: 


i. Workman who worked for less than 2 years with contractor shall be entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 

il. Workman who worked for more than 2 years but less than 5 years with contractor shall be entitled 
Rs.30,000/-. 

ili. Workman who worked for more than 5 years with contractors shall be entitled for Rs.50,000/-. 

25. Present workman employed on 0.04.20i4 and was terminated on 27.0.20l8 (more than 2 years and less 


than 5 years), so he is entitled for Rs.30,000/-. 


26. Respondent no.2 is directed to pay the workman Rs.30,000/- within one month from the date of the 
notification of the award failing which the respondent no.2 shall further pay interest @6% per annum from the date of 
award till the date of making payment. It is also added here that in case respondent no.| requires safai karamcharis, 
then workman may be engaged through any contractor on sympathetic ground. 


27. Let copy of this award be sent to Central Government for publication as required under Section !7 of the 
Act. 


KAMAL KANT, PO-cum-Link Officer 
नई दिल्‍ली, 7 मई, 2026

S.O. 847.—In pursuance of Section !7 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (4 of 947), the Central

[No. L-2025/0/2026- IR (B-I)-20] 
SALONI, Dy. Director

IN THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL-CUM-LABOUR COURT-I, 
CHANDIGARH 


(Present:- Mr. Kamal Kant, PO-cum-Link Officer).

Versus 
l. Admn. Commander Station Head Quarter (Civil Defence), ChandiMandirCantt. Panchkula, Haryana. 
2. M/s Sun Rise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd., 2253/] oe Floor, PipliWala Town, Mani Mazra, Chandigarh. 


----Respondents 
Present:- Sh. Jang Bahadur, AR for workman. 
Sh. Satinder Singh, AR for respondent no.. 
Respondent no.2 already ex parte. 
Award :0.03.2026 
l. Workman has filed the present claim petition under Section 2A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 


(hereinafter called the Act) against his termination w.e.f. 27.0.20l8 claiming reinstatement into service along with 
continuity of service and full back wages. 


2: It is maintained by the workman in the claim petition that that he was appointed as sweeper/safai karamchari 
on 6.08.20i2 by respondent no.l at minimum wages prescribed by the Central Government. But the said minimum 
wages were never paid to him. Management has unlawfully deducted 2% EPF share, |.75% ESI and Rs.300/- as 
TDS from hissalary despite of the fact that his total wages comes under the ambit of taxfree limit. He was working 
under respondent no.! directly, but respondent no.! in order to avoid its liability always shows his employment in 
record under different contractors, which tantamount to unfair labour practice. 


3. It is further maintained that workman including 200 other safai karamcharis of respondent no.! raised the 
demand for non-deduction of TDS from the wages paid by respondent no.!. However, their demand was not accepted. 
Other workers working under respondent no.| formed union and subsequently his services were dispensed with w.e.f. 
27.0.208. It is also maintained that respondent no.! retained juniors in service and also made fresh appointments 
after termination of his services and no compensation was paid under Section 25F of the Act. Thus, there is violation 
of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act. Hence this petition. 


4. Notice of the petition was given to respondents. 


5. Respondent no.! filed reply to the claim petition maintaining therein that the workman has not come to the 
Court with clean hands as he was appointed as sweeper by respondent no.2 (contractor) at minimum wage rate as 
prescribed by the Central Government. As per Government Policy dated 8.03.20I (Ex.R-l), Non-Cantonment 
Military Stations have been authorized to outsource conservancy safaiwala. The answering respondent entered into 
contract with various private agencies for outsourcing of sweepers (safai karamcharis). In July 208, the answering 
respondent entered into contract with M/s AA Foundation for safety and M/s Sunrise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd. through e- 
tendering for outsourcing of safai karamcharis to provide conservancy services in ChandiMandir Military Station for 
the financial year 208-l9. Respondent no.2 submits bill for payment for the days the safai karamcharis performed 
their duties and answering respondent is only responsible to forward these bills to PCDA Western Command 
Chandigarh to release payment directly to the contractor’s account. Salary is paid to the workman by the contractor. 
Compliance of PF, ESI Rules etc. is carried out by the contractor as per contract agreement. Answering respondent 
has no responsibility of disbursement of wages and deduction of EPF and ESI etc. The workman and other safai 
karamcharis formed union in the headquarter, which is not permitted and also carried out demonstration inside the 
premises of Station Headquarter on 27.!0.208 and failed to perform his duties. Thereafter, answering respondent 
asked respondent no.2 to ask the workers to perform their duties. Answering respondent has no role or responsibility 
for termination services of the workman. There is no relationship of employer and employee between the answering 
respondent and the workman. In view of Army Headquarter Letter, the civilian employees of the answering 
respondent cannot organize themselves into trade unions as station headquarter is an administrative office of the 
Government of India. In spite of that, workman along with other safai karamcharis formed union unauthorizedly and 
indulge in unlawful activities. It is maintained that workman is the employee of contractor and therefore, termination 
is the prerogative of the contractor. It is prayed that the claim be dismissed. 


[भाग aves 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 4283 


6. Respondent no.2 also filed reply maintaining therein that workman worked with it w.e.f. 05.07.20I8 to 
27.0.208. Respondent no.2 has not terminated the services of workman. Workman was paid minimum wages as 
declared by the Centre Government from time to time. Workman himself left the job at his own will without notice. It 
is prayed that the claim may be dismissed. 


Evidence of workman:- 


क्र. In order to prove his case, workman examined himself as WWI and tendered into evidence his affidavit 
WWI/A and has been cross examined by the AR for respondent no.l. AR for workman closed the evidence on 
23.07.2025. 


Evidence of respondent no.|:- 


8. Respondent no.l! examined Colonel K P Singh S/o Late Colonel P. P. Singh, OIC, Civic Cell, Station Head 
Quarter, Chandi Mandir Panchkula as MWI, who tendered into evidence his affidavit Ex.MWI/A along with 
documents Ex.RI to Ex.R4. AR for respondent no.! closed the evidence on 29.0.2026. 


Sr. No. Document Dated Exhibit 
Policy 8.03.20I] Ex.R-I 
2 Agreement with respondent no.2 05.07.208 Ex.R-2 
3 Copy of ECR of EPFO Ex.R-3 
4 Letter of Head Quarter 22.04.980 Ex.R-4 
9. It is pertinent to mention here that respondent no.2 was proceeded ex parte on 20.04.2023. 


Submissions of workman:- 


0. While arguing the case, AR for workman contended that in this case, workman was appointed by respondent 
no.| under a camouflage agreement with respondent no.2. The workman was appointed directly by respondent no.| 
on 6.08.202 as sweeper and his services were dispensed with on 27.0.20l8 without any reason in violation of 
Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act as no compensation was paid to the workman and junior to him were retained in 
service and fresh appointment were made after termination of the workman. So far as the agreements between the 
respondent no.! & 2 are concerned, they are totally not tenable and is unfair labour practice. The work performed by 
the workman is of perennial in nature. The contractor was not having any license. The contractor has left after three 
months. No domestic inquiry was held before terminating the service of the workman, which is mandatory under the 
Act. 


Submissions of respondent no.|:- 


ll. On the other hand, AR for respondent no.l contended that the workman was engaged by respondent no.2 
(contractor) as per agreement executed between respondent no.| & 2 on outsource basis and there is no relationship of 
employer and employee between respondent no.| and the workman. Wages were paid by the respondent no.2 to the 
workman and there was no role of respondent no.l in appointing or terminating the services of the workman. 
Workman is employee of respondent no.2. If there is any violation of provision of the Act, then it is done by 
respondent no.2. As per Ex.R3, all dues were paid by the contractor with regard to EPF and ESI etc. The workman 
and other safai karamcharis formed union in the Headquarter, which is not permitted as per Ex.R4. Respondent no. 
being the Government Agency, workman is trying to take benefit of his appointment by respondent no.2 and has filed 
a false claim after concealing material facts and claim of the workman is not maintainable. 


2. I have given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 


Findings:- 

3. The first contention regarding the applicant is that whether applicant comes within the definition of workman 
as is defined in Section 2(s) of the Act. I may mention that applicant was appointed as sweeper as per his claim 
petition and affidavit submitted before the Tribunal. In plain words the applicant was performing his duties as 
labourer/unskilled worker. He was not in supervisory or administrative post requiring him to perform only 
administrative duties. While interpreting Section 2(s) Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Devinder Singh Vs. 
Municipal Council, Sanaur, AIR 20] Supreme Court 2532,has observed as follows:- 


“The source of employment, the quantum of recruitment, the terms & conditions of employment? contract of 
service, the quantum of wages/ pay and mode of payment are not at all relevant for deciding whether or not a 
person is a workman within the meaning of Section 2(s) of the Act. The definition of workman also does not 
make any distinction between full time and part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. 
There is nothing in the plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be inferred that only person 
employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing whole time job is a workman and the one 
employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty 
for fixed hours is not a workman.” 


4284 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 6, 2026/VAISAKHA 26, 948 [Part II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


4. Thus Hon’ble Supreme Court has clarified that the definition of workman also does not make any distinction 
between full time or part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. There is nothing in plain language of 
Section 2(s) from which it can be infer that only person employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing 
whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a 
casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a workman. In view of the ratio of law enunciated in the 
above ruling, in my considered opinion the applicant herein admittedly falls within the definition of ‘workman’ under 
Section 2(s) of the Act. 


5. The Hon’ble Supreme Court after analyzing the catena of cases has laid down in Balwant Raj Saluja Vs. Air 
India Limited in Civil Appeal ७०.70266 dated 25.08.20I4, two well recognized tests to find out whether the labours 
are the contract employees of the principal employer are:- 


3) Whether the principal employer pays the salary instead of contractor and 
2) Whether the principal employer controls and supervise the work of the employees? 


6. The facts regarding the payment of salary by the management or contractor has not been stated in the claim 
petition of the workman. In fact, claim petition is totally silent regarding the payment of wages, salary, letter of 
appointment by respondent no.! or by contractor or anything likewise. Thus, this basic feature for holding the 
relationship of employer and employee i.e. between respondent no.! and workman totally lacking not in the pleading 
but also in the evidence submitted by the workman.However, MWI Colonel K P Singh in his cross examination has 
stated that it is responsibility of the contractor to pay monthly wages to the conservancy staff provided by him. 
Respondent no.l has nothing to do with the minimum wages, DC rate to the conservancy staff. Contractor is 
responsible to give notice to the conservancy staff provided by him. Respondent no.! has no roll in terminating the 
services of the workman. Thus, it was the respondent no.2 who was required to ensure the payment of wages to the 
workers engaged by him from time to time. 


7. It is also added here that respondent no.! has also placed on record documents Ex.R-| to Ex.R-4. Ex.R-2 is 
the contract agreement between respondent no.| & 2. Thus, from the adduced evidence, it emerges that workman was 
paid salary by the contractor and he was being controlled and supervised by the contractor. Thus, there does not exist 
any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.l. However, there exists relation 
of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


8. It is also added here that as per definition of contractor as given in the Section 2(c) of the Contract Labour 
(Regulation and Abolition) Act, 970 (hereinafter called as Contract Act), respondent no.2 was supplying contract 
labour to the respondent no.!. Thus, respondent no.2 would fall under the definition of contractor. Section 2(c) of the 
Contract Act is reproduced as under: 


“Contractor”, in relation to an establishment, means a person who undertakes to produce a given result for the 
establishment, other than a mere supply of goods or articles of manufacture to such establishment, through contract 
labour or who supplies contract labour for any work of the establishment and includes a sub-contractor; 


9. TheHon’ble Apex Court in the case of Banglore Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A. Rajappa /9०78(36) 
FLR 266 dealt at length with the ambit and scope of expression “industry” as defined in Section 2(j) of the Act, and 


has laid down triple test for determining whether a particular establishment is industry or not. The triple test is where 
(a) systematic activity (b) organized by cooperation between employer and employee (the direct and substantial 
element is commercial (c) for the production or distribution of goods and services to calculated to satisfy human 
wants and wishes prima facie is an “Industry” in that enterprise. The workman was doing work i.e. systematic activity 
for respondent no.| at the asking of respondent no.2 and there was cooperation between the workman and respondent 
no.2, which involve substantial commercial element for rendering service to satisfy human wants. Thus, respondent 
no.2 would be termed as industry. 


20. Workman in his deposition has clearly stated that there was non-compliance of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of 
the Act. He also stated that he was appointed on 6.08.20l2 and he was paid minimum wages prescribed by Central 
Government and was working with respondent no.!. He was not given any compensation before terminating his 
services on 27.0.208. In his cross examination, he has further stated that his salary used to be credited in his bank 
account. But he has not placed on record any bank account, in which his salary was credited in order to enable this 
Court to see who was making payment to the workman. However, there is no denial that the workman was paid salary 
by respondent no.2 (contractor). However, from the agreement dated 05.07.20I8 (Ex.R-2) placed on file by 
respondent no.l, it emerges that safai karamcharis were provided by respondent no.2 (contractor) to work with 
respondent no.l. Thus, workman was employee of respondent no.2 and not of respondent no.! as has been discussed 
above. There does not exist any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.|. 
However, there exists relation of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.2. 


2I. Admittedly, there is non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2. However, as regards 
violation of other provisions i.e. Section 25G and 25H of the Act, there is no evidence lead by the workman. Hence, 
workman is entitled for compensation from respondent no. 2. 


[भाग aves 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 4285 


D2. It is also added here that there is no iota of evidence that any unfair labour practice used by respondent no.| 
by entering into contract with contractors for supply of safai karamcharis and agreement executed between respondent 
no.! and 2 cannot be said to be any unfair labour practice. So far as contentions of AR for workman that the work 
performed by the workman is of perennial in nature and no domestic inquiry was held before terminating the services 
of workman are concerned, in this regard, it is pointed out that workman was entitled only for compensation under 
Section 25F of the Act by respondent no.2 (contractor) and no domestic inquiry was required to be conducted. Merely 
because work is of perennial in nature does not mean that any right has accrued in favour of the workman to get 
employment with respondent no.l, which is a department of Civil Defence run by Government of India and for that 
regular recruitment procedure is provided. Workman cannot seek backdoor entry into service. Moreover, workman is 
not employee of respondent no.| being employee of respondent no.2 (contractor). 


23. In view of above discussion, it is held that since respondent no.2 (contractor) is currently not working with 
respondent no.l, so keeping in view the tenure of service rendered by the workman and salary given to him during the 
period of his stay with the contractor and non-compliance of Section 25F of the Act, this Tribunal is of the considered 
opinion that workman is entitled for compensation. 


24. Keeping in view the fact and circumstances of the present case, the following scheme of compensation is 
deemed fit by this Tribunal: 


i. Workman who worked for less than 2 years with contractor shall be entitled for Rs.20,000/-. 

il. Workman who worked for more than 2 years but less than 5 years with contractor shall be entitled 
Rs.30,000/-. 

ili. Workman who worked for more than 5 years with contractors shall be entitled for Rs.50,000/-. 

25. Present workman employed on 6.08.20l2 and was terminated on 27.0.20I8 (more than 5 years), so he is 

entitled for Rs.50,000/-. 

26. Respondent no.2 is directed to pay the workman Rs.50,000/- within one month from the date of the 


notification of the award failing which the respondent no.2 shall further pay interest @6% per annum from the date of 
award till the date of making payment. It is also added here that in case respondent no. requires safai karamcharis, 
then workman may be engaged through any contractor on sympathetic ground. 


27. Let copy of this award be sent to Central Government for publication as required under Section !7 of the 
Act. 


KAMAL KANT, PO-cum-Link Officer 
नई दिल्‍ली, 7 मई, 2026

(hereinafter called the Act) against his termination w.e.f. 27.0.20l8 claiming reinstatement into service along with 
continuity of service and full back wages.

on 0I.09.202 by respondent no.! at minimum wages prescribed by the Central Government. But the said minimum 
wages were never paid to him. Management has unlawfully deducted 2% EPF share, |.75% ESI and Rs.300/- as 
TDS from his salary despite of the fact that his total wages comes under the ambit of tax free limit. He was working 
under respondent no.! directly, but respondent no.| in order to avoid its liability always shows his employment in

prescribed by the Central Government. As per Government Policy dated 8.03.20l (Ex.R-l), Non-Cantonment

respondent entered into contract with M/s AA Foundation for safety and M/s Sunrise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd. through e-

the financial year 20I8-l9. Respondent no.2 submits bill for payment for the days the safai karamcharis performed

Chandigarh to release payment directly to the contractor’s account. Salary is paid to the workman by the contractor. 
Compliance of PF, ESI Rules etc. is carried out by the contractor as per contract agreement. Answering respondent 
has no responsibility of disbursement of wages and deduction of EPF and ESI etc. The workman and other safai 
karamcharis formed union in the headquarter, which is not permitted and also carried out demonstration inside the 
premises of Station Headquarter on 27.0.208 and failed to perform his duties. Thereafter, answering respondent 
asked respondent no.2 to ask the workers to perform their duties. Answering respondent has no role or responsibility 
for termination services of the workman. There is no relationship of employer and employee between the answering
