Respondent no.2 already ex parte. 
Award :0.03.2026 
I, Workman has filed the present claim petition under Section 2A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 


(hereinafter called the Act) against his termination w.e.f. 27.0.208 claiming reinstatement into service along with 
continuity of service and full back wages. 


2: It is maintained by the workman in the claim petition that that he was appointed as sweeper/safaikaramchari 
on 0I.09.208 by respondent no.l at minimum wages prescribed by the Central Government. But the said minimum 
wages were never paid to him. Management has unlawfully deducted 2% EPF share, |.75% ESI and Rs.300/- as 
TDS from hissalary despite of the fact that his total wages comes under the ambit of taxfree limit. He was working 
under respondent no.| directly, but respondent no.! in order to avoid its liability always shows his employment in 
record under different contractors, which tantamount to unfair labour practice. 


4306 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 6, 2026/VAISAKHA 26, 948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


3. It is further maintained that workman including 200 other safaikaramcharis of respondent no.| raised the 
demand for non-deduction of TDS from the wages paid by respondent no.!. However, their demand was not accepted. 
Other workers working under respondent no.| formed union and subsequently his services were dispensed with w.e.f. 
27.0.208. It is further maintained that the workman has completed 240 days in the preceding year from the date of 
his termination. It is also maintained that respondent no.! retained juniors in service and also made fresh 
appointments after termination of his services and no compensation was paid under Section 25F of the Act. Thus, 
there is violation of Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act. Hence this petition. 


4. Notice of the petition was given to respondents. 


5. Respondent no.! filed reply to the claim petition maintaining therein that the workman has not come to the 
Court with clean hands as he was appointed as sweeper by respondent no.2 (contractor) at minimum wage rate as 
prescribed by the Central Government. As per Government Policy dated 8.03.20I (Ex.R-l), Non-Cantonment 
Military Stations have been authorized to outsource conservancy safaiwala. The answering respondent entered into 
contract with various private agencies for outsourcing of sweepers (safaikaramcharis). In July 20i8, the answering 
respondent entered into contract with M/s AA Foundation for safety and M/s Sunrise Facilitator Pvt. Ltd. through e- 
tendering for outsourcing of safaikaramcharis to provide conservancy services in ChandiMandir Military Station for 
the financial year 20l8-l9. Respondent no.2 submits bill for payment for the days the safaikaramcharis performed 
their duties and answering respondent is only responsible to forward these bills to PCDA Western Command 
Chandigarh to release payment directly to the contractor’s account. Salary is paid to the workman by the contractor. 
Compliance of PF, ESI Rules etc. is carried out by the contractor as per contract agreement. Answering respondent 
has no responsibility of disbursement of wages and deduction of EPF and ESI etc. The workman and other 
safaikaramcharis formed union in the headquarter, which is not permitted and also carried out demonstration inside 
the premises of Station Headquarter on 27.0.208 and failed to perform his duties. Thereafter, answering respondent 
asked respondent no.2 to ask the workers to perform their duties. Answering respondent has no role or responsibility 
for termination services of the workman. There is no relationship of employer and employee between the answering 
respondent and the workman. In view of Army Headquarter Letter, the civilian employees of the answering 
respondent cannot organize themselves into trade unions as station headquarter is an administrative office of the 
Government of India. In spite of that, workman along with other safaikaramcharis formed union unauthorizedly and 
indulge in unlawful activities. It is maintained that workman is the employee of contractor and therefore, termination 
is the prerogative of the contractor. It is prayed that the claim be dismissed. 


6. It is pertinent to mention here that respondent no.2 was proceeded ex parte on 07.05.2024. 
Evidence of workman:- 


7. In order to prove his case, workman examined himself as WWI and tendered into evidence his affidavit 
WWI/A and has been cross examined by the AR for respondent no.l. AR for workman closed the evidence on 
22.07.2024. 


Evidence of respondent no.|:- 


8. Respondent no.! examined Colonel K P Singh S/o Late Colonel P. P. Singh, OIC, Civic Cell, Station Head 
Quarter, ChandiMandir Panchkula as MWI, who tendered into evidence his affidavit Ex.MWI/A along with 
documents Ex.RI to Ex.R4. AR for respondent no. closed the evidence on 29.0.2026. 


Sr. No. Document Dated Exhibit 
l. Policy 8.03.20I] Ex.R-! 
2 Agreement with respondent no.2 05.07.208 Ex.R-2 
3. Copy of ECR of EPFO Ex.R-3 
4 Letter of Head Quarter 22.04.980 Ex.R-4 


Submissions of workman:- 


9. While arguing the case, AR for workman contended that in this case, workman was appointed by respondent 
no.| under a camouflage agreement with respondent no.2. The workman was appointed directly by respondent no.| 
on 0I.09.20i8 as sweeper and his services were dispensed with on 27.0.20I8 without any reason in violation of 
Section 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act as no compensation was paid to the workman and junior to him were retained in 
service and fresh appointment were made after termination of the workman. So far as the agreements between the 
respondent no.! & 2 are concerned, they are totally not tenable and is unfair labour practice. The work performed by 
the workman is of perennial in nature. The contractor was not having any license. The contractor has left after three 
months. No domestic inquiry was held before terminating the service of the workman, which is mandatory under the 
Act. 


[भाग aves 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 4307 


Submissions of respondent no.|:- 


0. On the other hand, AR for respondent no.l contended that the workman was engaged by respondent no.2 
(contractor) as per agreement executed between respondent no.| & 2 on outsource basis and there is no relationship of 
employer and employee between respondent no.| and the workman. Wages were paid by the respondent no.2 to the 
workman and there was no role of respondent no.! in appointing or terminating the services of the workman. 
Workman is employee of respondent no.2. If there is any violation of provision of the Act, then it is done by 
respondent no.2. As per Ex.R3, all dues were paid by the contractor with regard to EPF and ESI etc. The workman 
and other safaikaramcharis formed union in the Headquarter, which is not permitted as per Ex.R4. Respondent no.| 
being the Government Agency, workman is trying to take benefit of his appointment by respondent no.2 and has filed 
a false claim after concealing material facts and claim of the workman is not maintainable. 


ll. Moreover, it is also contended that the workman has worked for less than two months i.e. from 
0.09.208 to 27.0.208, whereas, workman has pleaded that he has worked for 240 days in the preceding years 
from the date of his termination. 


2. I have given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 


Findings:- 

3. The first contention regarding the applicant is that whether applicant comes within the definition of workman 
as is defined in Section 2(s) of the Act. I may mention that applicant was appointed as sweeper as per his claim 
petition and affidavit submitted before the Tribunal. In plain words the applicant was performing his duties as 
labourer/unskilled worker. He was not in supervisory or administrative post requiring him to perform only 
administrative duties. While interpreting Section 2(s) Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Devinder Singh Vs. 


Municipal Council, Sanaur, AIR 20I] Supreme Court 2532,has observed as follows:- 


“The source of employment, the quantum of recruitment, the terms & conditions of employmen?/ contract of 
service, the quantum of wages/ pay and mode of payment are not at all relevant for deciding whether or not a 
person is a workman within the meaning of Section 2(s) of the Act. The definition of workman also does not 
make any distinction between full time and part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. 
There is nothing in the plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be inferred that only person 
employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing whole time job is a workman and the one 
employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty 
for fixed hours is not a workman.” 


4. Thus Hon’ble Supreme Court has clarified that the definition of workman also does not make any distinction 
between full time or part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. There is nothing in plain language of 
Section 2(s) from which it can be infer that only person employed on regular basis or a person employed for doing 
whole time job is a workman and the one employed on temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a 
casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours is not a workman. In view of the ratio of law enunciated in the 
above ruling, in my considered opinion the applicant herein admittedly falls within the definition of ‘workman’ under 
Section 2(s) of the Act. 


5. The Hon’ble Supreme Court after analyzing the catena of cases has laid down in Balwant Raj Saluja Vs. Air 
India Limited in Civil Appeal ७०.70266 dated 25.06. 207/4, two well recognized tests to find out whether the labours 
are the contract employees of the principal employer are:- 


3) Whether the principal employer pays the salary instead of contractor and 
2) Whether the principal employer controls and supervise the work of the employees? 


6. The facts regarding the payment of salary by the management or contractor has not been stated in the claim 
petition of the workman. In fact, claim petition is totally silent regarding the payment of wages, salary, letter of 
appointment by respondent no.!l or by contractor or anything likewise. Thus, this basic feature for holding the 
relationship of employer and employee i.e. between respondent no.! and workman totally lacking not in the pleading 
but also in the evidence submitted by the workman.However, MWI Colonel K P Singh in his cross examination has 
stated that it is responsibility of the contractor to pay monthly wages to the conservancy staff provided by him. 
Respondent no.l has nothing to do with the minimum wages, DC rate to the conservancy staff. Contractor is 
responsible to give notice to the conservancy staff provided by him. Respondent no.! has no roll in terminating the 
services of the workman. Thus, it was the respondent no.2 who was required to ensure the payment of wages to the 
workers engaged by him from time to time. 


7. It is also added here that respondent no.! has also placed on record documents Ex.R-| to Ex.R-4. Ex.R-2 is 
the contract agreement between respondent no.| & 2. Thus, from the adduced evidence, it emerges that workman was 
paid salary by the contractor and he was being controlled and supervised by the contractor. Thus, there does not exist 
any relationship of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no.l. However, there exists relation 
of employee and employer between the workman and respondent no. 2. 


4308 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 6, 2026/VAISAKHA 26, 948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


8. It is also added here that as per definition of contractor as given in the Section 2(c) of the Contract Labour 
(Regulation and Abolition) Act, 970 (hereinafter called as Contract Act), respondent no.2 was supplying contract 
labour to the respondent no.!. Thus, respondent no.2 would fall under the definition of contractor. Section 2(c) of the 
Contract Act is reproduced as under: 


“Contractor”, in relation to an establishment, means a person who undertakes to produce a given result for the 
establishment, other than a mere supply of goods or articles of manufacture to such establishment, through contract 
labour or who supplies contract labour for any work of the establishment and includes a sub-contractor; 

9. The Hon’ble Apex Court in the case of Banglore Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A. Rajappa 
2928(36) FLR 266 dealt at length with the ambit and scope of expression “industry” as defined in Section 2(j) of the 
Act, and has laid down triple test for determining whether a particular establishment is industry or not. The triple test 
is where (a) systematic activity (b) organized by cooperation between employer and employee (the direct and 
substantial element is commercial (c) for the production or distribution of goods and services to calculated to satisfy 
human wants and wishes prima facie is an “Industry” in that enterprise. The workman was doing work i.e. systematic 
activity for respondent no.| at the asking of respondent no.2 and there was cooperation between the workman and 
respondent no.2, which involve substantial commercial element for rendering service to satisfy human wants. Thus, 
respondent no.2 would be termed as industry. 

20. Workman has pleaded that he was appointed on 0.09.20l8 and his services were terminated w.e-f. 
27.0.20i8. This fact has also been pleaded by him in his affidavit. How he has worked 240 days in the preceding 
year of his termination on 27.0.208 is not at all understandable. The workman has filed a false affidavit before the 
Court regarding his claim for working for 240 days in preceding year from the date of his termination. 

2]. However, as regards violation of other provisions i.e. Section 25G and 25H of the Act, there is no evidence 
lead by the workman. 

22 In view of the above findings that workman has filed false affidavit regarding his working for 240 days in 
the preceding year from the date of his termination on 27.0.20I8, his claim petition is not at all maintainable and 
therefore, the claim petition is dismissed. 


23. Let copy of this award be sent to Central Government for publication as required under Section 7 of the 
Act. 


KAMAL KANT, PO-cum-Link Officer

Appearances : 
Shri. N. A. Kulkarni, Ld. Advocate for First Party. 


Shri. R. P. Shaligram, Ld. Advocate for Second Party. 
AWARD 


This is a reference between workman and his employer to adjudicate upon the dispute arising out of 
termination of his service vide Section 2A of the Industrial Disputes (Amendment) Act, 947 [in short, ‘the Act’] as if 
it was a dispute referred to this Court by the appropriate Government in accordance with the provisions of the Act. 


2. Initially, the first party made an application to the Conciliation Officer of the appropriate Government for 
conciliation of the dispute. But in vain. The Conciliation Officer of the appropriate Government did not conciliate the 
dispute or refer the same to this Court till expiry of 45 days. Hence, the first party approached this Court after expiry 
of such 45 days. 


Statement of Claim : 


3. The case of the First Party as per Statement of Claim (हा.ए-]) in nutshell is : He was in employment with 
the second party as Clerk from 26.08.!99l. He was elected as General Secretary of Central Bank Employees’ 
Congress, Pune, Recognized Union by the Bank in the year 20l0. In such capacity, he had filed Writ Petition No. 
692/205 before Hon’ble Bombay High Court challenging the Circular dated 26.06.205 issued by the second party 
Bank. It was disposed of by recording statement on behalf of the Bank and thereby the bank was constrained to 
deduct the amount of union levy from the arrears payable to the employees arising out of implementation of Xth bi- 
partite settlement and pay over to the concerned union. 


4. On that count, the second party issued a charge-sheet dated 09.09.20I5 to the first party alleging that he 
committed a misconduct in respect of Regional Manager of Aurangabad region shri. K. C. Prakash. The First Party 
objected to it calling it illegal and contrary to the Circular dated 25.05.995; a memo dated 26.08.204 given by the 
second party was replied by the first party on 04.09.204; then till 09.09.205, he was not issued any charge-sheet and 
thereby the reply of the Second Party was deemed satisfactory as he was not issued charge-sheet within one month. 
However, domestic enquiry was conducted against him. He was not allowed to be represented by Subhash Chaudhary 
on the ground of retirement from service. Subhash Chaudhary though an ex-employee is an office bearer of the union. 
The first party was not allowed to conduct cross-examination of shri. Prakash, still his report was considered by the 
Enquiry Officer. The second party also examined shri. M.F. Noor, but he admitted that there was only discussion 
between the first party and Prakash. The first party was not allowed to examine Subhash Chaudhary and others as 
witnesses. Though he was held guilty as a result of the enquiry, but the enquiry is not legal and the findings recorded 
by the Enquiry Officer are perverse. The first party replied the show cause notice, but it was not considered. His 
services were terminated on |2.06.207. On the date of termination of the first party, Reference (IT) No.48/20I5 was 
pending before Industrial Court, Pune. Still the second party illegally terminated his services without following 
provision of Section 33(2) (b) of the Act and it is void-ab-initio. It is therefore prayed that the Second Party be 
directed to reinstate the First Party with continuity of service, full back wages and all consequential benefits. The 
reference be answered in favour of the First Party. 


Written Statement : 


5. The Second Party filed Written Statement (Exh.C-4) denying all the adverse allegations against it. Its crux is 
that : on 04.0.20l0 at l.00 pm the first party leading a group of 07 to 08 people prevented shri K. C. Prakash, 


430 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 6, 2026/VAISAKHA 26, 948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


Regional Manager, Aurangabad Region in front of bank premises at 3!7, M.G. Road, Camp, Pune from boarding his 
car after review meeting at Zonal Office, Pune. He kept on arguing in spite of invitation to come to office for 
discussion in the matter. Thus, he created unpleasant situation on the road and thereby committed gross misconduct. 
On that count, the second party issued him charge-sheet dated 09.09.205 for committing misconduct under para- 5(j) 
and (l) of the Disciplinary Action Procedure as laid down in the Memorandum of Settlement dated 27.05.2002. 


6. The enquiry was conducted against him. The documentary evidence was produced before the Enquiry 
Officer. The second party examined witnesses shri. Prakash and shri. Noor. The opportunity was given to the first 
party to conduct their cross-examination. Neither witness nor document was produced by him. He was allowed to be 
represented by defence representative. Thereafter, the Enquriy Officer held him guilty. The copy of the findings was 
forwarded to him. He commented thereon. Thereafter, he was served the proposed final order dated 5.05.207. The 
said proposed order was fixed for hearing on 25.05.207. Accordingly, the first party availed opportunity. Thereafter, 
his services were terminated by letter dated 2.06.20!7 under clause 6(b) of Memorandum of Settlement on 
Disciplinary Action Procedure for Workmen, 2002. The enquiry conducted against him is legal. The findings recorded 
by the Enquiry Officer are not perverse. He is not entitled to any reliefs. His case is false and is liable to be dismissed. 


7 Following Issues are framed by my Ld. Predecessor below Exh.O-3. Out of them, Issues No.l and 2 are 
already decided as Preliminary Issues by order dated 3.02.2024 by my Ld. Predecessor. I am now deciding rest of 
the Issues as follows 


ISSUES FINDINGS 
l) | Does the First Party prove that the enquiry conducted by First Party against him Yes

is not legal, fair and proper ! By order dated 73.02.2024.

REASONS 
8. As per the case record, in the order passed by my Ld. Predecessor on Preliminary Issues on 3.02.2024, it 


was held that the enquiry conducted against the First Party is not legal, fair, and proper, and the findings recorded by 
the Enquiry Officer are perverse and not based upon the evidence and materials before him. Accordingly, he has 
answered Issues No.! and 2. 


[भाग aves 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 43]] 
Arguments : 


9. Ld. Advocate for second party argued that first two Issues were taken up as preliminary Issues. As per 
order passed by Id. predecessor of the Court on |3.02.2024, it was held that the enquiry was not fair, legal, proper and 
the inferences drawn are perverse and thus that enquiry was vitiated. The burden of proof is on the first party. Perusal 
of the charge-sheet reflects that there is single specific charge against the first party of surrounding ( Gheroing) shri. 
K. C. Prakash on public road after official working hours by the first party and 7 - 8 co-workers. The second party has 
examined two witnesses. The witness shri. Prakash is the sufferer of the misconduct of the first party. During his 
examination-in-chief, he reiterated the incident mentioned in the charge-sheet as it is. He has reminded intact during 
his detail cross-examination. Second witness shri Mohammed Noor has supported the version of shri. Prakash during 
his examination-in-chief. It shows that the incident dated 04.!0.203 at about l0 p.m. had actually happened. He has 
reminded firm on his stand during his cross-examination. On the other hand, the only first party witness shri. 
Bhandarkar has himself admitted his presence at the spot of incident at the relevant time. The presence of shri. 
Prakash is also admitted by him at page-6 of his chief-examination. Thus second party has proved the charge by 
adducing material oral and documentary evidence on yardstick of preponderance of probabilities. During his cross- 
examination para-|8, the first party has admitted that he was earning and maintaining his family on his own, there is 
no documentary evidence that he searched for other job/s. Facts of the case-law cited are different from the facts of 
this case. It is not clarified as to Reference for what was pending; how the first party related to that Reference and 
therefore the case-law cited is not applicable to this case. Considering all aspects, the Reference be rejected with 
costs. 


0. Ld. Adv. for First Party argued in detail in lines with his Statement of Claim, oral and documentary 
evidence relied upon, it relevance to the case in his view has been mentioned in detail. It is submitted that the first 
party joined the second party as a clerk. He was a union leader. Bank witness shri. Prakash has admitted this in his 
cross-examination. The first party was instrumental in filing Writ Petition No. 692/20I5 before Hon’ble Bombay 
High Court challenging the Circular dated 26.06.20I5 issued by the second party Bank. It was disposed of by 
recording statement on behalf of the Bank and thereby the bank was constrained to deduct the amount of union levy 
from the arrears payable to the employees arising out of implementation of Xth bi-partite settlement and pay over to 
the concerned union. This became a trigger point and second party started targeting the first party. Issuance of charge- 
sheet of stated incident of 04.0.20l6 i.e. 02 years after its alleged occurrence on 09.09.206 is the biggest proof in 
that regard. Memo was given to first party on 26.08.20I4 and he submitted his reply to that and the issue was closed 
there itself. Perusal of the letter dated !8.03.20I5 by HR Manager to the field Manager i.e. the communication 
between two senior bank officers reflects that nothing was there in that matter; multiple objections were raised in that 
letter. In spite of it, the charge-sheet was issued. Perusal of the Circular of the bank dated 25.05.995 makes it clear 
that charge-sheet was illegally issued in contravention to that Circular. The first party had filed a Writ Petition before 
Hon’ble Bombay High Court and in the order in that case, all the points and contentions have been kept open. After 
conducting the so called enquiry the service termination order dated 2.06.20l7 was issued. Reference (IT) 
No.48/20I5 was pending in the Hon'ble Industrial Court, hence, the bank ought to have filed Approval Application as 
per Section 33 (2) (b) of the Act, but the second party violated that provision. As held in the verdict of Duncan 
Engineering Ltd. Vs. Ajay C. Shelke [ 2022() Mh.L.J 485] that termination order is void-ab-initio. Bank witness 
Shri. Prakash has admitted in his cross-examination that shri. Bhope was not a member of my union then why shall I 
gherao shri. Prakash. If there was grave incident, then why no any police complaint was filed. It is because nothing 
serious happened. Evidence of bank witness shri. Noor recorded in enquiry proves it. Without actually admitting, but 
only or a moment for the sake of argument, if we consider happening of the stated incident, still the punishment 
inflicted is shockingly disproportionate. Section |! (a) of the Act needs to be considered. In my Statement of claim 
para-ll, it is specifically mentioned that I searched for the job but could not get it due to termination by the second 
party. Hence, the Reference be allowed as claimed. 


Preliminary Issues Order : 


ll. As per case record, my ld. Predecessor passed order (Exh.O-4) on 3.02.2024 deciding preliminary Issues 
No.! and 2 holding that ‘the enquiry conducted against the First Party is not legal, fair and proper and that the 
findings recorded by Enquiry Officer are perverse and not based upon the evidence and materials before him.’ 


Evidence : 


2. Second Party has examined shri. K. Chandra Prakash as Second Party witness — | at Exh.C-34 and shri. 
Mohammad Firdaus Noor as S.P.W.-2 at Exh.C-44. Reliance is also placed on the documentary evidence. 


3. To substantiate the claim, First Party examined himself as Shubhendra K. Bhandarkar as First Party witness- 
at Exh.U-I]. Reliance is also placed on the documentary evidence. 


AS TO ISSUE NO. 3: 


4. At this juncture, it is to be examined whether the charge of misconduct levelled against the First Party is 
proved to the satisfaction of this Court by acceptable evidence. 


432 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 6, 2026/VAISAKHA 26, 948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


58 It is the allegation of second party that the first party has committed misconduct on 04.0.20I3 at about l0 
pm by leading a group of 07 to 08 people prevented shri K. C. Prakash, Regional Manager, Aurangabad Region in 
front of bank premises at M.G. Road, Camp, Pune from boarding his car after review meeting at Zonal Office, Pune 
and that he kept on arguing in spite of invitation to come to office for discussion in the matter and thereby he 
created unpleasant situation on the road. The first party has denied the charge in toto. 


6. S.P.W.-I shri. Prakash deposed in his examination-in-chief that in the year 203, he was Regional Manager 
at the second party bank till 3.07.2022. On 04.0.20l3, he had been to Pune for Regional Manager’s review 
meeting. After having dinner, he was coming back to board his office car. It was parked near Zonal Office. While 
entering into Car, a group of 7 to 8 members gheraoed him and started arguing regarding posting of shri. Bhope. He 
told them that it is not right place and time to discuss official matter. He also told them to come to Aurangabad. The 
first party shri. Bhandarkar and Subhash Choudhary were in the said group. His driver helped him to enter into the 
car. At that time, shri. Noor, the then Assistant General Manager was present there. He lodged a complaint (Exh.C-35) 
with the Zonal Manager, Pune and its copy was sent to Central Office, Mumbai. The copy of charge-sheet dated 
09.09.20i5 (Exh.C-36) bears signature of Anand J. Sharma, Chief Manager, Zonal Office, Pune. The first party was 
removed from service with superannuation benefits as per order (Exh.C-37). Its administrative order dated !2.06.207 
is at Exh.C-38. The original complaint dated 07.0.203 is at Exh.C-40. The original complaint dated 07.0.20I3 is at 
Exh. C-4.) 


7. In the continued contest, perusal of document (Exh.C-4) reads that it is the written complaint given by shri. 
K. C. Prakash against the first party narrating the incident dated 04.0.203 at about !0:00 p.m. Here, Id. advocate for 
second party argued that oral deposition of the witness is duly corroborated by his written complaint (Exh.C-4) 
regarding the misconduct of the first party. 


8. Bare perusal of his cross-examination indicates that the witness has stuck up to his case and there does not 
appear any vital admission detrimental to the case of second party that the first party has committed misconduct on 
04.0.20I3 at about 0 pm by leading a group of 7 to 8 people prevented shri Prakash, Regional Manager, 
Aurangabad Region in front of bank premises at M.G. Road, Camp, Pune from boarding his car after review meeting 
at Zonal Office, Pune and that he kept on arguing in spite of invitation to come to office for discussion in the matter 
and thereby he created unpleasant situation on the public road. 


728 Ld. Advocate for second party pointed out during argument that perusal of para-7 of his cross-examination 
and more particularly the suggestion given on behalf of the first party that ‘all regional managers were invited for the 
meeting dated 04.0.20l3 and they had attended the said meeting’ indicates that presence of shri. Prakash (Regional 
Manager of Aurangabad Zone) and thereby happening of the incident dated 04.!0.20l3 is not totally denied nor 
disputed by the first party. 


20. Ld. Advocate for first party argued that this witness has admitted in his cross-examination para-6 that shri 
Bhope was not a member of Central Bank Employees Congress union of which the First Party was Secretary, hence, 
the first party had no concern with the grievance of shri. Bhope and there was no reason to gherao shri. Prakash for 
any problem of shri. Bhope. Ld. Advocate for second party replied that this analogy has no substantial merit. In my 
view, it cannot be denied that it also happens that even though a particular workman is not a member of a union, still 
the office bearer of the union do advocate the cause / grievance of such workman because his such benevolent type 
action appeals to that workman and it impresses upon him that even though he not being a member of that union, still 
that union is advocating his cause. It creates some affection in his mind for the union due to which he may think of 
joining that union. Such action also would be helpful to the office bearer to create his image that he advocates for the 
cause of the workmen irrespective of union, to become a mass leader. Hence, in my considered opinion, the 
submission on behalf of the first party does not seem to have tangible merit. 


27. S.P.W.-2 shri. Noor testified in his examination-in-chief that in the year 20l3, he was Assistant General 
Manager at the regional officer of Central Bank of India. He was in the employment till 3|.0.2020. On 04.0.203, 
He was working in Regional Office, Pune. There was a meeting of Regional Managers at Zonal Office, Pune. After 
the meeting, they had been to dinner. After having dinner, they were coming back to Regional Office. The office car 
of Regional Manager shri Prakash was parked there. There were 7 to 8 people waiting near the said car. While 
entering into the car, the first party shri. Bhandarkar stopped them. He wanted to talk with shri. Prakash regarding 
transfer of someone. Shri Prakash told him that it is not right place and time to discuss official matter. He also told 
him to come to the office. The first party shri. Bhandarkar insisted him to give assurance. At that time, shri Prakash 
told him to witness the matter. 


22. Perusal of his cross-examination shows that he has remained firm on his stand taken in examination-in-chief. 


23. Upon examining the oral and documentary evidence in context of the factual matrix of this case, it reflects 
that the Second Party has adduced substantial tangible evidence on record to prove that the first party has committed 
misconduct on 04.0.20I3 at about l0 pm by leading a group of 7 to 8 people prevented shri Prakash, Regional 
Manager, Aurangabad Region in front of bank premises at M.G. Road, Camp, Pune from boarding his car after review 
meeting at Zonal Office, Pune and that he kept on arguing in spite of invitation to come to office for discussion in the 
matter and thereby he created unpleasant situation on the road. 


[भाग aves 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 433 


24. FP.W-| shri. Bhandarkar reiterated allegations in his Statement of Claim in his examination-in-chief 
affidavit. He has also relied on the documentary evidence i.e. Memorandum of Settlement dated 0.04.2002 (Exh. 
U-3), Circular of the Bank dated 26.06.20I5 (Exh.U-4), Order passed by the Hon’ble Bombay High Court in Writ 
Petition No.692/20I5 (Exh.U-I5), Circular dated 20.07.20I5 (Exh.U-6), Statement of account about levy deduction 
and credit to union account (Exh.U-7), Circular of Bank dated 25.05.995 (Exh.U-8), Letter of the Central Office to 
Field General Manager, Zonal Office Pune dated !8.03.20I5 (Exh.U-l9) and Order passed by the Hon'ble Bombay 
High Court in Writ Petition No.!563/205 (Exh.U-20). 


25. Ld. Advocate for second party pointed out that he has not denied in his cross para-!7 that shri Noor (S.P.W- 
2) was along with shri Prakash (S.P.W-I) at the relevant time. In my view, perusal of his cross-examination indicates 
that he has remained firmed on the core of Second Party case that the first party has committed misconduct on 
04.0.20!3 at about 0 pm by leading a group of 7 to 8 people prevented shri Prakash, Regional Manager, 
Aurangabad Region in front of bank premises at M.G. Road, Camp, Pune from boarding his car after review meeting 
at Zonal Office, Pune and that he kept on arguing in spite of invitation to come to office for discussion in the matter 
and thereby he created unpleasant situation on the road. 


26. In my considered view, upon examination of the oral and documentary evidence adduced by both the sides in 
light of the verdict and case record, it reflects that second party has proved on preponderance of probabilities that the 
incident dated 04.0.20I3 at about 0 p.m. at Mahatma Gandhi Road, Camp, Pune in premises of the Zonal Office of 
the First Party Bank had happened that the first party along with 7 to 8 persons prevented shri K. C. Prakash from 
boarding in his car, argued with him on public road and insisted to take immediate decision of posting of shri. Bhope, 
the Branch Manager of Osmanabad, creating unpleasant situation on the public road. 


27. In my opinion, the action of second party to obstruct his very senior officer on public road and preventing 
him from boarding his car after completion of office work etc and arguing with him regarding official work on public 
road do amount to misconduct. Hence I answer Issue no.3 in the ‘Affirmative’. 


AS TO ISSUE NO. 4: 


28. Now, the next point under consideration here is whether the misconduct is so grave as to terminate the 
second party from service ? 


29. On this aspect, ld. advocate for first party argued that there is no charge against the first party that he 
physically assaulted or thereby injured shri Prakash and hence the punishment given to the second party is shockingly 
disproportionate; due process was not followed and this is illegal victimization of the first party. 


30. Ld. Advocate for second party submitted that conjointly obstructing the senior officer on public road at night 
in presence of public by 7-8 persons to force shri Prakash to take decision regarding shri Bhope is very very serious 
misconduct and hence the punishment inflicted is just, legal and proper. 


3I. Ld. Advocate for First Party drew my attention to the Circular of the Second Party Bank dated 25.05.995 
(Exh.U-8) core of which reads as : ‘Further to our above Circular, it is hereby clarified that the Disciplinary action 
will be treated as pending only from the date of Charge-sheet for the purpose of withholding promotion, terminal 
benefits, acceptance of resignation / voluntary retirement, etc. This will, however, not cover the cases where R. C. has 
been registered by the Central Bureau of Investigation against the Bank Officials. Such cases shall be out of the 
purview of this Circular. 


32: In cases not involving vigilance angle, where explanation is called by way of a memo / communication from 
concerned employee, he should be informed within a period of one month from the date of receipt of complete reply 
as to whether the explanation is satisfactory or not. If he is not informed in writing within a period of one month from 
the date of receipt of complete reply, then it shall be deemed as satisfactory. In case the explanation to the memo / 
communication is not found satisfactory, and charge-sheet is necessitated to be issued, then the same should be served 
within a period of two months from the date of letter written to the concerned employee. However, this time- frame 
will not apply to vigilance cases.’ 


33. L.d Advocate for First Party further pointed out that in the letter of General Manager — H. R. D. at Central 
Office of the Second Party Bank had written letter (Exh.U-9) to the Field General Manager at Zonal Office, Pune 
regarding this case. It has mention that ‘the issue was more than 0! year old and appropriate time to examine it was 
October-203; the statement of shri. Prakash does not contain description of any physical threat; ingredient and 
intensity of the threat...’ 


34. Upon examining all aspects in light of the above circular (Exh.U-8) and the communication (Exh.U-!9) 
supra, and also the provision contained in section 33 (2) (b) of the Act, it reflects that due process was not followed 
while terminating the service of the First Party. 


35. In my considered view, we are the largest democracy of the World. We are governed by the Constitution of 
India, the supreme law of the land. Article !9 of the Constitution provides for fundamental right of freedom of speech 
and expression. It appears that the workers union was formed in exercise of that right. The first party had taken up the 
grievance of shri. Bhope because his appointment / posting was of Osmanabad which falls under zone of Aurangabad 


434 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 6, 2026/VAISAKHA 26, 948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


of which shri Prakash was zonal Manager at that time and was the authority to take decision in that regard. Fairly 
raising a dispute of a workman by a union office bearer workman colleague can be understood in this context. 
However, in this case, it is necessary to note that the first party raised the issue outside the bank office that too at 
night time !0 p.m. which is much later after the official working hours of the bank. Moreover, insistence to take such 
official work decision immediately on public road is not proper at all. Gheraoing of very senior officer by 7-8 
workmen in the quest of immediate resolution of a workman problem of official nature is also not justifiable. It is 
equally true that there is no case of physical assault or abuse in filthy language by first party to shri Prakash. Upon 
examination of all mitigating and aggravating factors, in my considered view, though there is misconduct by first 
party, it is not so grave to terminate his service. Hence, I answer Issue No.4 in the ‘Affirmative’. 


AS TO ISSUE NO. 5: 


36. The Second Party has claimed his reinstatement, full back wages and consequential benefits. The First Party 
has strongly opposed the claim. 


3d: As inferred supra, upon examination of all mitigating and aggravating factors, in my considered view, 
though there is misconduct by first party, it is not so grave to terminate his service and that the First Party was 
illegally terminated from the services of the Second Party. Hence, it becomes clear that the first party is entitled to the 
claim of reinstatement. 


38. As discussed in detail supra, it is on record that the action of second party to obstruct his very senior officer 
on public road and preventing him from boarding his car after completion of office work etc and arguing with him 
regarding official work on public road do amount to misconduct. On the other hand, F.P.W.-! Bhandarkar has 
admitted in his cross-examination para-|8 that he was earning and maintaining his family on his own and that there is 
no documentary evidence that he searched for other job/s. Keeping in mind all these aspects in light of the case 
record, it will be just and proper to grant 50% back wages and consequential benefits to First Party. Accordingly, I 
answer Issue No.5 as ‘Partly as directed’. 


AS TO ISSUE NO. 6: 


39. As inferred supra upon detailed discussion in light of the verdict and case record, the First Party was illegally 
terminated from the services of the Second Party and that the first party is entitled to the claim of reinstatement, 50% 
back wages and consequential benefits from the date of termination of First Party i.e. from 2.06.207 till his actual 
reinstatement in the original position. In the result, the reference deserves to be partly allowed. It will be proper to 
direct both the sides to bear their own costs. It will be appropriate to prescribe time frame for compliance of the 
directions. 


40. Accordingly, I proceed to pass the following Award : 
AWARD 
The reference is hereby answered in Partly Affirmative. 
a) The Second Party is directed to reinstate the First Party in his original position on or before 20.03.2026. 


b) — The Second Party to pay the 50% back wages and consequential benefits from the date of termination of First 
Party i.e. from |2.06.20I7 till his actual reinstatement in the original position. 


c) Parties to bear their own costs. 


d) Copies of the award be sent to the appropriate Authority / Government for necessary information and action 
as per rules. 


K. S. JADHAV, Presiding Officer 
Date  2/0/2026

For the Respondent: Sri Y. Ranjith Reddy, Advocate 
AWARD 


The Government of India, Ministry of Labour by its order No. L-!202/59/2000-IR(B.T) dated 3.07.2000 
referred the following dispute under section !0(.)(d) of the I.D. Act, !947 to Industrial Tribunal No.I, Hyderabad for 
adjudication and later the same has been transferred to this Tribunal bearing ID No. 6/2000, as per orders of Central 
Government vide Lr.No.H-!026/i/200I-IR(C.ID dated 8.0.200I, requiring this forum to decide the question: 


SCHEDULE 


“Whether the action of the management of State Bank of India, Local Head Office, Hyderabad in 
terminating the services of Sri 0. Satyanarayana, by way of oral orders with effect from 3.03.997 is 
justified? If not, what relief the workman is entitled?” 


After receipt of the reference, it was numbered as ID No. 90/2002 and notices were issued to both the Workman and 
the management. 


2. Before unfolding the factual matrix of the instant industrial dispute it would be apposite to have a bird eye 
view of a chequered history of the instant industrial dispute. Earlier instant industrial dispute along with the batch 


436 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 6, 2026/VAISAKHA 26, 948 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


cases was decided by this Tribunal vide common award dated 7.5.2005 and the reference was answered in favour of 
the Respondent and against the Workman. That said common award dated 7.5.2005 was challenged by the 
aggrieved Workmen in various Writ petition No.6470/20I4 along with batch of writ petitions before the Hon’ble 
High Court of Andhra Pradesh and Hon’ble High Court vide its’ judgement dated 23.6.20I4 allowed all batch of writ 
petitions and set aside the common award dated 7.5.2005 passed in ID No.222 of 200 and other batch of ID cases 
by Central Government Industrial Tribunal cum Labour Court, Hyderabad and the Respondent management of State 
Bank of India was directed to reengage the Writ Petitioners in position, which they have been occupying prior to 
their termination and further directed to consider their cases for regularization as and when the substantive vacancy 
arises. This judgement dated 23.6.20l4 of Hon’ble High Court of AP, passed in WP No.6470/204 and batch 
petitions was challenged by Respondent management before the Hon’ble High Court in Writ Appeal No. 268 of 
20i4. The aforesaid Writ Appeals No.l268/204 and other Writ Appeals were decided by Division Bench of High 
Court of Andhra Pradesh by a common judgement dated 20.3.209. Hon’ble High Court in aforesaid Writ Appeal 
was pleased to pass the order which is extracted as below:- 


“ 7. Hearing the learned senior counsel for the SBI and the learned senior counsel for the contesting unofficial 
Respondents, we see that while the learned single Judge was justified in setting aside the award af the Tribunal, the 
proper course to be adopted was to remit all the cases to the Tribunal. This we say for reasons more than one. Firstly, 
in such matters, claims have to be decided on individual basis, as different persons have different claims as to the 
length of officiation or discharge of duties and functions; quality of engagement, drawings, accounting of the post for 
each one of them, who have worked etc. All these issues will not be the same in all the cases. Therefore, each case 
ought to have been directed to be decided by the Tribunal afresh on individual basis. The second and most important 
aspect is the learned single Judge has in one go ordered re-employment of all the workmen. This is not a relief that 
could have been granted without answering the individual issues; each issue relating to each case could not have 
been decided by the writ Court within the format of its adjudication procedures and scope. The adjudicating body, 
which has to do that activity, is the Industrial Tribunal. Therefore, we are of the view that while we would sustain the 
order of the learned singe Judge insofar as it interfered and sets aside the award of the Tribunal, the further findings 
and directions, issued through the impugned order have to go and the individual cases have to be sent back for 
consideration of the Tribunal. Such further procedure before the Tribunal will have to be carried forward with the 
materials already on record and also by affording an opportunity to the persons, who have claims as well as the 
management to place their rival contentions and further material before the Tribunal. The learned counsel appearing 
for the workmen are justified in pointing out that enormous delay has already happened and further action by the 
Tribunal in this line may be expedited. 


9. In the result, these writ appeals are ordered; 


(4) affirming the impugned common order of the learned single Judge to the extent it sets aside the common award 
dated /7.05.200935 of the Industrial Tribuna!; 


(2) the further findings and directions issued through the impugned common order are vacated: 


(3) all the matters shall be remitted to the Industrial Tribunal with a direction to dispose of them within an outer limit 
of five (5) months from the date of receipt of a copy of this order; and, 


, 


(4) the parties to make appearance before the Tribunal on the given date.’ 


Thus, in view of the above direction of Hon’ble High Court of Andhra Pradesh in W.A.No.l268 of 204 and batch 
cases. This Tribunal has taken up the matter of industrial dispute for hearing and deciding it afresh in view of the 
aforesaid guidelines laid down by the Hon’ble High Court in Writ Appeal No.l268/20l4 vide judgement dated 
20.3.09. 


3. The factual matrix of instant industrial dispute as narrated by the Workman in his claim statement are that, 
the he joined the service of the Respondent Bank at the workman joined in the services of the Management Institution 
as Non Messenger on !986 to !996 and rendered unblemished service spreading over a period of about 8 years, and 
by dint of hard work till his services where terminated by oral orders with till his services where terminated by oral 
orders with effect from .04.997. Further, it is submitted that the management of Respondent bank decided to give 
a chance to temporarily employed personnel “found suitable for permanent appointment” by wait- listing them by 
offering permanent appointment or waitlisting till such opportunity arises. 


4. That on !7.!i.987 a settlement was reached between All India State Bank of India Staff Federation and the 
Management of State Bank of India - settlement one, under this settlement three categories of employees were 
listed. That is, A) those who have completed 240 days in 2 months or less after .7.975. 8) those who have 
completed 270 days in any continuous block of 36 calendar months after |.7.975. C) 0) those who have completed 
minimum of 30 days aggregate temporary service in any calendar year after .7.975 or (ii) 70 days aggregate 
temporary service in continuous block of 36 months after |.7.975. 


5. The persons who satisfied in all the above 4 categories were to be interviewed by a selection committee and 
the said selection committee would determine the suitability of the said candidate for permanent appointment. 
Therefore, the bank prescribed certain qualifications and from among the candidates satisfying the qualifications the 


[भाग aves 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : मई 6, 2026/वैशाख 26, 948 437 


suitable candidates were enlisted by a selection committee. The Clause 7 of the said agreement provided with the 
selected candidate would be wait listed in order of their respective categorization and the selected panel would be 
valid upto December, 99. Clause 0 of the settlement specifically provided that henceforth. “there will be no 
temporary appointments in the subordinate cadre”, except on a restrictive basis in the specified category, “from 
amongst empanelled candidates as per existing guidelines of the bank”. The Workman further submits that 
consequent upon the said agreement and the draft, a notification was issued in the newspapers. The last date for 
responding to the advertisement was 30.8.988. A written examination followed by viva-voce in May, !989 was 
held. A selected panel was prepared, as per Clause 7 of the agreement, ie. Settlement No.l, the selected panel was to 
be valid upto December, 99!, the Workman submits that circular was issued on 26.4.9! by the said letter it is 
mentioned that the terms of the agreement dated 7.l.87 was modified vide second agreement dated 6.7.88 was 
entered into between the parties. In terms of the said agreement a chance was to be given, “to all eligible temporary 
employees for permanent appointments. The appointments were against the vacancies likely to arise during the 
years 995-96, circular made it clear that in view of the enormity of the problem an extension of the currency of the 
panel, eligible temporary employees who have been empanelled could not appear in the earlier interviews and have 
been pursuing their cases thereafter, “will be given another chance to appear for interview”. 


6. Workman submits that there were total five settlements. The settlement dated 7.].87 is the |7 
settlement (Ex Ml), settlement dated 6.7.88 is 27 settlement (Ex M2), settlement dated 27.0.88 is the 37 
settlement (Ex M3), then settlement dated 9..9l is 4" settlement and settlement dated 30.7.96 is 57 settlement 
(Ex M6). In between there is minutes of conciliation proceedings dated 9.6.95 marked as Ex M5. That due to all 
these settlkements which were extended by subsequent settlements thereby created reasonable expectations in the 
list of the selected candidates arose with that it’s a question of time their appointments or services would be 
regularized in the services of the bank. The Workman was working with the bank on temporary basis was under 
the bonafide hope that sooner his services will be regularized with the bank. He is thereby closed all his options 
elsewhere. It is needless to point out that employing person to whom hope of employment in substantial terms was 
made is a facet of Article 2 of the Constitution of India. 


7. Further Workman submits that the Government of India issued Circular No. F-3/3/l04/87-IR, dated 
6.8.990. Under said circular the chief Executives of all public sector banks including the Management herein were 
specifically instructed that until the problem of existing temporary employees is fully resolved, no bank is permitted 
to make any permanent appointments. That some of the persons similarly situated like this Workman aggrieved by 
the inaction on the part of the management of the bank is not regularizing their services from out of the selected panel 
and not clearly focusing the vacancy position, filed W.P. No. 494/97 before Hon’ble High court of A.P.. It is 
specifically averred in the said writ petition that the Management of the bank had failed to implement the settlement 
and that it violates the various fundamental rights guaranteed under the constitution of India. The Hon’ble High 
court of A.P .. by order dated 5.3.97 directed the bank to implement the settlement as amended from time to time. It 
also directed the bank to carry out the terms of settlement before the expiry of March, !997. The Hon’ble High 
Court also recorded finding that the Bank cannot escape its liability of enforcement of the Settlement. In view of the 
directions given by the High Court all candidates whose names appeared in the select panels prepared on the basis of 
the agreement entered into on !7.!.87 under which the panel was valid upto December, 99], and on the basis of a 
settlement dated 27.0.988 whereby the panels were made alive upto 3i.3.997 under which the panel was valid 
upto December, 999. The other agreement dated 6.7.!988 under which the panel was valid upto 992 and on the 
basis of the settlement dated 27.0.998 whereby the panels were made alive upto 3].3.[997 were under the bonafide 
impression that their cases will be considered for regularization and were living on the basis of the said reasonable 
expectation. Unfortunately, contrary to the aforesaid directions given by the High Court and contrary to the 
settlements entered into between the parties, the bank issued proceedings dated 25.3.]997, dated 27.3.997 and 
3.3.997 instructing the various authorities of the Management not to continue the temporary employees those who 
are in services of the bank from .4.97. The said order was followed by the Management. Aggrieved by the said 
action the Workman herein and similarly situated candidates have filed a writ petition before the Hon'ble High Court 
and by way of Writ Petition No.9206/97 seeking a declaration that the proceedings issued by the Deputy General 
Manager and the Assistant General Manager (Respondents 3,4 and 5 therein) on 25.3.97, 27.3.97 and 3[.3.97 as 
illegal and also non-continuance of the Workmen therein in service by absorbing them in the services of the bank as 
violative of Section 2(p) and 8(.) read with Rule 58 of Central Rules and sought for specific direction to the bank to 
absorb them in service. 


8. Further, Workman submits that in the counter affidavit filed in Writ Petition No.9206/97, the bank has 
submitted that it has about 805 branches in Andhra Pradesh alone. It has stated that due to exigencies of 
circumstances and on account of the urgent need in its banks, it employed temporary employees in subordinate cadre. 
It is pertinent to mention that it does not state the urgent or need or the nature of temporary employees that it had 
engaged. Enquiry into the same would reveal that the stand taken by the bank either on the ground of urgent need or 
of temporary employees is a facade to perpetuate unfair labour practice. It is designed to on the one hand, keep the 
employed in the erroneous zone of hope and on the other to ensure that benefits that a model employer will extend 
under various statutes to its employees is not required to be borne out by the bank. A reading of the counter affidavit 
would show that the bank would opines that being just fair and reasonable are which obviously is reprehensible and is 
a facet of unfair labour practice.
